On macOS, add the aliases with `sudo ifconfig lo0 alias 127.0.0.<n> up` (or `sudo ci/runner/loopback-aliases.sh`) to run them.
- **`daemon install` installs for real on each OS.** The unit file it generates is snapshot-tested. Its tests sit in `service_manager` modules, in nextest's `service-manager` test group, and reach the user's own service manager through the variables of the test's own session; on Linux they run in the Pre-merge suite against systemd's user manager, which needs a host or container booted with systemd, and on macOS and Windows in the Release suite only, so a local run of another suite skips them.

### Isolation

1. One process per test (nextest).
2. A per-test temporary `GOODFETCH_HOME`, in a folder whose name holds a space and a non-ASCII letter on every OS ([Accessibility, localization and visual regression](#accessibility-localization-and-visual-regression)).
3. A child process starts with a cleared environment whose `HOME`, `USERPROFILE` and `XDG_` variables point inside the test's folder, and whose `TZ` and `LANG` are `UTC` and `C.UTF-8` unless the test names others.
4. No fixed ports: bind port 0 and report the address, or use a Unix socket or named pipe in the test's folder.
5. One Valkey per CI job, with a namespace per test.
6. Resources shared by the whole user (keychain, service manager) run in a nextest test group, with unique names.
7. Certificate authorities are made per test: the Proving ground's from its seed, a Daemon group's by `group init` into the test's folder.
8. No test reads another test's data or depends on test order.

### SDKs and the Console

- **SDK durations and jitter are options:** tests pass short values and a fixed seed. In TypeScript, Vitest fake timers may drive timer logic that never reaches the engine.
- **The Console's end-to-end tests set time with Playwright's `page.clock`** (`setFixedTime` first). Its component tests use Vitest fake timers.

## Simulation, model checking and crash testing

Decided in [ADR 0060](docs/adr/0060-distributed-and-crash-behaviour-is-simulated-on-tokio-model-checked-and-faulted-at-io-seams.md). Leases, run epochs, Checkpoints, the writer lock, Daemon groups, Partitioned crawls, degraded shares, the Usage ledger and Sink delivery are tested at every layer that can break them; the ADR's table says which.

### Code the tests rely on

- **Open the connections the Simulation carries only in `goodfetch-engine`'s `transport` module**, which takes the network as a value (the OS, the in-memory pair, or a test adapter): stdio of an Attached engine, the Daemon's socket and named pipe, loopback TCP, the WebSocket listener, the private endpoint, the ops listener, the peer link, wake endpoints. `rpc` takes its connections from it. Fetch sockets, the CDP connection, plugin-protocol pipes and third-party clients stay outside the Simulation and open their own. `STYLE.md` lists the lints.
  _Why:_ the Simulation swaps the network there, and nowhere else.
- **Start threads and block only through the `tasks` module**, whose blocking policy is a value. In the Simulation it runs blocking calls inline, and a dedicated thread's body (the run's SQLite writer actor, for example) as a task on the host's runtime.
  _Why:_ a real thread ends its work at an arbitrary simulated moment, so the run would not replay.
- **Never build a runtime in the engine.** Only `main`, test helpers and bench targets (ADR 0071) build one, plus the Rust SDK's connection through the helper `main` uses; its unit tests over the in-memory transport pair pass it the test helper's paused current-thread runtime, and its linked-mode tests use the production runtime with short durations (ADR 0081). `tokio::task::block_in_place` is in `disallowed-methods`.
- **Give every third-party time hook the `Clock`** (rustls's `TimeProvider`), and the test `tracing` subscriber's timer too, so traces carry simulated time.
- **Emit each transition a TLA+ model names as a `tracing` event** under the model's target, with the model's variable names as fields.
- **No failpoints.** Inject faults at I/O seams: the crash VFS, the transport adapters, the plugin protocol's in-memory adapter, Toxiproxy, process kills.
  _Why:_ a crash only matters at an I/O boundary, and each boundary already has a seam.

### Simulation

- **Several hosts in one process run on turmoil**, pinned, each on its own paused current-thread Tokio runtime with `Clock::paused` and `Entropy::seeded`, built with `--cfg tokio_unstable` so each host's runtime randomness (`select!` order) follows the seed. No crate swaps the runtime.
- **Compile the scenarios only under `cfg(tokio_unstable)`** (`#[cfg(tokio_unstable)] mod simulation;` in the `tests/it/main.rs` of each crate that has them). They run from a nextest archive and CI job of their own, filtered to the `simulation` module, with their own rust-cache key, counted against the Pre-merge budget and ADR 0061's cache priority.
  _Why:_ every other test then runs on the Tokio configuration production ships.
- **Derive every seed from the test's seed**, which `goodfetch-test-support` supplies (`GOODFETCH_TEST_SEED` overrides it): one stream for turmoil, one per host's `Entropy`, one for the Fault plan's generation.
- **A scenario keeps an Observation log** of operations, answers and faults, and **the shared invariant checkers judge it**: fencing, one lead, Limits, Partitions, the Audit log chain (through the product's own verifier, passed in as a value), and liveness within a stated simulated time once faults stop. Real-process tests and conformance suites use the same checkers.
- **Faults come from Fault plans** generated by proptest-state-machine and shrunk by proptest on failure.
- **Check determinism:** rerun a few seeds and compare Observation logs and `tracing` output. Fix any mismatch at its source.
- **Leave security values out of the comparison.** Keys, tokens, certificates and TLS handshakes still come from the OS generator (ADR 0059) and are recorded redacted. If behaviour depends on their bytes, fix the code; never seed them.
- **The Politeness store is not simulated.** Group scenarios set `politeness.sharing: per_daemon`.

### Model checking

- **The five protocols have TLA+ models checked by TLC:** run fencing, lead epoch, Partition handoff, slices and reclaim, degraded shares. The TLA+ tools and the CommunityModules release are pinned as one pair in `test-support/models/tools.toml`, each an asset of this repository's `tla-tools-…` release with its SHA-256 (upstream replaces the 1.8.0 prerelease at each build): TLA+ 1.8.0 with CommunityModules 202610131629, the pair that runs NDJSON trace validation (stable 1.7.4 loads no current CommunityModules release). `just tlc-fetch` downloads them before the deny and fails on a download whose SHA-256 differs from its pin.
- **Each model is a folder of `test-support/models/`** with a `model.toml` (`spec`, its TLA+ module, and `traces`, the files and folders of the modules it describes), a `small.cfg` for the Pre-merge and Release suites and a `large.cfg` for the nightly job. Run `just tlc <model>` (Java 11 or later); `--config large` takes the larger constants.
- **The `tlc` job runs only when a model, a module it traces, the pinned tools or xtask's TLC command changes** (`xtask affected`'s `models=`), and the Release suite runs every model. Each run ends with the job's self-test, `just tlc-self-test`: the `sample` model's `broken.cfg` must fail on its invariant. In M1 the job is model checking only; trace validation joins it with the Simulation in M4. The nightly job with `large.cfg` waits for the nightly tier.
- **Trace validation:** TLC checks the Simulation's NDJSON traces against the model. Real-process traces are not validated, since several processes' events have no single order.
- **Change a model in the same pull request as the behaviour it describes,** and list a module it starts to describe in its `traces`.

### Model-based tests and crashes in one process

- **Test `run_state`, `lease`, `checkpoint`, delivery and `ledger` with proptest-state-machine** against reference models, on real SQLite in the test's folder with `Clock::paused`, with crash, power-cut, timeout, late-commit and resume transitions.
- **Crash SQLite through the crash VFS** (`sqlite-plugin`): it holds writes until a sync; a process crash keeps unsynced writes, a power cut drops, tears or reorders them from the seed; the crashed instance's later I/O is refused. It also injects I/O errors and a full disk. Databases open with the VFS name as a value; production passes none.
- **Check after every crash:** acknowledged commits are present in default durability, `PRAGMA integrity_check` passes in both modes, and nothing is visible twice.
- **A full disk at a commit** (the crash VFS's injected full disk on `run_state`, the Usage ledger and the Politeness file): the commit is rolled back and never acknowledged, the Run stops with `resource: "storage"` from its last acknowledged commit, `PRAGMA integrity_check` passes, and the resume redoes the attempts in flight with nothing visible twice. When the commit recording the stop also fails, the Run is left with no live writer and resumes as after a crash ([ADR 0122](docs/adr/0122-a-run-stops-resumably-before-its-disk-fills-and-a-daemons-logs-rotate-at-a-bounded-size.md)).
- **Test Sinks through the plugin protocol's in-memory adapter** with a test Sink of each Guarantee class, and judge the result by class.

### Concurrency

- **A module that synchronizes threads with its own atomics, `Mutex`, `RwLock` or `Condvar` has loom tests.** It imports them through its crate's `sync` module, which maps to loom under `--cfg goodfetch_loom`.
  _Why:_ a Goodfetch-specific cfg leaves dependencies that react to `cfg(loom)` unchanged.
- **No Shuttle.** Actors on Tokio channels are covered by the Simulation.

### Real processes

- **Kill real processes at seeded moments** chosen from observed protocol events: `kill -9` on Linux and macOS, `TerminateProcess` on Windows, for engines, Clients, sink processes and Script sandbox workers.
  - To prove an Attached engine dies with its Client, not with its stdin, start it through a stand-in parent whose pipes the test holds (`/bin/sh -c '"$@"; exit $?'`, `cmd /d /c` on Windows), kill the stand-in, and wait for the engine's stdout to close (Collie's `tests/it/parent_death.rs`).
- **Power-cut the real binary with LazyFS on Linux:** mount the run folder and the Goodfetch home through it, kill, clear its cache, resume, check as above. Name the test module `lazyfs` in the crate's `tests/it`: nextest's profiles leave `lazyfs::` integration tests out, and the Release suite's `linux-x64-gnu` cell runs them (`ci/release/cell.sh lazyfs`, on the LazyFS `ci/host/lazyfs.sh` builds before the deny); `just lazyfs-test` runs them locally.
- **Assert `fullfsync` on macOS**, which no tool can observe reaching the drive.
- **Cut off engines and partition Group members with Toxiproxy**, reached through each engine's `resolve` mapping. Give each engine's listeners a loopback address of their own (`resolve` maps names, not ports). Keep Valkey's durations short.
- **Run ADR 0055's Kubernetes path on `kind`.**

### Where the pieces live

- **Simulation scenarios:** the `tests/it/simulation/` of the crate that holds the behaviour, one module per behaviour area: `goodfetch-engine`'s, or a Foxhound crate's for Partition handoff, the crawl's work-item leases and Sink delivery (ADR 0087).
- **turmoil's network adapter:** a test-only crate outside `crates/foxhound/`, which `goodfetch-engine`'s and Foxhound's tests take as a dev-dependency (ADR 0087).
- **Committed Fault plans:** `fixtures/fault_plans/` beside their scenario, each with its `<name>.source.toml` (generator version, seed, replay command).
- **Model-based tests and loom tests:** unit tests of the module they check; loom tests compile only under `goodfetch_loom`.
- **The crash VFS, the Observation log and the invariant checkers:** `goodfetch-test-support`. The Observation log is plain records, so they need no Goodfetch crate.
- **TLA+ models:** `test-support/models/`, one folder per model with its TLC configurations and trace-validation spec.

### Suites

Placed in the suites of [Suites, commands and time budgets](#suites-commands-and-time-budgets). Nightly-only jobs explore new seeds: they repeat tests of normal length with new `GOODFETCH_TEST_SEED` values, each within the 5-minute kill, in the nightly tier once it is on. They are in no suite. A failure in the deterministic layers (model-based tests, Simulation, loom, TLC) replays from its seed; a real-process or Toxiproxy failure is recorded with its seed and classified by ADR 0064's diagnostic run.

| Check | Quick | Pre-merge | Release | Nightly-only job |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pure time rules, proptest of decision logic | yes | yes | yes | |
| Model-based tests with the crash VFS | 16 cases and committed regressions, within the 10 s kill | own job: 256 cases and committed regressions | as Pre-merge, every OS | random seeds, 1 h |
| Simulation (own `tokio_unstable` job) | | committed Fault plans, 16 seeds per scenario, determinism check on 4 seeds, within 10 min | as Pre-merge | random seeds, 2 h |
| loom | | changed crates, 2 preemptions, within 10 min | every crate, 2 preemptions | every crate, 3 preemptions |
| TLC | | when a model or traced module changes, with trace validation, within 10 min | every model | larger constants, 2 h |
| Real-process crashes, writer lock, shared per-user files | | fixed seeds | every OS | random seeds, every OS |
| LazyFS power cut | | | Linux x64 | |
| Toxiproxy cut-offs and partitions | | fixed seeds | yes | random seeds |
| `kind` lead scenarios | | | Linux x64 | |

- **Set the model-based tests' case count per test**, with the `ProptestConfig` `goodfetch-test-support` builds for the running suite: 16 in Quick, 256 in the Pre-merge job that reruns them by a nextest filter, counted against the Pre-merge budget. Never set `PROPTEST_CASES`, which would lower every property test.
- **A failure in a deterministic nightly-only job is a bug, not flakiness.** A real-process or Toxiproxy failure goes through [Flaky tests and Live checks](#flaky-tests-and-live-checks) (diagnostic run, quarantine), and rule 3 of Regressions still applies when it does not reproduce. No release candidate is cut while either is open.
- **Over budget, shard, speed up or move a test to a later suite** (ADR 0061). Never lower case counts or seeds, and never drop a scenario, to fit.

### Regressions

1. A failing Simulation writes its shrunk Fault plan to a file and prints the command that replays it.
2. The fix commits the plan beside its scenario, with proptest's regression file. Committed plans run in the Pre-merge suite.
3. A failure from a non-deterministic layer (real processes, LazyFS, Toxiproxy, `kind`) is reproduced at the lowest deterministic layer that shows it, and that reproduction is the regression test. If none can, file an issue to extend the Simulation, and commit the failing seed to the real-process scenario meanwhile.
4. A TLC counterexample becomes a Fault plan and a fix to the model or design. A trace-validation failure is fixed in the code or in the stale model.
5. A test that once found a bug is never removed or weakened while its behaviour is still required; it may be replaced by a test covering the same failure.

## Flaky tests and Live checks

Decided in [ADR 0064](docs/adr/0064-tests-are-never-retried-and-unpinned-checks-run-as-live-checks.md). The Quick, Pre-merge and Release suites and the CI tiers are those of [ADR 0061](docs/adr/0061-three-test-suites-each-run-by-one-just-command-under-a-time-budget.md).

### What is a test

- **A test pins every input:** commit, Proving ground seed, `Entropy` seed, browser build, images by digest, recorded model exchanges. A **flaky test** passes and fails with the same inputs.
- **A check with an input that moves without a commit is a Live check.** It never goes in a suite.
  _Why:_ a vendor's change or outage is then never mistaken for a broken change.

### No retries

- **Runners keep zero retries with every lock on:** nextest `retries = 0` and `flaky-result = "fail"` in every profile, with no per-test override; Vitest `retry: 0` with no per-test `retry`; Playwright `retries: 0` and `failOnFlakyTests: true`; no pytest rerun plugin and no `flaky` marker.
- **No workflow step retries a test:** no retry action, no shell loop, no `continue-on-error` on a test step.
- **No test loops until it passes.** Wait on a condition with a deadline (auto-waiting, `expect.poll`, a CDP event, a state you can name); never repeat the action under test until it succeeds.
- **The no-retry check in the Quick suite (`xtask no-retry`) refuses all of the above**, and the flags and variables that undo them (nextest `--retries`, `--flaky-result`, `NEXTEST_RETRIES`, `NEXTEST_FLAKY_RESULT`; Vitest `--retry`; Playwright `--retries`; Chromium's `--test-launcher-retry-limit` other than 0, a gtest run without it, and `--num-retries`; wptrunner's `--retry-unexpected` above 0, `--rerun` above 1 and `--repeat-until-unexpected`; typ's `--retry-limit` and `--isolated-script-test-launcher-retry-limit` other than 0, `--retry-only-retry-on-failure-tests`, and a `RetryOnFailure` expectation). Downloads before the deny of ADR 0043 may retry: they test nothing.
- **Re-run a failed job only when no test step failed in it** (lost runner, failed download). The "all green" job fails when an earlier attempt of its run had a failed test step. **Name every step that runs a suite or a test runner starting with `Test`** (`Test: just test quick`, `Test: Miri on opted-in unsafe code`): that prefix is how "all green" tells a test step, and `xtask no-retry` refuses a step that runs a runner, `just test`, `just repeat` or `just miri` under another name.
- **Never assert elapsed real time, a rate or a duration.** Those are measurements.

### Before merge

- **Every new or changed test runs 20 times in the pull request**, and one failure fails it. New: absent from the base commit's listing, so a renamed test too. Changed: its file changed, plus the tests Vitest's `--changed` and Playwright's `--only-changed` reach through their imports. The run is sharded to fit the budget, with the Pre-merge job's shards (one while that job is one); the count is never lowered, and nothing skips it. A red run stops at the end of its first red iteration, since one failure has already failed it: a green run still runs every iteration. A test still on the quarantine list is not run, as in every suite.
- **Taking a test off the quarantine list takes 200 passing runs** in that pull request.
- **The stress run is `just stress [base]`**, the Pre-merge job's `Test: just stress` step. `xtask stress select` compares each runner's listing at the head (`cargo nextest list --message-format json`, `vitest list --json --staticParse=false`, which loads each file so that each `test.for` and `test.each` case is listed under the name it runs as (so the listings get the suite and test tool manifest variables `just repeat` runs with), `playwright test --list --reporter=json`, `pytest --collect-only -q`) with the base's. The cache-warming job saves the four listings of each `main` commit in the build cache (`xtask stress listings`, under the target folder's `stress-listings/<commit>/`); a base with none is listed from a checkout of it in the same job, which installs the base's own npm packages and Python environment from its lockfiles (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --prefer-offline` and `uv sync --frozen --all-extras`, from the package store and uv cache `just fetch` filled), so its Vitest and Playwright configs and its pytest tests import its own workspace packages, not the head's. Each count's runs go through `just repeat --until-red` (a parameterized pytest case by the node ID of each of its runs, `test_x[a-1-20]` …, which pytest-repeat names), and their JUnit files to `target/junit/repeat-<count>/`, which the ingest reads as run that many times.
- **Tests run in random order, seed printed:** pytest-randomly (`-p randomly --randomly-seed`), Vitest `sequence.shuffle` with `sequence.seed`, Playwright `--shuffle` with `fullyParallel`, each runner's arguments printed by `ci/order-arguments.sh`, which every recipe and Release cell passes and xtask's order-seed tests run twice on one seed and once on another; nextest runs each test in its own process. The seed is the first 8 bytes of the tree hash under test, read as an unsigned integer (`xtask order-seed`, or `ci/order-seed.sh` where no Cargo runs), in every run but the constrained test-health run, which takes a fresh one; Vitest takes its low 53 bits, the most its shuffle can count up from. Pass it back as `GOODFETCH_ORDER_SEED` to reproduce an order.
- **Vitest resets shared state:** `restoreMocks`, `unstubEnvs` and `unstubGlobals` are on, only as a safety net: `vi.stubEnv` and `vi.stubGlobal` stay banned ([Writing a test](#writing-a-test)).
- **Leaks and hangs fail:** nextest's leaky result is `fail`, every test has its suite's hang timeout, and the constrained test-health run runs Vitest with `detectAsyncLeaks`.
  - **`just` runs nextest with `NEXTEST_DOUBLE_SPAWN=0`;** export it too when you run `cargo nextest` yourself on macOS.
    _Why:_ on macOS under load, a test that started no process failed as leaky when nextest started it through its double-spawn stub: its output pipes stayed open past the 200 ms leak timeout after it exited, even with one test at a time. Started directly, it never did. On Linux the stub made no difference: there, such tests failed as leaky only with many tests at once on a CPU-starved host, as often with the stub as without, and never with one test at a time. The stub only guards against a hang when Ctrl-Z stops the runner, so the setting costs nothing on either platform.
  - **The leak timeout stays 200 ms.** On Linux, at the mutation run's settings (nice 10, four tests at once, host load up to about 50), every test of `just mutants`' baseline closed its output within 5 ms of exiting, a 40-fold margin, and six whole baselines had no leaky failure. A leaky failure there is a real leak: name the process that holds the test's output before you raise the timeout.

### After merge

- **Every runner writes JUnit XML in every tier and attempt, pass or fail.** An ingest workflow, which runs none of the product, copies it to the results store: the `test-results` repository beside the main one (private until it is public), one compressed file per run attempt, with a record per test run (test ID, runner, suite, tier, job, OS, architecture, runtime, shard, commit, tree hash, run, attempt, outcome, duration, order and `Entropy` seeds). Nothing is pruned. While the repository is private, the ingest runs after each run of a workflow with a hosted job only, and one daily hosted ingest job copies the runs that ran only on the owner's machine, such as the pull-request tier's ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)), so their flaky tests are found a day later.
- **The store's files and their records** (`xtask results ingest`, `.github/workflows/results.yml`): `runs/<year>/<month>/<run>-<attempt>.tar.gz`, by the month the attempt started, holding `run.json`, `test-runs.jsonl`, `jobs.jsonl` (each job's labels, runner OS and duration) and `junit/<artifact>/…` as written. An artifact belongs to an attempt when it was uploaded after the attempt started. A test's ID is its runner's (the quarantine list's), its runner comes from the JUnit file's name (`nextest-ci.xml`, `vitest-node24.xml`), and the file's suffix after a Vitest, Playwright or pytest runner joins the artifact's runtime. **Every job that uploads JUnit runs `ci/junit-context.sh` first**, pass or fail: it writes `context.json` beside the JUnit files with the commit and tree the job tested, which each test run is recorded under (a run's head may be another commit), the job's ID, the order seed (`ci/order-seed.sh`), the `Entropy` seed (`GOODFETCH_ENTROPY_SEED`) and, after `just repeat <count>`, the count, without which the ingest refuses the artifact. A job whose tests ran different counts of times, as the stress run's 20 and 200, puts each count's JUnit files in a `repeat-<count>/` folder instead. Only hosted jobs hold the store's token, the Actions secret `TEST_RESULTS_TOKEN`: the ingest, the test-health jobs, the diagnostic run's `select` job and the Release suite's `budgets` job. It is a fine-grained token on the `test-results` repository: the jobs that only read the store need Contents: Read, and only the ingest, which pushes, needs Contents: Read and write. Nothing falls back to `github.token`, which cannot read another private repository. It never reaches the owner's machine.
- **Allure Report 3 shows the history,** built from the store by the daily test-health job (`xtask results allure`, then `allure generate`): each test's latest result per configuration, and one history point per run attempt with its status and duration.
- **A test is flaky when** it passed and failed on the same tree hash and configuration, or when a diagnostic run passes it at least once: 20 more runs at the same commit and on the same OS, after a failure in the merge queue, a manual-dispatch Release run, the nightly tier or a test-health run. A diagnostic run never changes a verdict. The scan (`xtask results scan`, after each ingest) finds both, since a diagnostic run's results share the failed run's tree and configuration, and keeps one issue per flaky test, found again by a marker line in its body. `.github/workflows/diagnostic.yml` runs each failed test (at most 20 a run) in a job of its own, through `just repeat 20 --test <runner>=<test ID>`.
- **Test health runs from M1 on, even while the repository is private, with no paid resource:**
  - the **daily test-health job** (scheduled, Linux, `main`'s head, 15-minute timeout) runs the 10 oldest quarantined tests 50 times each, then the scan and the Allure report; while ADR 0135 keeps the workflow off, `just reports <store>`, run daily by the server's cron, lists every runner's tests and writes the [Feature report](#the-report) and the Journey report into the results store under `reports/<date>/`;
  - the **constrained test-health run** (weekly and after each manual-dispatch Release run, Linux, 120-minute timeout) runs the Pre-merge suite with half the runner's CPU and memory, a fresh order seed and `detectAsyncLeaks`;
  - these and the diagnostic runs share a ceiling of 1,000 included minutes a month, counted in the Linux-equivalent minutes of [Suites, commands and time budgets](#suites-commands-and-time-budgets). A diagnostic run of a cell on the owner's machine runs there and counts nothing. At the ceiling, constrained and then diagnostic runs are skipped until the month ends: `xtask results ceiling`, run before each, counts the month's jobs of `test-health.yml` and `diagnostic.yml` from the store (each job's minutes rounded up) and admits a constrained run only while its 120 minutes fit, and a diagnostic run while the 10 minutes of each of its hosted jobs do (`--jobs`); a skip goes to the job summary. The coverage job of [Feature coverage](#feature-coverage) counts toward it too, and starts only while the month's count is below 550 minutes, so a run that starts just below can pass 550 by up to its 60-minute timeout; the in-diff mutation job runs on the owner's machine while the repository is private (ADR 0135), so it counts nothing and never stops.
  - Once the nightly tier is on, both run every night with no ceiling.

### A flaky test found after merge

1. CI opens (or reopens) one `flaky-test` issue for it, a sub-issue of the spec that owns the test, in the milestone of that spec's ticket that added it ([ADR 0136](docs/adr/0136-each-implement-spec-run-is-one-whole-spec-and-milestones-are-ticket-metadata-and-owner-checkpoints.md)). Until [#1719](https://github.com/goodfetch/goodfetch/issues/1719), `xtask issues` still files it in the earliest open milestone with an open issue.
2. The same day, add the test to the quarantine list with its issue.
3. If the test or the code it covers changed in the last 7 days, you may also revert that change: revert the product code, and the tests stay quarantined.
4. Fix the cause. A longer timeout alone, a sleep or a retry is not a fix. A race or leak in the product is fixed in the product, and the test stays as it was.
5. The fix takes the test off the list after 200 passing runs; the issue closes when it merges.

- **The quarantine list is one file in the repository**, `quarantine.toml` at the root (upstream Chromium tests the Chromium build gate excludes are not on it: see [Goodfetch Chromium's expected results](#expected-results)). Each entry has a test ID, its open `flaky-test` issue and a date; the Quick suite's check (`xtask quarantine check`) refuses an entry without an open issue or naming a test that no longer exists. Every suite command skips the listed tests through its runner's own filter (`xtask quarantine exclude`). An entry is a `[[test]]` table:
  - `runner`: `nextest`, `vitest`, `playwright` or `pytest`;
  - `id`: the test's ID as its runner lists it: nextest's `<binary ID> <test name>` (`goodfetch-engine::it dispatch::route_waits`), Vitest's full name with its suites joined by ` > `, Playwright's file, suites and title joined by ` › `, pytest's node ID. nextest skips by name, so a name that is part of another test's name is refused;
  - `issue`: the number of its open `flaky-test` issue;
  - `date`: the day it was quarantined (`2026-10-18`).
- **Quarantined tests still run** 50 times a day in the daily test-health job (the 10 oldest entries), into the results store: `just repeat 50 --quarantined 10`.
- **A quarantined test blocks** (unlike an upstream exclusion of [Goodfetch Chromium's expected results](#expected-results)) its milestone's gate, every release (`xtask release` refuses while the list is not empty), and counts its feature as untested.
- **A flaky test is never removed or weakened to make a suite pass:** quarantine it, fix it, or replace it with a test covering the same behaviour (for example by moving its unpinned part to a Live check).

### Live checks

- **Each Live check has an owning area and a placement** listed in ADRs 0064 and 0065 (the live-model checks): real SaaS Sinks, installed Chrome's stable, beta and dev channels (pinned majors are tests), public testnets, the Clean-up rules' daily gate, the online conformance, the benchmark, the Performance series (ADR 0071), the Cloud Run deploy, live-model evaluations, Scorecard; and, from ADR 0070, the newest MCP conformance suite, Inspector and SDK clients, each agent framework's newest releases, the n8n scanner on the published node, and real Integration platform accounts; and fuzzing (ADR 0067); and, from ADR 0068, the docs site's `docs-external-links` (nightly, or before each release candidate while the nightly tier is off) and `docs-deploy-smoke` (after each production deploy), which block nothing directly ([Console and docs site](#console-and-docs-site)); and, from ADR 0073, `screen-reader` (nightly, or before each release candidate while the nightly tier is off), blocking nothing directly; and, from ADR 0074, the Chromium patch preview and the Chromium build currency, owned by area 8, which block nothing directly ([Goodfetch Chromium](#goodfetch-chromium)); and, from ADR 0089, `lend-libraries-newest` and `crawler-examples-newest`, owned by area 11 (nightly, or before each release candidate while the nightly tier is off), which block nothing directly ([SDKs](#sdks)); and, from [ADR 0097](docs/adr/0097-one-docs-site-renders-each-products-section-from-one-source-and-neither-product-leads-with-access.md), `migration-guides-current`, owned by area 11 (nightly, or before each release candidate while the nightly tier is off), which fails when a migration guide's competitor has a newer major, or a newer minor at `0.y`, or a newer patch at `0.0.z`, than the one pinned (a pin its manifest marks as held is checked within its own major, or its minor at `0.y` and its patch at `0.0.z`, and fails when its reason lapses), and blocks nothing directly.
- **Security advisories:** the gate's `just audit` checks every lockfile offline against the snapshot `ci/advisories.toml` pins, so only a commit moves its verdict; the newest RustSec, npm and PyPI advisories are the live advisory audit's, `just audit-live`, daily on `main` (from the server's cron while the hosted workflows are off), which blocks nothing directly (ADR 0064's note of 2026-10-22).
- **Outcomes are pass, fail or inconclusive.** Inconclusive means the external side was unavailable; it never counts as a pass.
- **Results go to the results store** under the `live` tier, except fuzzing's, which stay private ([Security](#fuzzing)). A Live check writes JUnit like a test, and a skipped test case is its inconclusive result. Declare each Live check with its owning area in `ci/live-checks.toml`; the scan names the area in the issue's title and fails on an undeclared one.
- **A fail, or three inconclusive runs in a row, opens or updates one `live-check` issue**, a sub-issue of the spec that owns the Live check, in the milestone of that spec's ticket that added it (ADR 0136; until #1719, `xtask issues` still files it in the earliest open milestone with an open issue). If its cause is not stated within two business days, the next minor is not promoted until it is. Patches are not held, and the online conformance never blocks (ADR 0043). Fuzzing opens no `live-check` issue: its crashes stay private and block the next minor until fixed ([Security](#fuzzing)).
- **A `live-check` issue closes** once its cause is written down and either fixed or attributed to an external party with a linked upstream report, so an explained vendor outage never holds a milestone gate.
- **A measurement declares its trials, threshold and statistic before it runs,** and every trial counts. A failed trial is never replaced.

## Test-first process

- **An agent implementing a ticket follows the `tdd` skill.** The seams named in the spec's Testing Decisions are the seams agreed in advance ([ADR 0133](docs/adr/0133-agent-work-follows-the-matt-pocock-skills-as-written-and-the-fresh-review-is-retired.md)).
- **Every behaviour lands red, then green.** The pull request's Evidence section (the `pr` skill's) lists each new test with the failure it showed before the code existed as its Before, and the same test passing as its After. Commits stay green.
  _Why:_ a test never seen failing may test nothing, and green commits keep `git bisect` and the "all green" check working.
- **A bug fix starts with a test that reproduces the bug**, named by the behaviour, not the issue.

## Review checklist

A pull request's tests meet this list, a standard the `code-review` skill applies:

1. Each test sits at a seam the spec names.
2. The Evidence section lists each new test with its red failure.
3. One behaviour per test, named as a behaviour in glossary terms, in the right place for its level.
4. Through the interface: no private call, no `#[cfg(test)]` in product code, no `pub` added for tests, no mock of owned code.
5. Expected values come from an independent source; failures are matched on Error codes.
6. Deterministic and isolated: the rules of [Injection and determinism](#injection-and-determinism), no real sleep, its own data.
7. Parameterized cases are named.
8. Snapshots are redacted, cover structured output, and every change to one is intended.
9. Fixtures are under 256 KiB, and generated ones have their `.source.toml` file.
10. No helper is duplicated; shared setup is in test support, and a new test adapter crate is on CI's list ([Shared helpers](#shared-helpers)).
11. A new decoder, listener, Plugin kind, widening setting, Secret kind or output has its tests under [Security](#security).
12. Each declared Feature is one the test asserts ([Feature coverage](#feature-coverage)).
13. Each mutant that survives in changed code is killed, or the Evidence section explains it.
14. Each new exception names evidence no pinned test can replace.
15. A spec change and its `features/stories/` file agree.
16. A new Console string is in the catalog, a new component state has its Storybook story, and every changed Reference screenshot is intended ([Accessibility, localization and visual regression](#accessibility-localization-and-visual-regression)).
17. A new or changed Chromium patch has its row in `chromium/surfaces.toml` and its tests, and each new expectation entry has its category and evidence ([Goodfetch Chromium](#goodfetch-chromium)).
18. A new message or code from a party Goodfetch did not write has its named case in its translation point's table, which also holds one case for a failure it does not recognize (ADR 0127).
19. A new lifecycle state or event adds a named case for each pair it forms, with the outcome copied from its area spec's table (ADR 0128).
20. A Proving ground page or task a feature's test needs comes in the same pull request as the feature and its test (ADR 0032).

## Suites, commands and time budgets

Decided in [ADR 0061](docs/adr/0061-three-test-suites-each-run-by-one-just-command-under-a-time-budget.md), with the private-period matrix in [ADR 0108](docs/adr/0108-while-the-repository-is-private-the-release-suite-runs-its-macos-cells-on-the-owners-machine-and-its-hosted-cells-under-a-ceiling.md) and the private-period pull-request tier and hosted budgets in [ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md).

### The three suites

Each suite runs everything the suite before it runs.

| Suite | What it adds | Command | Runs in CI |
|---|---|---|---|
| Quick | Format and lint checks; in-process tests of what changed (Rust unit tests in `src/`, never `tests/it`); the committed format fixtures; `xtask codegen --check`, `xtask wording` (ADR 0097), `xtask journeys check` (ADR 0063), `xtask clients check` (ADR 0069) and `xtask affected`'s own tests | `just quick` (`just quick all` for everything) | First job of every pull request, over the whole workspace |
| Pre-merge | The `tests/it` of every touched crate and its dependants; conformance on the local Proving ground; touched Sinks against their containers (every Sink until the merge queue exists, ADR 0032); touched SDKs, adapters and Integrations on one runtime; the Console on Chromium; docs examples; ADR 0032's static checks; the Journey task lists' drift check, `just journeys-tasks --check`, and the rule on each Journey variant's tests, `just journeys-tests` (ADR 0063), the Feature check over every runner's declarations, `just features-tests` (ADR 0072), and the Client conformance case-list check over Vitest's listing, `just clients-tests` (ADR 0069) | `just pre-merge` (`just pre-merge all`) | Every pull request, on Linux |
| Release | Everything, for every package, on the full matrix of ADR 0024; deployment-target smoke runs; tests that need released binaries | `just release` (this host's cell), `just release-ci` (the whole matrix on CI) | Merge queue, nightly, every release |

- **A Quick-suite test starts no process, browser or container and binds no socket.** Anything that does is a `tests/it` test, or its runner's equivalent.
  _Why:_ the Quick suite is what you run after every edit, so it must stay seconds long.
- **The Chromium build gate holds pinned tests in no suite** ([Goodfetch Chromium](#goodfetch-chromium)), because they need the build's source tree. Like the test V8 build job ([Security](#confinement)), it is its own required check beside "all green".
- **Checks that are non-deterministic by nature are in no suite**: real SaaS Sinks, live models, the online Proving ground, real vendors, Chrome's beta and dev channels, the benchmark, the Performance series. They keep their own tiers.
- **The Release suite gates every merge**, through the merge queue. Before the merge queue exists, it runs on manual dispatch, and passes before each milestone's owner test and before any release.
  _Why:_ a macOS, Windows, arm64 or runtime failure found after the merge lands on `main`, which ships nightly.
- **While the repository is private, a manual-dispatch Release run is a smaller matrix** ([ADR 0108](docs/adr/0108-while-the-repository-is-private-the-release-suite-runs-its-macos-cells-on-the-owners-machine-and-its-hosted-cells-under-a-ceiling.md)):
  - Linux x64 and arm64 (glibc and musl) and Windows x64 run on GitHub-hosted runners;
  - macOS arm64, and macOS x64 under Rosetta 2 (labelled `macos-x64 (Rosetta 2)`), run on the owner's machine;
  - native macOS Intel and Windows arm64 first run once the repository is public: the first release's build publishes nothing until the release pipeline's Release run passes on the full native matrix, while GitHub still offers an Intel macOS image (fall 2027 at the latest). Rosetta 2 serves the macOS x64 cell only while the owner's machine runs macOS 27 or earlier; macOS x64 support past either date is decided in [#546](https://github.com/goodfetch/goodfetch/issues/546).

  The owner's-machine cells run only on `main`, so no pull request or fork reaches that machine through the Release suite; with no branch protection while private, write access is the boundary. A passing run is every one of those cells green on one commit of `main`, from one or more dispatches (`just release-ci` takes a commit of `main`, its head by default, and `main`'s workflow refuses any other). A cell held back by the ceiling, timed out in the queue or failed with no failed test step may run again; a cell with a failed test step leaves the commit red (ADR 0064). The workflow is `.github/workflows/release-suite.yml`: its first job runs `xtask release-suite plan`, and `xtask release-suite verdict --commit <sha>` says whether every cell is green on a commit across its dispatches, as `just release-ci` prints after each run, on any branch, naming each cell that is not green, a skipped one included. The cells are `linux-x64-gnu`, `linux-x64-musl`, `linux-arm64-gnu`, `linux-arm64-musl`, `windows-x64`, `macos-arm64` (with the visual projects beside it, in the pinned image in the owner's machine's VM) and `macos-x64`; the runtimes follow [SDK and CLI tests](#where-they-run): every line on `linux-x64-gnu`, the oldest and newest elsewhere, free-threaded CPython on every Linux cell.
  _Why:_ the full matrix on hosted runners costs about 1,350 of the 2,000 included minutes a run, nearly three quarters of it macOS.
- **While the repository is private, the pull-request tier runs on the owner's machine** ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)). Every job of the tier runs there, ADR 0072's in-diff mutation job included, and whatever builds or runs the repository's code runs in a Linux VM that Lima creates for the job and destroys after it: pinned by digest, with no secret, a `contents: read` token and nothing writable from the host but the job's workspace. Only pull requests from branches of this repository run; a fork's job fails before its first step. Nothing falls back to GitHub-hosted runners. Once the repository is public, the tier goes back to hosted Linux runners.
- **Run the VM's userland as Linux x86-64, under Rosetta for Linux.** The VM is a Lima VZ VM, Linux arm64 with Rosetta for Linux on, and the tier's toolchains, tools and images are their x86-64 builds, so the checks pinned to Linux x64 (visual tests, count snapshots, `xtask perf`, the size estimate, `cargo fuzz check`, the in-diff mutation job) keep their platform. A check that fails under Rosetta runs natively on Linux arm64 while private, alone, with a note in the ADR that pins it. If Rosetta for Linux ends with macOS 27, every check moves to Linux arm64 ([#546](https://github.com/goodfetch/goodfetch/issues/546) decides it).
  _Why:_ 183 pull requests were opened in the 30 days before 2026-10-18, and at 10 hosted minutes each the tier alone would take more than four times the minutes left.
- **When a check waits for the owner's machine, say so and wait.** A job queues while the runner is offline and fails after 24 hours; the machine never sleeps on power. Before merging, read `gh pr checks <number>`, and never merge while "all green" is pending or failed. If a check is pending and `gh api repos/goodfetch/goodfetch/actions/runners` shows the `owner-machine` runner offline, tell the owner. A failed job with no runner name in `gh run view <run-id> --json jobs` never reached the runner: re-run it with `gh run rerun <run-id> --failed` once the runner is online. A job that reached the runner and failed is an ordinary failure, never re-run, except a job with no `Test` steps whose figures are deterministic, re-run once the owner accepts its change (`perf`, and `size` where it gates). The owner gets GitHub's default Actions notifications of failed runs.
- **Keep the hosted jobs of manual-dispatch Release runs under 600 included minutes a month.** The organization's private repositories share the account's 2,000, so at most 400 are left for every other hosted job, which ADR 0135 budgets: the ingest after each run with a hosted job, 90; one daily ingest job for the runs that ran only on the owner's machine, 60; and the checks run by hand (ADR 0065's Live checks, ADR 0043's online conformance), 90, whose recipes refuse a dispatch that would pass it. That is 240; the other 160 stay unallocated. A line past its budget opens or updates a `needs-triage` issue from the daily test-health job. Count in Linux-equivalent minutes: Linux 1, Linux arm64 1, Windows 2, macOS 11. The workflow's first job reads the month's count and estimates the run from its cells' last recorded durations (270 before any is recorded). If the run would pass 600, no hosted cell starts: the summary names the minutes needed and the date the month turns, the run fails, and no diagnostic run follows. A dispatch off `main` starts its hosted cells only if a run on `main` still fits after it. A run whose own estimate passes 600 still starts as the month's first Release run on `main` whose hosted cells start, and opens a `needs-triage` issue, like a budget breach.

### Commands

- **Run `just quick` before every push.** `just hooks` installs an opt-in pre-push hook that does it.
- **`just test <suite> [packages]`** runs one suite's tests without lints. CI jobs call it, and so can you.
- **`just repeat <count> --test <runner>=<test ID>`** runs a test `count` times whatever each run's result, as a diagnostic run does; `--quarantined <n>` takes the quarantine list's n oldest entries, and `--tests-file <file>` a file of `<runner>=<test ID>` lines, as the stress run hands it its selection. A runner whose arguments would pass the operating system's limits (Linux's 128 KiB for one argument, `getconf ARG_MAX` for all) runs in batches, each with the same counts and its part of the tests, each batch's JUnit file kept as `<runner>.<batch>.xml`. Every run counts, so it is no retry. With `--until-red`, given first, as the stress run gives it, the run stops at the end of its first red iteration: nextest runs one iteration a command (`--stress-count=1`, since nextest's `--max-fail` ends a stress run in the middle of an iteration), each iteration's JUnit file kept as `<runner>.<batch>.<iteration>.xml`, and after a red one no later iteration, runner or count runs. A selected LazyFS power cut (`lazyfs::`) runs past nextest's default filter where `GOODFETCH_LAZYFS` names a LazyFS build, and fails the run elsewhere rather than being dropped: a Linux host that runs the local gate builds LazyFS once (`just lazyfs-build` prints the executable's path), keeps that `bin/` folder outside the gate's checkout, whose `target/lazyfs/` each gate clears, and exports `GOODFETCH_LAZYFS` to it.
- **`just stress [base]`** runs the stress run of [Before merge](#before-merge) against `base`, the merge base with `origin/main` by default. The incremental local gate narrows it, `just stress "" <since> <workspace member>…`: only the new and changed tests of the workspace members the change since an earlier gate run's head reaches, plus each test whose file that change changed; Vitest, Playwright and pytest keep every one of their new and changed tests, so they run in the head's order as in a full run ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)).
- **`just gate-backstop [ref]`** is the incremental gate's backstop ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)), daily from the gate host's cron on `main`'s head (the milestone branch's during M1), from the main checkout: `just pre-merge all` in full (`GOODFETCH_GATE_FULL=1`) in a gate worktree of its own, then each result an incremental run of `just merge` on the same tree reused and the full run found red, a selection miss, listed in `~/gate-backstop/misses.txt` and failing the backstop; a suspect crate of a red per-member step reruns alone to confirm it. Fix each miss in the gate's selection or evidence, with a test that plants it.
- **`just linux` and `just visual` run, on a host that is not Linux, what only the Pre-merge suite's Linux job checks:** the tests only Linux decides (`tc netem`, the Secret Service, the IPv6 Happy Eyeballs check, the extra loopback addresses) in the CI image with every Linux OS service, and the visual projects in the pinned Playwright image, each through Docker or Podman. Without either, `just linux` is listed as skipped and `just visual` fails, as ADR 0073 has it; `just screenshots-update` writes Reference screenshots in that image while GitHub Actions is off. `just pre-merge` runs them beside its host steps, with `just perf`, and runs `just stress` after its tests. A test that only Linux decides joins `ci/linux.sh`'s selection.
- **`just counts`** prints each counted row's figure and fails over its limit, and `just counts --valgrind` each row read under Valgrind ([Quality register](#quality-register)).
- **`just fmt`, `just lint` and `just build`** format, check and build Rust, TypeScript and Python.
- **What changed comes from `xtask affected`**, in CI and locally; `just quick all` and `just pre-merge all` run over the whole workspace. Locally it diffs the working tree, uncommitted changes included, against the merge base with `origin/main`. Rust selects through `rdeps(…)`, so dependants run too. A crate whose tests run another crate's executable through the test tool manifest names that crate in `[package.metadata.goodfetch] runs`, and a pnpm or uv package names it in its `package.json`'s `goodfetch.runs` or its `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.goodfetch] runs`. A change to that crate, or to a crate its build depends on (a normal or build dependency, not a dev-dependency), reaches them as a dependency's would: `goodfetch-conformance` names the Proving ground and both Products, so a change to the Proving ground or to the engine runs the self-test suite, and the test-support packages name them too, so the same change runs their tests and their dependants', the Console's and the SDKs'. The Quick suite runs in-process tests only (nextest's `quick` profile: no `tests/` binary), so it runs no test that starts an executable, whatever `xtask affected` selects.
- **A local run lists what it could not run on this host, and why**, such as containers without a container engine, or another OS. Its exit code covers what ran. CI is the verdict.
- **`just release-ci` dispatches the Release suite on your pushed branch** and follows it to the end. While the repository is private, on `main` it also runs the macOS cells on the owner's machine, which stands in for the reference host, and on any other branch the hosted cells alone; it takes the cells to run, all by default, and on `main` a commit of `main`, its head by default: `just release-ci windows-x64,macos-arm64 <sha>` ([ADR 0108](docs/adr/0108-while-the-repository-is-private-the-release-suite-runs-its-macos-cells-on-the-owners-machine-and-its-hosted-cells-under-a-ceiling.md)).

### Time budgets

Budgets are the median (p50) of a job's last 20 runs in its own tier, on GitHub-hosted runners with warm caches: pull-request runs for Quick and Pre-merge; merge-queue runs (manual-dispatch runs while the repository is private), nightly and release runs for Release. Cells on the owner's machine are labelled with it and kept out of the budgets; they keep the 90-minute job cap. While the pull-request tier runs on the owner's machine, its Quick and Pre-merge budgets are measured on its runs there, labelled with the machine: each job from its start, and "all green" from the first job's start, so waiting for the machine is not counted ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)).

| Suite | Where | Budget | Hard cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Quick | Locally, one crate changed, incremental build | ≤ 60 s (guidance) | none |
| Quick | CI, whole workspace | tests ≤ 2 min, job ≤ 10 min | 20 min |
| Pre-merge | Pull request | "all green" ≤ 20 min, each job ≤ 15 min | 30 min per job |
| Release | Merge queue | ≤ 60 min per group, each job ≤ 45 min | 90 min per job, 120 min for the queue |
| Release | Nightly and release pipelines | each job ≤ 3 h | 5 h per job |

- **While the repository is private, the hosted Release cells' hard caps are past 90 minutes** (ADR 0061's agent decision of 2026-10-23): `linux-x64-gnu` 300, `linux-arm64-gnu` 210, `windows-x64` 150 and the musl cells 120, as one 2-vCPU job holds each cell's build, tests and, on the gnu cells, both benchmark builds. A cap stops a hang; the budget above is unchanged.
- **Per-test caps stop hangs, never measure speed.** In the Quick suite a test is marked slow after 1 s and killed at 10 s; above it, slow after 30 s and killed at 5 min. nextest's `quick` and `ci` profiles, Vitest, Playwright and `pytest-timeout` all use these two levels.
- **A suite that outgrows the kill runs in parts, never under a longer cap** (ADR 0064). The page suite runs as one nextest test per Feature group, `page::the_page_suite_proves_<group>_against_collies_attached_engine`, each on a Proving ground copy and an engine of its own, through `ProtocolSuite::with_capabilities`; `page::the_page_suites_parts_take_each_of_its_capabilities_once` checks that the parts take every capability of `conformance/page/cases.toml` once, so a new capability joins a part, or a new part, in the change that declares it. A part that comes near the kill on a loaded host splits along its capabilities into parts of their own, as the clean-up part did into `clean_up_blocking` (the Filter lists, the never-block entries and `browser.block_resources`), `clean_up_blocking_off` and `clean_up_hiding`. Each part's `Covers:` line names the Features its cases cover that its run reaches, as the exercise audit reads them, and each keeps its own document.
- **No run fails for being slow within its cap.**
  _Why:_ benchmark suites on shared runners vary by about 2.7% run to run (CodSpeed's measurement), and whole jobs vary more, so a one-run timing gate would be flaky.
- **A breached budget opens an issue** through the budget check, run nightly and at the end of every manual-dispatch Release run (its `budgets` job), labelled `needs-triage`. Fix it within two weeks: shard the job, speed up the tests, or move a test to a later suite with the reason in its pull request.
  - The check is `xtask budgets`. It reads each job's last 20 successful runs in its own tier from the results store, and each suite's "all green" or group time from its runs' first attempts; it compares their median with this table, and opens or updates one issue per breached suite or job, listing the 20 durations. A tier with no row here, or a row without the budget it needs, is noted as `missing-budget`. Its tests read this table, so rewording it fails them.
  - It never fails a run. While the repository is private, it runs as the last job of each manual-dispatch Release run (`release-suite.yml`'s `budgets`); it moves to the nightly tier once that is on. A run whose last job ends before its first starts is left out of a whole-run median and noted as `negative-span`.
  - The Quick suite's "tests ≤ 2 min" is not checked: the store keeps no step timings.
- **A stress run of new and changed tests, if the pull-request tier runs one, counts against the Pre-merge budget.** Shard it to fit; never lower its run count to meet a budget.

### Sharding, caching and reports

- **Shards are deterministic:** nextest archives once per target and splits with `--partition hash:m/n`; Vitest and Playwright use `--shard`; pytest uses `pytest-xdist` inside one job. Never split by recorded timings.
- **While the repository is private, each Release cell is one job holding one shard** (`ci/release/cell.sh`): it archives the target's tests once and runs `--partition hash:1/1`. A second job per cell would spend setup minutes under ADR 0108's ceiling and pass the archive through the account's 500 MB of Actions storage. A cell that breaches its 45-minute budget is split into a build job and shard jobs named `<cell> shard <k>/<n>`, which the ceiling's estimate and the verdict count as that cell's.
- **The tests of the pinned Chrome for Testing build run on the cells that have it.** Chrome for Testing has no build for Linux arm64 or musl, where Collie launches the distribution's Chromium (ADR 0024), so `.config/nextest.toml`'s `ci` profile leaves those tests out on `linux-arm64-gnu`, `linux-arm64-musl` and `linux-x64-musl`, by name, with the reason: each launches the build from `browser_cache`'s Browser cache, or reads which build launches or whether a browser is installed. No test yet launches a distribution's Chromium on those cells.
- **Caches save only from `main`**: a cache-warming job on every push to `main` builds without testing and saves `Swatinem/rust-cache`, the V8 build, pnpm's and uv's caches (keyed by their lockfiles); the nightly and manual-dispatch Release runs save the other targets'. The merge queue saves none, since pull requests can't restore its caches. Chromium, images and released binaries are cached by digest.
- **The warming job shares the pull-request jobs' rust-cache `shared-key` and profiles.** Linux pull-request caches take priority within GitHub's 10 GB; a larger cache is declined while there is no revenue (ADR 0076), so fit within it.
- **Past 9 GB, the least recently used caches that are not the pull-request tier's Linux caches are deleted until the total fits** (`xtask evict-caches`, through GitHub's cache interface), so the priority holds before GitHub's own eviction. The pull-request tier's cache keys start with `pull-request-`, or with `v0-rust-pull-request` for rust-cache under a `shared-key` starting with `pull-request`, and a Linux one names `Linux` as rust-cache and `runner.os` write it. While the repository is private, the eviction runs at the end of each Release run on `main` (its `caches` job), since those runs save the GitHub caches and the warming job's token holds no write scope on the owner's machine.
- **While the repository is private, the build cache is a folder on the owner's machine** ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)). The cache-warming job runs there in a VM after each push to `main`, one run at a time, and is the only job that mounts the folder writable; pull-request VMs mount it read-only and copy what they need. The tier uses no GitHub cache. Hosted Linux jobs, such as test health and the coverage job, restore the Linux caches that manual-dispatch Release runs on `main` save.
- **Size reports are saved by tree.** Every merge-queue run, and while private every manual-dispatch Release run on `main`, uploads each target's report of both Products (`xtask size`, the thin-LTO `ci` profile) as the artifact `size-report-<tree>-<target>`, kept 90 days, where `<tree>` is the tested commit's tree. A run that needs `main`'s report asks for it by name: `gh api 'repos/goodfetch/goodfetch/actions/artifacts?name=size-report-<tree>-<target>'` with `main`'s head's tree. A `main` whose head no Release run tested has no report: the estimate and the increase check then report each file as "no baseline" and gate nothing on growth, as a benchmark with no base is reported and never gated (ADR 0071); the increase check still fails a crate tarball over its ceiling.
- **CI test builds use the `ci-test` Cargo profile** (`test` with `debug = "line-tables-only"`, and the dependencies at `opt-level = 1`: the Proving ground's tests run 4.5 times as fast, for twice the dependencies' build time; debug assertions and overflow checks stay on).
- **CI's Linux builds link with mold and its Windows builds run on a Dev Drive**; local builds keep the default linker and disk. Hosted jobs get both from the host-tools action (`rui314/setup-mold` and `samypr100/setup-dev-drive`, pinned by SHA, the workspace's `target` a junction to the Dev Drive), and the owner's machine's VM jobs get mold from the CI image, at the version `ci/tool-versions.sh` holds equal. Each sets `-Clink-arg=-fuse-ld=mold` for its target, since Rust's own default on x86-64 Linux, rust-lld, ignores the system linker.
- **Every run writes JUnit XML**, local or CI. Every CI job uploads it, pass or fail, as `junit-<suite>-<tier>-<os>-<arch>[-<runtime>]-<shard>`.
- **Every test job writes its failed tests to its summary page**, pass or fail: `ci/junit-summary.sh` (with `vm` in the owner's machine's VM) runs `xtask junit-summary` after `ci/junit-context.sh`. The page lists each failed test of `target/junit/` with its failure's first 15 lines and the `goodfetch-test-seed=<n>` lines it printed, or says that none failed. A JUnit file it cannot read is listed and fails the step, never skipped. pytest writes each failed test's captured output into its JUnit file (`junit_logging`), where the seed line is.
  _Why:_ a contributor reads the failure and the seed that replays it without opening the logs (ADR 0061).

### Merge queue

GitHub offers the merge queue to a private repository only on GitHub Enterprise Cloud, so it starts when the repository is public in the organization ([ADR 0108](docs/adr/0108-while-the-repository-is-private-the-release-suite-runs-its-macos-cells-on-the-owners-machine-and-its-hosted-cells-under-a-ceiling.md)). Until then the Release suite runs on manual dispatch, as above. On that day:

- **Turn on the queue for `main` with ADR 0061's settings:** "Only merge non-failing pull requests" on, a status check timeout of 120 minutes, and group size and build concurrency within the free plan's 5 concurrent macOS jobs. "all green" and the Chromium build gate are required there too.
- **Run the Release suite on `merge_group`,** on the full native matrix of ADR 0024 (native macOS Intel and Windows arm64 included), on hosted runners only: `release-suite.yml` stops naming `owner-machine` and loses its ceiling, and the allowlist loses its entries.
- **In the queue the Chromium build gate only takes its fast path,** failing on a digest with no verdict attestation for the group's head, and the Release suite fetches the Goodfetch Chromium by digest from the committed manifest (ADR 0074).
- **In a group, each entry's base is the tree of the entry before it**, which is how GitHub builds groups, so its increase check reads the size report keyed by that tree.
- **The queue saves its per-target size reports and no cache.** Pull requests cannot restore a cache saved on a queue's `gh-readonly-queue/…` branch: GitHub's cache scopes give a pull request its own, its base's and the default branch's, and name no queue branch ([dependency caching](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/dependency-caching)). Its jobs set `save-if: false` on their caches.
- **`main` reruns no tests after a merge,** and pull requests then run only the touched Sinks: `xtask affected` switches its `sinks` output to the touched set (#134's `sinks-touched`).

## AI features and agent skills

Decided in [ADR 0065](docs/adr/0065-ai-features-are-tested-without-live-models-and-measured-by-oracle-graded-evaluations.md). Suites are the Quick, Pre-merge and Release suites of [ADR 0061](docs/adr/0061-three-test-suites-each-run-by-one-just-command-under-a-time-budget.md); Live checks are those of [ADR 0064](docs/adr/0064-tests-are-never-retried-and-unpinned-checks-run-as-live-checks.md), in no suite. A **live model** is one a provider serves and Goodfetch doesn't pin (a cloud model, directly or through a gateway); a pinned local model on a pinned server is a test input.

### Stand-ins for a model

| Layer | Stand-in | Suite |
|---|---|---|
| In-process AI logic | the Scripted model | Quick |
| Each adapter's wire format | Model recordings replayed by `httpmock` at the adapter's base URL | Pre-merge |
| The binary, Journeys, AI end to end | the Proving ground's Fake model, in all three formats | Pre-merge |
| SDK code that calls a model | a model callback registered through the SDK handler | Pre-merge |
| Local model servers | the real server, pinned, with a pinned small model | Release (Linux cell) |

- **No test of an AI feature calls a live model.** Only the Live checks below reach one.
  _Why:_ providers no longer offer determinism: current Anthropic models refuse `temperature`, Gemini ignores it, and OpenAI's `seed` is best effort.
- **The Scripted model fails on a call beyond its script and on unused steps.** Script errors and faults as replies, and assert on the requests it recorded.
- **AI Journeys name the Fake model as their Model provider.** Ask it for faults (rate limit, overload, timeout, cut stream, malformed JSON, refusal, wrong but plausible answer) through the task, never through a test-only switch in the binary.
- **Check Usage against the Fake model's own token count.**

### What an AI test asserts

- **Assert outcomes Goodfetch owns:** the Success check result, Grounding, the Plan file, the Effect journal, the Record, the Error code, Usage, what the oracle saw. Never a model's wording.
- **Never rely on temperature or a seed for determinism.**
- **Snapshot the whole request each AI operation sends, per format, with `insta`** (Quick). A prompt or tool-description change then shows as a reviewed diff.

### Model recordings

- **Record each cell of the matrix:**
  - per format on the direct provider: text, tool call and result turn, parallel tool calls, structured output, streaming of each, usage (cached and reasoning tokens), prompt caching, search grounding, image input, and the errors the engine branches on;
  - the named gateways, for the OpenAI-compatible format;
  - each hosted Decision provider (Jev, OpenAI's Decisions API): a choice, an abstention, a low-confidence choice, and its errors.

  A missing cell fails a Quick check. While there is no revenue ([ADR 0076](docs/adr/0076-tests-and-ci-run-on-free-tiers-and-ai-calls-go-through-the-vercel-ai-gateway.md)), cells are recorded through the Vercel AI Gateway and labelled `via gateway`, which counts; the direct-provider, other-gateway and hosted Decision provider cells, and Gemini's native format, which the gateway does not serve, wait and are reported missing without failing.
- **Match on method, path and the canonical JSON body.** An unmatched request fails, and so does an unused recording.
  _Why:_ matching on the path alone replays a stale answer to a changed request.
- **Replay each stream whole and re-cut at seeded boundaries.** The stream parsers also have `proptest` and a fuzz target.
- **Keep only allowlisted headers at write time:** `content-type`, the version and beta headers the adapter sends, `retry-after` and the rate-limit headers the adapters read. Remove key query parameters, and replace request and organization IDs. A Quick check fails on anything else, or on anything shaped like a provider key.
- **Record only against Proving ground pages and tasks.**
- **Refresh on cause:** a changed request, a provider-check report, or 30 days before a manifest model's retirement. Use the recorder job, which opens a pull request, and review the diff. Never refresh on a timer or automatically.
- **Write faults that aren't worth recording as explicit `httpmock` stubs**, marked as synthetic.

### Pinned local models and the in-engine model

- **llama.cpp server and Ollama run in the Release suite's Linux cell**, each at a pinned version with a pinned small model downloaded by digest before ADR 0043's deny, with the dependency installs. vLLM joins them on its CPU build where it can serve the model.
  - Assert protocol behaviour only.
  - Send every sampling parameter the engine relies on.
  - Record the server version and model digest.
- **In the release pipeline**, every model on ADR 0012's tested list runs the same tests. vLLM runs there on its CPU build; a GPU host is declined while there is no revenue (ADR 0076), so where the CPU build can't serve the model, vLLM waits. LM Studio runs there where a runner supports its headless server.
- **The in-engine Decision provider model is checked on a labelled set with a margin:** on Linux in the Pre-merge suite, and on every OS in the Release suite. Never compare confidences exactly.

### Live checks that reach live models

- **The provider check runs nightly and compares shapes, never content.** It covers fields, types, stream events, error bodies and the headers adapters read, for every recorded cell: direct providers, gateways and hosted Decision providers (while there is no revenue, only the cells recorded `via gateway`; ADR 0076). A difference opens or updates its `live-check` issue.
- **Every live model call goes through the Vercel AI Gateway on its free tier** ([ADR 0076](docs/adr/0076-tests-and-ci-run-on-free-tiers-and-ai-calls-go-through-the-vercel-ai-gateway.md)): one key, `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, free-tier models only, no credits bought and auto top-up off, so no job can spend money. A `429` or an exhausted free credit makes the run inconclusive, never a purchase.
- **Every live job stops itself at its per-run cap.** The engine's runs use a currency Limit, Claude Code uses `maxBudgetUsd`, Codex has no budget setting of its own and calls models only through the gateway key, whose free credit caps it since the key cannot spend, and the provider check and recorder compute their worst case before starting. Each job prints its worst case first.
- **Coding agents in live jobs make no other calls:** Claude Code runs with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1`, and Codex with its update check and analytics turned off.
  _Why:_ the job reaches only its named model hosts, and ADR 0043's deny refuses anything else.
- **Live jobs run on the gateway's free tier from M4.** Fork pull requests never receive model keys.

### Evaluations

- **Evaluations are `bench/` campaigns on the local Proving ground**, never tests. They come in three sizes: pull-request (only for changes to prompts, request snapshots, catalog descriptions or skills), nightly (plus a weekly run on larger models), and ADR 0025's release benchmark.
- **The oracle grades every trial.** No model grades an Evaluation. Record the engine's Success check and Grounding results beside the oracle's verdict.
- **Run 3 trials per task and model** at the pull-request and nightly sizes, each from a clean Goodfetch home. Every trial counts.
- **Report** the verified rate with its 95% Wilson interval, pass^3, false successes, safety violations, tokens and dollars per verified result, and the Success check and Grounding disagreements with the oracle. State the bound that "zero" can claim at the trial count (about 3/*n*).
- **A false success or a safety violation fails an Evaluation at any size** and blocks the next release candidate as a correctness failure.
- **The regression rule** is the mean over tasks of the paired difference in verified rate against the last accepted baseline on the same models. The difference is in percentage points (never a log ratio), with a block-bootstrap interval. It fails only when the interval's upper bound is below −Δ. An inconclusive result is reported, never a failure.
- **Δ is proposed at 10 points and waits for the owner's approval.** Until then, a regression opens or updates a `live-check` issue and blocks no release candidate.
- **A task joins its regression set at pass^3 ≥ 0.9 on every nightly model for five nights.** Add harder tasks once a regression set passes everything for 30 nights. Add a task for every AI bug fixed.
- **The pull-request size uses development seeds; nightly and release use held-out seeds.**
  _Why:_ prompts tuned on the tasks that judge them overfit.
- **Pin models in the manifest, chosen from the gateway's free tier, and run old and new side by side for five nights before dropping one.** An Evaluation or skill Evaluation whose model is not in the free tier waits, reported inconclusive, until revenue (ADR 0076). Record the model, any fingerprint the provider returns, the gateway, the local server's version and digest, the commit, the seed and the prompt snapshot hashes.

### Agent skills and tool descriptions

- **Skills are checked with no model:**
  - in Quick, `xtask codegen --check` and the Agent Skills frontmatter limits;
  - in Pre-merge, a check that every operation, command and option they name exists in the catalog, and a run of every example command against the local Proving ground, checked by the oracle.
- **If area 11 ships the skills as a Claude Code plugin, run `claude plugin validate --strict`** at a pinned version in Pre-merge.
- **Skill Evaluations run the CLI with and without the skills**, through promptfoo, on Claude Code (small, mid and large models) and Codex. The oracle grades; `skill-used` is a signal. Open an issue on any skill that doesn't beat its baseline on some model.
- **Snapshot the catalog's MCP projection per Product and profile** (Quick). Keep each description at most 2,048 characters, and record each profile's total tool-definition size in the snapshot.
- **The tool-selection Evaluation checks a model's first tool call without executing it.**

### Manual tests

- **After a confirmed Evaluation regression, read the failed trials' Run traces** before accepting or fixing it.
  _Why:_ only a person can tell a wrong grade or a new failure mode from a real regression.
- **LM Studio**, where no CI runner can host its headless server: run the tested-list cases by hand before each release.

## Journeys

Decided in [ADR 0063](docs/adr/0063-end-to-end-tests-follow-a-catalog-of-numbered-journeys-kept-in-the-repository.md). A Journey is a goal a person reaches through Goodfetch's public surfaces; Journeys are the end-to-end tests.

### The catalog

- **One Journey per file, `journeys/J-NNN.toml`**, validated by `journeys/journey.schema.json`, which `xtask codegen` emits. Unknown fields fail.
- **A new Journey takes the number in `journeys/catalog.toml`** and increments it in the same pull request. If another pull request merged the same number first, take the next one.
- **Never change an ID on `main`, and never reuse one.**
- **Never change a Journey's `goal` under its ID.** Fix wording, steps, data or surfaces freely; for a different goal, set `status = "retired"` with `reason` and `replaced_by`, keep the file, and add a new Journey.
- **Write `goal` with RFC 2119 words**, readable as a specification without the code.
- **Write steps declaratively** ("export the Dataset as CSV"), never as clicks or flags, so the wording survives a change of implementation. Pin a step with `on` only when the goal needs that surface; give `manual` a reason only when no automated check can do it.
- **List every surface on which a person can reach the goal**, each with the milestone by which it must be proved, never earlier than the surface ships.
- **Name the Journey's `product`:** `collie` when Collie carries every step, `foxhound` otherwise. Its surfaces are that Product's.
- **A Collie Journey with a `cli` surface also runs as `J-NNN@foxhound-cli`:** the same test, given Foxhound's executable, in the same suites. It is the only variant that names a Product.
  _Why:_ it shows end to end that Foxhound's executable keeps Collie's behaviour, where the other containment checks show only that Collie's definitions are there.
- **Fill `covers` with Feature IDs** (`row:CO-18` for an ADR 0037 row; every kind is in [Feature coverage](#feature-coverage)).
- **Keep a Journey to its goal.** Edge cases, Error codes and limits go to unit, integration and conformance tests.

### Data and isolation

- **Each Journey that uses Proving ground pages has its own `seed`.** Never share one between Journeys. A Journey that touches no page has none. `GOODFETCH_TEST_SEED` never overrides a Journey's seed.
- **Pin each Proving ground task a Journey names with its `digest`** from the task list. The digest ignores the port of every URL, wherever it appears in a string value (an absolute link inside a Markdown body included), so the same seed gives the same digest on every run.
- **Commit the task list of each seed the catalog uses with `just journeys-tasks`**, which writes the Proving ground's `tasks --seed N` to `journeys/tasks/seed-<N>.json` and removes the list of a seed no Journey uses. `xtask journeys check` reads those lists, so the Quick suite starts no Proving ground; `just journeys-tasks --check`, in the Pre-merge suite, fails on a committed list that differs from the Proving ground's, is missing, or is of an unused seed. Never edit a list by hand.
- **Each variant run starts from scratch:** its own Proving ground on port 0 with the Journey's seed, its own Goodfetch home and cleared environment, `resolve` mapping the Proving ground's hosts to loopback, and its certificate authority trusted through the per-domain exception.
- **An AI Journey names the Fake model as its Model provider:** its variant's Proving ground serves it, and the setup steps point the Model provider setting at it through a public surface ([ADR 0065](docs/adr/0065-ai-features-are-tested-without-live-models-and-measured-by-oracle-graded-evaluations.md)). Never the Scripted model, never a live model.
- **Build the starting state through public surfaces** in the first steps. Never copy in a run file, database or home folder, except an older-format file for an upgrade Journey.
- **Run against the local copy only.** Online results stay conformance reports.
- **Drive the shipped artefacts**: the Products' executables and the SDK packages built from the commit, never an engine linked into the test. An `sdk-rust` variant drives its Product's shipped executable through the crate's client, as a Daemon, never the engine the crate can link ([ADR 0069](docs/adr/0069-sdks-and-the-cli-are-tested-against-a-scripted-engine-and-the-binary-of-the-same-commit.md)).
- **When a generator change alters a task a Journey names, the digest check fails: check the Journey still fits, and update it, digest included, in the same pull request.** Task ordinals are append-only within a family; never reuse one.

### Tests and results

- **Carry the variant, its Journey's ID and its surface, where the runner selects by them:**
