  - nextest: a module `j002` (the ID lowercased, hyphen dropped) in the test's path, and the test named after the surface with `_` for `-`: `journeys::j002::cli`, `journeys::j002::foxhound_cli`;
  - Vitest: the tags `J-002` and `sdk-typescript`, which `vitest.config.ts` declares from the catalog's files and the surfaces Vitest runs, `strictTags` on;
  - pytest: `@pytest.mark.journey(id="J-002", surface="sdk-python")`, registered, `strict_markers` on; keywords, since `-m` matches only those;
  - Playwright Test: the tags `@J-002` and `@console`.
  _Why:_ one Journey file holds several variants, such as `cli` and `foxhound-cli` in one executable crate, so the ID alone cannot select one.
- **Put a surface's Journey tests in a `journeys` folder of the package that ships it**, one file per Journey: `tests/it/journeys/j002.rs` in Rust, `tests/journeys/J-002.test.ts` (Vitest) or `J-002.spec.ts` (Playwright) in TypeScript, `tests/journeys/test_j002.py` in Python.
- **Start a variant's Proving ground, home and environment through test support** (`goodfetch-test-support`, or `goodfetch-test-env` through the `test-support/` packages), and drive the shipped binary.
- **A Rust variant (`cli`, `foxhound-cli`, `sdk-rust`, `http-listener`) is written with `JourneyRun`**, which takes the executable of the Journey's Product as a value: `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_goodfetch-collie")` or `-foxhound` in that Product's executable crate, the test tool manifest's entry (`tools()?.path(Tool::Collie)` or `Tool::Foxhound`) in its Rust crate's `sdk-rust` variants, Foxhound's manifest entry for a Collie Journey's `foxhound-cli` variant (Collie's executable crate takes no Foxhound crate), and any path for a skew run. When `GOODFETCH_TEST_ENGINE` is set, `JourneyRun` drives that executable in place of the one passed, and a `cli` variant written without `JourneyRun` takes its executable through `journey_engine(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_goodfetch-collie"))`, which returns the variable's path when it is set and the built executable otherwise, so a skew run reaches every variant.
  - Call `step` for each step, with the file's exact wording and in its order. A `manual` step is recorded `manual` without being called. A step called out of order, twice or with another wording is recorded `failed` without running; after the first failure no body runs, every later step and check is `not_reached` and no result goes to the oracle. At `finish`, each step never called is recorded `not_reached` after those called, so a skipped step fails the variant and every reader refuses its document.
  - Call `check` for each check that names no task, and `expect_task(check, &json!({ "status": …, "records": … }))` with the tool's result for each check that names a task, by the check's wording: the helper submits it to the variant's Proving ground's oracle for the check's task, records the answer, and records the check `passed` only when the answer is `verified`. Each such check is judged on its own submission, so two checks naming one task (two Crawls of one site) take two submissions, and the document holds the task once per check, each with its answer.
  - A check whose `surfaces` lists some variants' surfaces belongs to those variants only, as an SDK package's install checks belong to J-002's `sdk-*` variants: the helpers neither run nor record it in another variant, and the reader expects it only in those variants' documents (`foxhound-cli` takes `cli`'s). `journeys check` fails a check scoped to a surface the Journey has no variant on, or naming a task the Journey does not pin.
  - A step pinned `on` another surface drives that surface in its body, through that surface's own helper: the CLI through `run.command()` in any variant.
  - Each run writes its document as `<id>@<surface>.json` in the test's own folder, which `Finished::path()` names, and also in the folder `GOODFETCH_TEST_RESULT_DOCUMENTS` names: `just test` sets it to `target/result-documents/`, and each Release cell (`ci/release/cell.sh`) to `target/junit/result-documents/`, so the results store keeps its documents with its JUnit files. Read a run's document from `path()`: in the shared folder, another test running the same variant (the helper's own tests do) may write after it.
- **A `console` variant is written with the Playwright Journey helper**, `@goodfetch/test-support/journey`'s `journey(id, surface, async ({ step, check, expectTask, cli, daemon, provingGround, page, context, signIn }) => …)`, in `console/<package>/tests/journeys/J-NNN.spec.ts`. It reads `journeys/<id>.toml`, validated against `journey.schema.json` and parsed with `smol-toml` (`journey_invalid` otherwise; `other_journey` when the file holds another ID, `unlisted_surface` when the Journey has no variant on the surface), and declares one test of test support's Playwright `test`, tagged `@<id>` and `@<surface>`, with the annotation `journey` naming `<id>@<surface>`. Its `daemon` runs the executable of the Product the file's `product` names, with the Proving ground of its `[proving_ground]` table on the Journey's own seed and none without one, which neither `GOODFETCH_TEST_SEED` nor a `provingGroundSeed` around it changes; `{ engine: <path> }` runs another executable, for a skew run. `step`, `check` and `expectTask(check, { status, records })` record by `JourneyRun`'s rules above; a step pinned `on = "cli"` runs the executable through `cli(args)`, in the variant's home and cleared environment. Once the body ends, the test fails with `variant_not_proved` unless the run is `proved`. Its facts are attached to the test however it ended, and the configuration's reporter, `@goodfetch/test-support/result-document-reporter`, writes the variant's document as `<id>@<surface>.json` into `GOODFETCH_TEST_RESULT_DOCUMENTS` (`target/result-documents/` outside `just test`), deriving its outcome through `journeyDocument`, and writes no case log. When one run runs the variant in several projects, a failed document is kept over a later one. `journey(…, { file })` runs a Journey file outside the catalog, for test support's own tests. Its Console is the one the Daemon serves; while that Console has no sign-in (area 12), a variant that signs in passes the Console that stands in for it through `test.use({ consoleServer })`.
- **Journey tests run with their package's integration tests**: in the Pre-merge suite when the package is touched, and in the Release suite.
- **One test per variant.**
- **Each variant run writes one result document with `kind = "journey"`.** Never set its outcome by hand: derive it from the steps, the checks and the oracle's answers, through the Rust constructor (`goodfetch_protocol::result_document::JourneyDocument::new`) or a helper that passes the shared outcome vectors: `@goodfetch/test-support`'s `writeJourneyDocument` or `goodfetch-test-support`'s `write_journey_document`. A step not reached, or any Proving ground task not `verified`, fails the variant, and so does an error that failed the variant's test outside its steps and checks: test support's `ResultDocumentReporter` writes the errors of a Playwright test that did not pass into the facts' `test_errors`, so a page error or an accessibility violation found at teardown (`failOnErrors`, `axe`) leaves the variant `failed`, never `proved`. Readers load documents through `goodfetch_protocol::result_document::read`, which recomputes the outcome and rejects a document that disagrees, then pass each `journey` document with its typed Journey to `read_journey_document`, the only way to a `JourneyDocument` from stored bytes, which rejects one whose steps, checks and named Proving ground tasks are not exactly its Journey's, in order, or that records a step `manual` the Journey does not mark manual (or the reverse). The shared outcome vectors, the comparison's among them, are in `test-support/fixtures/outcome-vectors/`.
- **Run chosen variants with `just journeys`:** `--id J-002`, `--surface cli`, `--milestone M1` and `--area 3`, each repeatable (values of one flag add variants, different flags intersect). It runs `xtask journeys select`'s filters, one per runner, through `just test pre-merge`, after naming each chosen variant no test carries (from the runners' listings), and fails when none of them has a test rather than run none and pass. `--engine <path>` runs them against that executable, through `GOODFETCH_TEST_ENGINE`, which `JourneyRun` and `journey_engine` read and the test tool manifest's `collie` and `foxhound` entries take; `--tag <tag> --engine <path>` runs the tag's own Journey tests and helpers, from a temporary worktree it removes, against it (ADR 0066). The runners of `mcp-*`, `skills`, `adapter-*` and `integration-*` variants are named by their own tickets; until then `select` lists those variants as skipped.
- **See each variant's outcome on `main` with `xtask journeys report --store <results store>`** (ADRs 0063 and 0094): every variant of an active Journey, by milestone, area and Product (`foxhound-cli` under Foxhound), from the result documents `main`'s latest Release run kept, each loaded through `goodfetch-protocol`'s reader. A variant is `proved` or `failed` as its documents give across the Release cells (`failed` when any is), `rejected` when the reader refuses one of them, never `proved` then, and `unproved` when the run kept none. With `--listings <folder>` (the runners' listings), a variant whose test ran in a Release cell that kept no document of it, as when the test panicked before `finish`, is `failed`. `--out <folder>` also writes `journeys.json` and `journeys.md`. `just reports` writes it beside the Feature report.
- **The catalog check fails** on a file that does not parse, an ID that differs from its file name, repeats or is not below the next free number, a Proving ground task missing from the task list for the seed or whose digest differs, a test naming a retired or unknown Journey or an unlisted surface (`foxhound-cli` counts as listed only on a Collie Journey that lists `cli`, with `cli`'s first milestone), two tests for one variant, a Journey test that carries no single variant, a variant milestone earlier than its surface, or a retired Journey without a reason. The rules on tests read the runners' listings, so they run in the Pre-merge suite (`just journeys-tests`, which also notes each variant no test carries yet); the others run in the Quick suite.

### Owner tests

- **List an owner test's "Journeys to try" from the catalog:** every variant of its milestone, by ID and title, with its manual steps, plus the milestone's `manual` exceptions of [Feature coverage](#feature-coverage). `xtask journeys report --owner-test M1` prints it, both Products' Journeys in one list, each `manual` exception whose Feature is due at the milestone after them; paste it into the owner test's issue, never write it by hand. The daily test-health job attaches it as `journeys-to-try.md`.
- **An owner test is ready only when every automated variant of its milestone is `proved` on `main`,** and no Feature due at its milestone is untested ([Feature coverage](#feature-coverage)).
- **A finding on a goal no Journey covers adds a Journey.**
- **An owner test installs both Products' executables** from a local build, and its list holds the Journeys of both Products.

## Upgrades and version mismatches

Decided in [ADR 0066](docs/adr/0066-upgrades-are-tested-from-a-frozen-corpus-and-skew-from-every-released-minor.md). Suites are the Quick, Pre-merge and Release suites of [Test suites, local commands and time budgets](https://github.com/goodfetch/goodfetch/issues/276).

### The format registry

- **Every versioned format is registered in `goodfetch-protocol`** with its version, schema and rule. A format read by an engine or spoken by a peer and missing from the registry is a bug.
  _Why:_ every check below is generated from the registry, so a registered format cannot miss one.
- **Expected outcomes come from the compatibility functions**, never from a hard-coded version pair. Each function is itself tested against a literal table.

### The Compatibility corpus

- **A new format lands with its `compat/<format>/head/` set and its scenario**: a `compat-<format>` example in the package that owns the format, which `xtask compat write` runs with the folder to write into.
- **Never edit a fixture by hand.** Regenerate `head/` with `xtask compat write`; `xtask compat check` fails on drift.
- **Never change a frozen set** (`compat/<format>/<major>.<minor>/`). If the reading of an old file must change, change its snapshot in a reviewed pull request whose changelog entry says so.
  _Why:_ a frozen file is what a released version really wrote.
- **Change a format's schema only with a version bump** once a frozen set exists. `xtask compat check` fails otherwise.
- **Bump a format's version only on `main`.** A patch never changes one: `xtask release` compares a patch's `head/` versions and schemas with the `x.y.0` tag's.
- **Freeze from the release tag.** `xtask compat freeze --tag <tag>` copies the tag's `head/`, never `main`'s, which may hold unreleased versions. The release's entry in `compat/releases.toml` records its format versions, and `xtask compat check` requires a frozen set for each.
- **Fixtures cover the whole schema:** every property in a JSON document and a non-null value in every column. They hold synthetic data and Secret references only.
- **A corpus test is golden.** It reads, writes back and compares with its snapshot. A migrated SQLite store must also match a fresh store's schema. Checkpoints resume, Plans replay without a model, Recipes run and transcripts replay.
  _Why:_ "it opens" misses a misread.

### `upgrade-files`

- **Each source in `compat/upgrade-paths.toml` is tested from every frozen set:** golden result, idempotent, the previous major's files unchanged, unrelated files untouched. A source not in the table and a newer major are refused.

### Refusals

- **Every refusal is tested from a fabricated version**, generated per format by `xtask compat refusals` into `crates/goodfetch-protocol/generated/refusals/<format>/`, one file per case: a newer minor, an older major, a newer major, and each exception's own outcome (Usage-ledger family, Response cache, Clean-up rules, Operator policy, and the lowest minor of the shared stores of [ADR 0098](docs/adr/0098-both-products-share-one-goodfetch-home-and-one-daemon-per-major-run-by-the-fullest-product-installed.md)). The Error code and message are snapshotted. Between an engine and a Browser host only another major is refused: a fabricated `hello` of another minor checks that both sides settle on the lower one, and a Session needing more than an older Browser host's minor checks `browser_host_outdated` (ADR 0096).
- **A handshake refusal is the reply to `hello`**, before any other message, and the connection then closes. Never test refusal with a time budget.
- **Test each handshake refusal twice:** in the Quick suite, in process, by sending the fabricated `hello` to the handshake code over an in-memory transport; in the Pre-merge suite, through the conformance harness's own `hello` to a running engine. No build carries a version override.
  _Why:_ the Quick suite starts no process and binds no socket.
- **The Product check is a handshake refusal too** ([ADR 0095](docs/adr/0095-both-products-speak-one-protocol-from-one-catalog-marked-by-product-and-their-sdks-share-collies-client-packages.md)): a Client that needs Foxhound is refused by a Collie engine with `not_in_product`, before the version check; a Collie Client is accepted by a Foxhound engine; a Group member of the other Product is refused by the peer protocol's handshake; a `hello` that says it is Goodfetch Cloud's gateway makes a Collie engine's `not_in_product` give ADR 0096's gateway `fix`; and a Browser host's `hello` meets no Product check. Each case is tested in process in the Quick suite; in the Pre-merge suite, `hello`'s through the conformance harness and the peer handshake's through a Daemon of the other Product joining a group.

### Released artifacts

- **Skew covers every released minor of the current major**, latest patch of each: SDKs, each Product's Rust crate's client, the CLI and the MCP server against the current Daemon, the SDKs over stdio through their Product's engine-path variable, `GOODFETCH_COLLIE_ENGINE_PATH` or `GOODFETCH_FOXHOUND_ENGINE_PATH` (every Attached engine applies the Daemon's rule at `hello`; this variable is how its version can differ from its SDK's), the Console, the Browser host in both directions (older engines against the current Browser host, and the current engine against older Browser hosts, which settle on the lower minor) and first-party Plugins.
- **An old Client runs its own release's Journeys** ([ADR 0063](docs/adr/0063-end-to-end-tests-follow-a-catalog-of-numbered-journeys-kept-in-the-repository.md)) from its tag, against the current binary given by path. Each variant keeps its own Proving ground and Goodfetch home. A Journey that asserts something outside semver is fixed on `main`, and its variant is excluded by variant ID (`J-NNN@<surface>`), with a reason, in `compat/skew-exclusions.toml`.
- **Skew runs per Product:** each Product's Clients, SDKs over stdio and Console build against the current executable of the same Product, and each old Journey against the current executable of its Product. An older Collie opens the current files of the formats both Products carry; an older Foxhound, those of Foxhound's.
- **Test a pair across Products where a promise covers it:** the Product check of `hello` that [ADR 0095](docs/adr/0095-both-products-speak-one-protocol-from-one-catalog-marked-by-product-and-their-sdks-share-collies-client-packages.md) adds, for every pair of Products, with fabricated `hello`s as version refusals are (the [Product check's cases](#refusals)); and, where [Collie and Foxhound installed on one machine](https://github.com/goodfetch/goodfetch/issues/486) lets two installs share a file, an older Collie opening the one the current Foxhound wrote. A released Collie Client is not run again against Foxhound's executable: the containment checks show Foxhound serves Collie's operations unchanged.
- **Rolling upgrades run under load** in a three-member group, once with the Lead Daemon last (a StatefulSet's default order with the Lead Daemon as pod 0) and once with it first, then a promotion from an older Group backup and the rollbacks.
- **The Helm upgrade starts from a release's chart,** installed by digest from `compat/releases.toml`, then runs `helm upgrade` to the current chart and `helm test`. Never `ct install --upgrade`.
  _Why:_ it upgrades from the git target branch and passes with a skip on a SemVer-breaking bump or an unchanged chart.
- **Rolling upgrades and the Helm upgrade run on Foxhound,** whose load includes a Partitioned crawl. A Quick check renders Collie's and Foxhound's charts with `helm template` and fails on any difference but the image.
- **Older engines open the files the current engine wrote.** The Usage-ledger family and the list of installed executables open; the Run index, the Schedule store, the Saved identity store, user configuration and the Operator policy open unless their lowest minor is newer ([ADR 0098](docs/adr/0098-both-products-share-one-goodfetch-home-and-one-daemon-per-major-run-by-the-fullest-product-installed.md)); a current Checkpoint, Plan, project settings file and Schedule file are refused with the upgrade message. Two majors run side by side on one machine.
- **Old artifacts come only from `compat/releases.toml`,** fetched from the pinned artifact mirror with the trusted root before the outbound deny, and checked by digest and by `gh attestation verify --bundle --custom-trusted-root`. One local command fetches the same set.
- **Never skip for a missing artifact.** A missing or mismatched artifact fails the job. An empty version list runs the current build against itself.
  _Why:_ a test that passes because nothing ran hides decay, as SQLite's and Cargo's skipped old-binary tests do.

### Suites

| Check | Suite |
|---|---|
| Corpus golden reads, fabricated refusals of files and of `hello` in process, compatibility-function tables, `upgrade-files` from fixtures, `xtask compat check` | Quick |
| Corpus replays, handshake refusals through the conformance harness, `upgrade-files` against the binary | Pre-merge |
| Every test that needs released artifacts: the skew matrix, rolling upgrades, older engines, two majors, the Helm upgrade | Release |

- **Tests that need released artifacts belong to the Release suite**, which the merge queue runs. Its nightly rerun and its run on each release's own bytes cover them; none is a nightly-only job.
- **Group each older minor's skew checks into as few jobs as fit the job budget.** One job per check exceeds the 20 concurrent jobs after two minors.
- **Never drop a minor, and never move a skew check out of the merge queue.** A Release suite budget breach is fixed with fewer or faster jobs per minor, or with more concurrency on standard runners; larger runners are declined while there is no revenue (ADR 0076).
  _Why:_ the Release suite has no later suite to move to, and an untested minor decays.

### Two Products on one machine

Decided in [ADR 0098](docs/adr/0098-both-products-share-one-goodfetch-home-and-one-daemon-per-major-run-by-the-fullest-product-installed.md). Skew between the two Products' released versions follows [ADR 0094](docs/adr/0094-both-products-are-released-and-signed-together-and-each-test-runs-on-collie-unless-it-needs-foxhound.md).

- **Run both executables of one commit in one Goodfetch home** (Pre-merge suite):
  - a Foxhound Client that meets a Collie Daemon gets a Foxhound Daemon once the Collie one hosts no live run;
  - a Foxhound installed but never run becomes the pick when the `daemon restart` that a refusal's `fix` names under `engine: daemon` runs, from the path the Client would run attached;
  - a Collie Client refused by version on a machine with Collie alone is told to run its own executable's `daemon restart`, a Rust Client to install its Product's executable at its version first, and either is told to install Foxhound at its version only when its own executable would still not be the pick, while an older Foxhound that can be the pick counts as installed;
  - when only a linked program has raised a store's lowest minor past every installed executable, `daemon start` fails with the version refusal, and an `auto` Client runs attached;
  - after a newer Collie raises the Run index's lowest minor past an installed older Foxhound, the pick is that Collie, the Foxhound Daemon takes no new run and hands over to it once its live runs end, and the Console and Collie's Schedules keep working;
  - a Collie Client never moves the Daemon to Collie while a Foxhound that can be the pick counts as installed, so Clients of the two Products alternating restart it at most once;
  - a Client the Daemon refuses runs attached with a warning under `engine: auto`, and fails at connect with `not_in_product` under `engine: daemon`;
  - a Collie run and a Foxhound run through one proxy set to `politeness.proxy.concurrency: 1` never have two requests in flight through it, and a 429 a Collie page meets leaves the Politeness file's domain limits unchanged.
- **"Installed" and the clean-up run on the test `Clock`** (Quick suite): an entry whose executable is gone still counts within `browser_cache.max_unused_age`; a build pinned by a counting entry or declared by an installed Plugin, launched within that age, or held by a running browser is kept; an entry the engine cannot read is kept; a program's entry that links a newer Foxhound through its Rust crate, and an executable's entry gone from its path with no versioned copy, keep their builds but are never the pick.
- **A newer engine raises the lowest minor only for what an older one would misread** (Quick suite, from fabricated versions): a newer minor that adds a Run index row an older engine reads leaves the marker unchanged, and one that writes a user configuration key its minor added raises it to that minor.
- **An older Collie opens every file Goodfetch home shares, as the current Foxhound wrote it** (Release suite, with ADR 0094's skew): the Usage ledger, the known-certificates store, the Politeness file, the list of installed executables, the Run index, the Schedule store, the Saved identity store, user configuration, the Operator policy, the Plugin store and the Browser cache. Each holds Foxhound's own entries, such as a run and a Schedule that need Foxhound, Foxhound's setting keys of a minor the older Collie knows, a Sink Plugin and the Politeness file's domain limits. The older Collie keeps them untouched and deletes none of the current Foxhound's downloads. It reads the Daemon's runtime file and tokens, the Console key and the active-major marker to find and reach the Daemon. The Response cache, run folders and the per-install database follow their own rules: it runs without a newer Response cache, and refuses to resume a run a newer engine wrote.
- **Kill the clean-up after its rename** with the real-process crash tests: the next run finishes the deletion, and no browser ever launches from a half-deleted build.
- **A Collie engine leaves Foxhound's entries untouched:** rewriting user configuration keeps Foxhound's keys byte for byte; resuming a run that needs Foxhound answers `not_in_product` and leaves the run file's hash unchanged; a Collie Daemon records each occurrence of a Schedule that needs Foxhound as missed with `not_in_product`.

## SDK and CLI tests

Decided in [ADR 0069](docs/adr/0069-sdks-and-the-cli-are-tested-against-a-scripted-engine-and-the-binary-of-the-same-commit.md).

### SDKs

- **Unit tests run against the Scripted engine in-process**, through an in-memory transport; integration tests and Journeys run the Product's executable built from the same commit. `sdk-rust` Journeys run with a Daemon only; `linked` is never a Journey mode.
  _Why:_ the binary can't fabricate a refused `hello` or a crash mid-call, and a fake alone passes where the engine fails.
- **Scripts live only in `test-support/fixtures/engine-scripts/`**, never inline in a test. Every message step in them must validate against the committed JSON Schema bundle; only raw-byte steps, deliberately not JSON-RPC, are skipped.
  _Why:_ one validated corpus keeps the stand-in true to the engine for every SDK at once.
- **Pass the Product's engine-path variable and `GOODFETCH_HOME` in the SDK's environment-map option**, as the test-support fixtures build it from `goodfetch-test-env`. Never set them in the test's own process; an Attached engine gets exactly that map as its environment.
- **Process faults use `goodfetch-scripted-engine`**, reached as an Attached engine through the Product's engine-path variable (TypeScript and Python) or as a Daemon through a runtime file in the test's Goodfetch home (every SDK). No build of either Product carries a fault switch. In TypeScript, test support's `scriptedEngineEnv(testEnv, product, script, home?)` builds the environment map, installing the corpus's script as `scripted-engine/script.json` under the home it names; a Client conformance case tagged `attached` runs on it or on the Product's executable, never on the in-memory player.
- **No process outlives its test:** the test-support fixtures fail a test that leaves an engine, Daemon or handler process alive after teardown.
- **Each Product's SDK packages run against their own Product's executable**, and each implements every Client conformance case and passes the completeness checks for its own Product's catalog.
- **Every Client conformance case in `conformance/clients/` is implemented on every surface it lists**, carries its case ID, and runs in both of its surface's modes: named cases `attached` and `daemon` in TypeScript, Python and the CLI, `linked` and `daemon` in Rust, which links the engine and starts no Attached engine. A Quick-suite check fails on a missing implementation or an unknown ID.
  - Each case in `conformance/clients/cases.toml` lists its `surfaces` (`cli`, `sdk-rust`, `sdk-typescript`, `sdk-python`) and, for a capability that exists in some modes only, its `modes`.
  - A `cli` test carries its case the way a nextest Journey test carries its variant: a module `client_conformance::<case>` (the ID with `_` for `-`) in its path, and the test, or its rstest case, named after the mode: `client_conformance::hello_refused::refuses::case_1_attached`. It sits in Collie's or Foxhound's executable crate.
  - An `sdk-typescript` test carries its case as the Vitest tag `client_case/<id>` and its mode as the tag `attached` or `daemon`, both declared from the case list, so `strictTags` refuses an unknown case. It sits in a TypeScript SDK package under `sdks/typescript/`.
  - `xtask clients check` (in `just lint`) fails on a case missing on a listed surface in one of its modes, a test carrying an unknown case, a case on a surface or in a mode the case does not list, or no mode (or, in Vitest, two), and when the case list is missing. A test behind a `cfg` (on it or an enclosing module, `cfg(test)` aside) or `#[ignore]`d implements nothing, since it may never run, nor does a skipped or todo Vitest test: the one exception is a test behind the `cfg` of the one platform its case lists in `platforms`, such as `platforms = ["unix"]` for a case whose Windows half has no test yet. It reads the `sdk-typescript` tests from Vitest's listing, so that surface is checked in the Pre-merge suite (`just clients-tests`) and only noted in the Quick suite; it reads `sdk-rust` and `sdk-python` from M2, and until then notes a case they list.
- **For Client conformance cases, each runner writes a case log; only `goodfetch-conformance`'s writer turns it into a result document**, deriving each outcome through `goodfetch-protocol`'s constructor. A log entry never carries an outcome. A declared case missing from the log is `unproved`. In TypeScript the writer is the Vitest reporter `CaseLogReporter` ([Shared helpers](#shared-helpers)): one entry per case a test that ran carries, with the checks the test recorded through `clientCase` and a last check, `the test passed`, so a test that fails outside its checks fails its case; a skipped test writes none. `goodfetch-conformance write <case list> <header> <case log> <document>` turns a log into its document. Journeys write their own documents ([Journeys](#journeys)). The entry type is in `goodfetch-protocol` and its schema in the bundle; the script format's schema is `test-support/fixtures/engine-scripts/script.schema.json`. Both come from `xtask codegen`.
- **Lifecycle conformance cases** ([ADR 0078](docs/adr/0078-an-sdk-program-opens-an-explicit-engine-connection-and-closes-only-what-it-started.md)) run in every SDK: two connections at once in one process, a second `connect()` after `close()`, `closed` on a call after `close()`, nothing written to stdout or stderr by the SDK, no signal handler or event-loop patch after import or `connect()`, Ctrl+C reaching only the test's own process, a Borrowed page left open after its borrow ends, and an Attached engine's unexpected exit raising an error that carries its stderr tail.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Run a crawl inside a web service" and "Add an AI step to an existing Playwright program", and the lifecycle failures competitors shipped (ADR 0078).
- **Error and outcome conformance cases** ([ADR 0079](docs/adr/0079-failures-raise-grouped-typed-errors-and-expected-outcomes-are-values.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine: each Error code raises its class under its group; a code the SDK does not know raises its group's class with the code kept; unknown outcome kinds, Block causes, skip reasons and Verdicts are kept, and an unknown Verdict is never `verified`; after a dropped connection an in-flight read is re-sent, while an in-flight Effect raises `UnavailableError` and is never re-sent; each SDK declares its language in `hello` and shows the target the engine rendered; and no error or call log carries a Secret canary's value. The engine's target renderer is tested once per SDK language in the Quick suite, and a Quick-suite check fails when codegen would emit a class name that shadows a built-in of any SDK language.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Extract a typed object with a chosen model", "Crawl two page types into a Dataset" and "Reach a goal from one instruction, with limits and a verdict", and the false successes and stringly errors competitors shipped (ADR 0079).
- **Trace context conformance cases** ([ADR 0132](docs/adr/0132-each-request-may-carry-its-callers-trace-context-across-goodfetchs-own-links-and-the-engines-spans-stay-experimental.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine: a call inside an active OpenTelemetry span sends its `traceparent` and `tracestate` in `_meta`, a call outside one or without the OpenTelemetry API sends none, Baggage is never sent, and an SDK handler runs under the Trace context the engine's request carries. The engine's side runs through an in-process OTLP collector: the receiver rules, both span kinds, the run's root with its link, propagation over each link of the protocols in that link's tests, and no `traceparent`, `tracestate` or `baggage` header in what the Proving ground, the Fake model, each Sink's container and an `httpmock` webhook endpoint receive.
  _Why:_ a header that reached a site would mark the request as automated, and a Trace context dropped at one link splits the caller's OpenTelemetry trace (ADR 0132).
- **Time conformance cases** ([ADR 0080](docs/adr/0080-sdk-timeouts-nest-waits-arm-when-called-and-a-program-sets-limits-but-never-raises-them.md)) run in every SDK: an inner call ended by its outer timeout and naming it, `0` refused, the null form meaning no timeout, a response that arrives before its waiter is awaited still caught, a signal, a cancelled task or a dropped future reaching the scripted engine as `$/cancel` and stopping its work, a call stopped by another Client failing with `call_cancelled` while the caller's own cancellation never raises it, a Human pause not counted, a Limit reached ending the Run `stopped` and `incomplete`, and the sleep tombstone failing with `removed` in TypeScript and Python, and Rust's failing to compile (a compile-fail test).
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Fill a dependent form and read the results" and "Run a crawl inside a web service", and the timing, hang and cancellation failures competitors shipped (ADR 0080).
- **Concurrency conformance cases** ([ADR 0081](docs/adr/0081-each-sdk-follows-its-languages-concurrency-model-and-shapes-events-by-meaning.md)) run in every SDK: Python's sync API inside a running ipykernel loop and from several threads at once; Python's async API under asyncio and under Trio, and a call from another loop failing at once; Ctrl+C during a sync call reaching the scripted engine as `$/cancel`; a listener's exception raising `listener_failed` once, on its value's next call; a `Subscription` and an iterator unsubscribing on close, on leaving the loop and on release; an unread lossless iterator slowing its producer; a regular expression with look-around refused at the call, and `\d` and `\w` matching as in the pattern's own language; the async Python client starting an Attached engine under asyncio's selector loop on Windows, with no event-loop policy set; and in Rust, handles checked `Clone + Send + Sync + 'static` at compile time, a `Crawl` checked `Send + Sync` and its `serve` future `Send` at compile time, a handler that declares an Effect keeping that future `Send`, a crawl served from a task spawned on a multi-threaded Tokio runtime with handlers that borrow and handlers that own their captures, a handler that holds an `Rc` across an `.await` failing to compile (a compile-fail test, [ADR 0112](docs/adr/0112-a-rust-crawls-handlers-and-their-futures-are-send-in-axums-form-so-serve-runs-in-a-multi-threaded-server.md)), the crate's futures run on a single-threaded executor, each Product's `blocking` module (`goodfetch_collie::blocking`, `goodfetch_foxhound::blocking`) used inside an async runtime without panicking, and an async connection closed and dropped inside a running Tokio runtime without panicking.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenario "Run a crawl inside a web service", and the concurrency failures competitors shipped (ADR 0081).
- **Typed data conformance cases** ([ADR 0082](docs/adr/0082-sdk-schemas-come-from-each-languages-own-schema-types-payloads-are-typed-per-label-and-results-keep-what-the-sdk-does-not-know.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine: a schema in the language's idiom and a hand-written JSON Schema both reach the engine as the same JSON Schema; a candidate a native rule rejects is retried with that rule's message; a validator that throws stops the call with `schema_validator_failed` in TypeScript and Python, while in Rust a `Deserialize` error is retried and a panic unwinds in the caller; a non-JSON value is refused with its field path and never stringified; a wrong or oversized Payload fails its enqueue with `payload_invalid`; a resume after a Payload's JSON Schema changed is refused with `payload_schema_changed` before any work, while a Payload only a changed native rule rejects fails its own request with `payload_invalid`, never retried; a native rejection outside extraction fails its call without a retry; a result field or value the SDK does not know is kept and nothing raises.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Extract a typed object with a chosen model", "Crawl two page types into a Dataset" and "Resume a killed crawl and retry its failures", and the schema-library breaks and stringified payloads competitors shipped (ADR 0082).
- **Settings conformance cases** ([ADR 0083](docs/adr/0083-a-program-sets-settings-in-one-typed-option-explicit-values-beat-the-environment-and-no-value-is-silently-ignored.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine: a call's argument beats `settings`, and the nearest scope's `settings` beats an enclosing scope's and the project file's; an explicit `engine` beats `GOODFETCH_URL`; `timeout.run` on a Run and `timeout.connect` anywhere in `settings` fail with `setting_not_applicable`, a project file's `timeout.connect` bounds `connect()`, and one in user configuration is refused; an unknown `GOODFETCH_` variable, an empty one and an unparsable one each fail `connect()` before any call; the other Product's engine-path variable, set to a path that does not exist, is recognized and not read, so `connect()` succeeds and the TypeScript and Python SDKs start the Attached engine that their own Product's variable names (ADR 0090); an environment map passed as an option replaces the process's, which is read once and never at import; an unknown key, a wrong type, a setting no scope beneath can use (`browser.*` on a page, or set where a Borrowed browser's Session opens) and a locked or capped value each fail where the scope opens with `setting_unknown`, `setting_invalid`, `setting_not_applicable` or `setting_locked`, never clamped or ignored; a `browser.locale` set where a Session opens that conflicts with a `stealth` Identity profile or an imported login fails with `setting_locked` naming the setting that fixed it, while an inherited one yields; a capability the platform lacks fails with `capability_unavailable`; a project file sent to an Exposed Daemon that would widen a reserved allowance fails with `setting_locked`, while a Recipe's lock wins over it quietly, as on a local engine; `explainSettings()` names each value's layer and origin, and no log, error or `gf.engine` shows `GOODFETCH_TOKEN`'s value; the project file is found upward from the working directory, another is named by `project`, and `null` reads none; a misspelled key in a literal fails `tsc`, pyright and mypy, and a wrong value type for a Rust key fails to compile (a compile-fail test).
  _Why:_ they are the configuration failure points of every golden scenario, and the swallowed options, surprising precedence and import-time environment reads competitors shipped (ADR 0083).
- **Page API conformance cases** ([ADR 0085](docs/adr/0085-collies-page-api-keeps-playwrights-names-strictness-waits-values-cross-as-json-and-unanswered-requests-fall-through.md)) run in every SDK, those that need a page against the binary of the same commit on Proving ground pages (ADR 0069): an ambiguous target that becomes unique before its deadline is acted on, while one still ambiguous at the deadline raises `target_not_unique` listing every match in the SDK's language; two gestures sent at once on one page never interleave, while a read and an armed waiter run beside them, and a locator or route handler that clicks runs while the gesture it interrupted waits, without a deadlock; a locator handler that throws fails the action it interrupted with `page_handler_failed` and stays registered; a route that settles nothing falls through to the route registered before it, page routes before Session routes, and then to the network, and later routes run first; an `alert`, `confirm` or `prompt` with no handler, or with handlers that answer nothing, is dismissed, while a `beforeunload` dialog is accepted and its navigation completes; in TypeScript and Python, a second answer raises `route_already_handled` and a route handler that throws aborts its request and raises `page_handler_failed` from the call waiting on it, while in Rust a route or dialog dropped unanswered gives its default; a page function returning a DOM node, `NaN` or a cycle raises `page_value_invalid` naming its path and kind, one reading a variable of the program raises `page_function_failed` whose fix names the argument, and one in an isolated world does not see the page's globals; `locator.evaluateAll` hands its function every match, an open shadow root's included, and `locator.waitForFunction` the one match, resolved again at each check, until it is truthy; an init script runs before the page's own scripts on the next navigation and in a new frame until its `Subscription` closes, and Provenance marks the Session's main-world injections, none for a page without one; with `site_preferences.respect_use_signals` on and a declared Purpose, a Collie Run's own `goto` to a page whose Content Signal refuses that Purpose still loads it and records the signal in Provenance; a body read after `waitForRequestFinished` never fails, one the browser dropped raises `body_unavailable` whose fix names `waitForRequestFinished`, and one cut mid-transfer raises `response_truncated`; each event `page.on` takes fires on the Proving ground's page events page with ADR 0085's data, under the subscription's credits, a listener removed by its `Subscription` no longer fires, and `requestfailed` reports a blocked, an aborted and a failed request as Playwright does; a `text=` selector or a `:has-text()` pseudo-class raises `selector_invalid`; and a CDP session is refused with `capability_unavailable` on Firefox and WebKit, and on Chromium marks the Session's results unguarded.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Fill a dependent form and read the results", "Crawl with HTTP and a browser only where needed" and "Add an AI step to an existing Playwright program", and the driver traps competitors shipped (ADR 0085).
- **Page state conformance cases** ([ADR 0121](docs/adr/0121-service-workers-run-under-the-engines-interception-the-back-forward-cache-is-off-locators-reach-closed-shadow-roots-and-a-read-never-scrolls-a-list.md)) run in every SDK against the binary of the same commit on the Proving ground's page states, on Chromium unless named. On the page whose service worker answers one JSON call from its cache and passes another to the network, with `browser.service_workers` unset: the service worker's own request to a domain the run's policy forbids is blocked, and one a Filter list matches fails as `BlockedByClient`; a Session route sees the service worker's request and a page route does not; the call the service worker passes on arrives in the page's `response` event with `fromServiceWorker()` true, its network leg seen only by the Session route; the cached answer arrives in the page's `response` event, a `waitForResponse` and a crawl's kept responses with `fromServiceWorker()` true, and no route answers it; `page.request` to the cached URL reaches the network; in a crawl handler, a navigation the service worker passes on to the network takes one permit from the navigation gate, held until the page's load event, and one it answers from its cache takes none; with `browser.service_workers: block`, `register()` rejects, `register` is still the browser's native function, and every request of the page reaches its routes, also in a Profile directory where the service worker was registered before; on Firefox and WebKit no service worker registers, an explicit `allow` raises `capability_unavailable` naming `block`, and an `allow` inherited from the Run yields to `block`; on Firefox `navigator.serviceWorker` is absent and nothing is injected; on WebKit the Session's Provenance carries the stub's mark, and a Profile directory used in place that holds a service worker registration is refused with `capability_unavailable` naming `state_file`. After leaving a page and going Back, `goBack` returns the document's `Response`, fires `domcontentloaded` and `load`, a `waitForLoadState('load')` armed before it passes, and in a crawl handler the navigation takes a permit from the navigation gate. On the virtualized list of 1,000 rows, `extract`, `allTextContents` and an `ariaSnapshot` return only the rendered rows and leave the list's scroll position unchanged; an `ai` aria snapshot marks the list with 1,000 rows declared and the number rendered; and the docs recipe collects all 1,000 rows, each once. A target inside a closed shadow root, attached by script and declared with `<template shadowrootmode="closed">`, is found by `getByRole` and CSS, clicked through the guard and shown in an `ariaSnapshot`; an `<input>`'s user-agent root is never matched; XPath does not reach it; the page's own `element.shadowRoot` is still `null` and `attachShadow` still native; and on Firefox and WebKit the same locator raises `deadline_exceeded` whose `observed` names the closed root.
  _Why:_ each state breaks a promise the page API makes elsewhere: a service worker hides requests from the policy and from routes, a restore skips the load events waits rely on, a windowed list hides rows from reads, and a closed root hides a target a person sees (ADR 0121).
- **Navigation failure cases** (pitfall H01; [ADR 0085](docs/adr/0085-collies-page-api-keeps-playwrights-names-strictness-waits-values-cross-as-json-and-unanswered-requests-fall-through.md)) run in the page suite on Collie's executable against the Proving ground: a `goto` to a name that does not resolve raises `navigation_failed` carrying `net::ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED`, retryable, and one to a port that refuses connections carries `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` instead; a `goto` superseded by another before its document committed, while the Proving ground holds its answer, raises `navigation_interrupted`, and the other answers its response. The same suite runs the Fetch edge and Response decoding cases' Collie lines (ADRs 0118 and 0119, below): a redirect loop raises `too_many_redirects` with the first URL, the 19 hops the pinned Chromium follows and the browser's count of 20; a document cut by a length mismatch, a chunked body without its last chunk or a gzip body cut before its `Content-Length` raises `response_truncated` for a `load` wait and answers its response at `commit`; a plain body labelled gzip and an invalid gzip header raise `navigation_failed` carrying `net::ERR_CONTENT_DECODING_FAILED` while the unknown coding `utf-8` passes through; a 302 whose `Retry-After` asks for an hour, far past `timeout.navigation`, is followed at once; a `goto` to the files family's PDF emits `page.download`, answers no response and leaves the page on its document; and a Shift_JIS and a UTF-8 page's responses name their decoded encoding. The fetch edge and content-coding hosts are reached over plain HTTP/1.1 until the browser trusts the Proving ground's authority through the TLS relay (ADR 0042), which the HTTP/2 stream reset needs.
  _Why:_ a DNS failure, a site's failure and a navigation the program itself replaced need different answers, and drivers that report all three as one timeout or one generic error leave the program guessing (pitfall H01).
- **Popup cases** (pitfall B15; [ADR 0085](docs/adr/0085-collies-page-api-keeps-playwrights-names-strictness-waits-values-cross-as-json-and-unanswered-requests-fall-through.md)) run in the page suite on Collie's executable against the Proving ground's popup page: a listener armed for `popup` before the click that opens one catches the popup, a page of the opener's Session that the Session's `page` event announces too; one armed after the popup opened gets none, and `session.pages` lists the opener and the popup in opening order; the popup navigates as any page, and `page.close` closes it, emitting `close`, a second close changing nothing and a later call raising `closed`. In the page crate's tests, a popup of a handler's page takes its opener's navigation gate and lease check through the Recording gate (ADR 0125).
- **App-state wait cases** (pitfalls B02, B03 and B04; [ADR 0080](docs/adr/0080-sdk-timeouts-nest-waits-arm-when-called-and-a-program-sets-limits-but-never-raises-them.md)) run in the page suite (`waits`) on Collie's executable against the Proving ground, the engine's lines of the Time conformance cases: a `waitForURL` armed before the single-page app's `pushState` link, a `replaceState` and a hash change passes for each, and waits while the URL does not match; `networkidle` comes on the live board, whose WebSocket and server-sent-event stream stay open, once its periodic requests stopped, and on the late board only once its one request, which starts at `load` and is held past the 500 ms of quiet, finished; a `waitForResponse` and a `waitForRequest` sent before the click whose effect posts a request catch it; `locator.waitFor` (`visible`, `detached`) and `locator.waitForCount` resolve on the actionability pages that show, replace and de-duplicate their target, and `waitForFunction` answers its first truthy value; and every wait on the board that never idles ends at a short `timeout.wait` with `deadline_exceeded` naming what it waited for. On the service worker page, a `waitForResponse` armed before the navigation gets the cached call with `fromServiceWorker()` true, and the page reaches `networkidle`, which the worker's own requests never hold back.
  _Why:_ a waiter armed after the action that opens a popup misses it, and a new tab a program never sees leaks its work (pitfall B15).
- **Clean-up conformance cases** ([ADR 0047](docs/adr/0047-filter-lists-block-ads-and-trackers-by-default-from-clean-up-rules-each-run-pins.md), pitfall B08) run in the page suite (`clean_up_blocking`, `clean_up_blocking_off`, `clean_up_hiding`) on Collie's executable with Chromium against the Proving ground's `clean-up.test` pages, judged by the requests the Proving ground received: with nothing set, the ads page's own style sheet, script and photo load while its EasyList ad, its EasyPrivacy tracker and its Fanboy Annoyance script never arrive, each recorded in the Run trace as `page.request_failed` with `net::ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT`, and a `goto` to a document a Filter list blocks raises `navigation_failed` carrying it; with `filter_lists.ads`, `.trackers` and `.annoyances` off, all three load, and with `filter_lists.first_party_scripts` on, the site's own tracker and annoyance scripts load while its ad stays blocked; the challenge page's Akamai and HUMAN sensors, which EasyPrivacy blocks, and Cloudflare's challenge script and logo load under `browser.block_resources: ["image"]` while its tracker stays blocked; the stand-in page's call into Chartbeat, which a redirect rule answers with its stand-in, works; a route that sends a request on as a tracker's URL gets it blocked; and `browser.block_resources: ["image"]` blocks every image of the Session's first and later navigations, its script loading, warns once in the Run report (`block_resources_visible`), and `["video"]` is refused with `setting_invalid`. A page with no Block cause that fails its Page check while the lists are on is loaded once more with them off (ADR 0047): the page whose text its own EasyList-listed script renders passes that load, which answers its `goto`, and blocking is learned off for its domain, so the Run's other Session then loads the ads page's ad, tracker and annoyance; the ads page under a Page check it can never pass is loaded twice and still raises `page_check_failed`, blocking not learned off; and a `403` challenge whose EasyPrivacy-listed check script the lists blocked is loaded once more with them off, clears, and is learned off; Provenance's `blocking_off` entries name each load, its reason and whether blocking was learned off, and each later page loaded with blocking learned off. In process, on the embedded lists, every never-block entry's example loads over every list and every blocked kind, and the lists alone block the challenge vendors' requests; loading the embedded lists grows the process's own resident memory by less than the 9.06 MB of ADR 0057. Firefox and WebKit, cross-origin iframes and shadow DOM, hidden text, the hiding sheet, Consent prompts, paywall entries and the Wall allowance join with their tickets (ADRs 0047, 0048, 0052). The consent wall cases (ticket #718, [ADR 0048](docs/adr/0048-consent-prompts-are-refused-by-default-and-a-consent-wall-is-a-block-cause.md)) run on `consent.test`'s `/notice`, `/wall` and `/pay-or-accept`: the notice is hidden, while each wall raises `blocked` with `consent_required` from its `goto` or the page's next call, answered by nobody, `consent.handling: false` included; with `consent.domains` listing the domain in user configuration (the case writes the home's `configuration.toml` and puts back what was there), the wall is accepted by a trusted click and the Run trace's Provenance records the acceptance with its domain and approval; a run widening `consent.domains` is `setting_locked`, and `consent.on_wall: ask` is `capability_unavailable` until the Human pause takes an answer.
  _Why:_ a blocker that blocks a challenge's sensor fails the challenge it meant to spare, and ads and trackers cost bytes, proxy volume and noise in screenshots and AI input (ADR 0047).
- **Proxy cases** (pitfall H12; [ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-standard-access-by-default-and-escalation-by-new-session.md), [ADR 0053](docs/adr/0053-politeness-is-shared-by-every-run-of-a-user-and-across-machines-through-a-store.md) and [ADR 0092](docs/adr/0092-sessions-open-from-their-source-page-pause-waits-for-a-person-and-a-secret-is-a-placeholder-bound-to-domains.md); ADR 0037 / SA-22) run in the page suite (`sessions.proxy`, the proxies found through the Proving ground's `GET /network-fixtures`) and through `goodfetch-protocol`'s types, on Collie's executable against the Proving ground's forward proxies, judged by its connection record: a proxy that refuses the tunnel to an `https://` page and one that is down raise `proxy_unavailable` naming the proxy, retryable, never `navigation_failed`; a 407 for no credentials or a wrong password raises `proxy_auth_failed`; the Run's default Session and a Session with its own `proxy.*` reach the site each through its own proxy; a proxy on a page, in a page's settings or on a request is refused; on Chromium a SOCKS5 proxy's credentials (`socks5` and `socks5h`) reach the proxy through the Session's loopback relay, and the password is in no browser argument, no line of the engine's stderr and no file of the Goodfetch home; `socks4` and credentials in `proxy.server`'s URL fail with `setting_invalid` naming `proxy.username` and `proxy.password`; and a Client acting for a model setting `proxy.*` on a Run or a Session gets `setting_locked`. The proxy-or-site rule is property-tested in process.
  _Why:_ a failing proxy reported as the site's navigation failure sends a program retrying the wrong thing (pitfall H12), and a proxy that changes under the same cookies, or a password a model could point at its own server, defeats SA-22.
- **Politeness conformance cases** ([ADR 0086](docs/adr/0086-a-crawl-obeys-robots-txt-and-sends-one-request-at-a-time-per-domain-by-default-and-pressing-harder-is-recorded.md)) run against the engine on Proving ground hosts, with short durations set: with nothing set, a crawl keeps one request in flight per domain and the delay between starts, across two runs sharing the key and counting a handler's own `goto`; it obeys a robots.txt Disallow for the `goodfetch-foxhound` group, written in any case (`GoodFetch-Foxhound` applies, as RFC 9309 matches the token case-insensitively), for `site_preferences.robots_txt_user_agent`'s group merged with it, and for `*`, start URLs included; it does not obey a group that names only `Foxhound`, which a substring match, as CPython's `urllib.robotparser` does, would wrongly apply; it reads a robots.txt answered with 404 as no rules, while one answered with 429 or 500 backs off the domain, is never cached as no rules, and with no earlier copy leaves its waiting requests skipped with `robots_txt` after their retries; it waits out a Crawl-delay of 5 s across the domain key, two runs crawling it at once spaced by it (judged on the Proving ground's arrival times), and skips a host asking for more than `site_preferences.max_crawl_delay`, set to 3 s, with `crawl_delay`, unsent; a document that waits after the robots.txt its attempt fetched for a delay longer than the attempt's lease keeps the lease through the wait and is handled in its first attempt; the folding of the Crawl-delay into the key's delay (never below `politeness.domain.delay`, never above the bound) is a pure function of "now", property-tested in-process; it backs off on the site's 429, 502, 503, 504, a refused or timed-out connection and an HTTP/2 `ENHANCE_YOUR_CALM`, on the `http` and `browser` tiers alike and whatever the handler made of the page, but not on a 500 for a page, and holds the domain until the time the site's `Retry-After` asks, a request admitted meanwhile ending `failed` with `response_unavailable` unsent (Foxhound's `backoff_classes` cases, read from the Politeness file); through a forward proxy, the site's 429 backs its domain off and lowers no proxy ceiling, while a proxy's own 429 or 502 backs no domain off, the proxy-or-site rule being property-tested in process over the HTTP tiers' answers; it never gates a Collie program's own `page.goto` on robots.txt or counts it in a domain's Politeness; a crawl's Run that switches hedges or `fetch.tier_race` on at a concurrency of 1 is refused with `setting_not_applicable`; each value that presses a site harder (concurrency above 1, a shorter delay, backoff off or its waits shortened, robots.txt off, sharing other than `shared`, an operator token) appears once in the Audit log with its layer, in the Run report and as one warning in the host's log, and a Record fetched against robots.txt carries the Provenance mark; and in every SDK a crawl-only key set on a Collie Run on a Foxhound engine fails with `setting_not_applicable`. The robots.txt cache rule (a copy used for 24 hours, the last good copy kept applying while robots.txt fails) is a pure function of "now", tested in-process.
- **Crawl Operator policy cases** (spec #581's story 314; ADRs 0028 and 0086): for each registered key of `crawl.*`, `fetch.*`, `politeness.*` and `site_preferences.*`, a case generated from the catalog checks in process that an Operator policy's lock sets it and refuses another value from user and project configuration, a Recipe and the run (from code and from a CLI flag) with `setting_locked` naming the lock, and that its cap on each count and duration bounds the default, takes a value at the cap and refuses one past it, or a clear to no bound, the same way; on Foxhound's executable, a lock on `site_preferences.respect_robots_txt` refuses a crawl turning it off, a cap on `crawl.max_requests` refuses a crawl asking for more or for no bound, and `config explain` names the policy as the layer.
- **Pacing cases** (spec #582's Settings) run a crawl on the Proving ground's `pacing` host, whose latency rises with its time step, and are judged on its arrival times: with `politeness.domain.adaptive_delay` on, the gaps between pages lengthen as the host slows, never below `politeness.domain.delay` and never far past `politeness.backoff.max` (a 2 s delay, long enough for its tenth to cover a loaded host's transport); two runs sharing the domain key, each in an engine of its own, under a `politeness.domain.rate` of 30 a minute arrive no closer than 2 s apart together, within the tenth a start's send may come before the site sees it (an interval long enough for that tenth to cover a loaded host's transport). The weighted mean of the latency and the token bucket (GCRA) are pure functions of "now", property-tested in-process; two engines' runs on one Politeness file are held to 30 requests a minute over a minute of the paused `Clock`, a send that waited for the file counts from the moment the file recorded it, and a crawl request's send waits for its delay after its key's last send and, rated, for a token of its key's sends.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenario "Crawl two page types into a Dataset", and the unsafe defaults competitors shipped (ADR 0086).
- **The navigation gate** ([ADR 0087](docs/adr/0087-each-product-is-an-executable-crate-that-links-only-its-crates-and-has-its-own-size-budget.md)) is tested in the same cases: a handler's click that navigates, a redirect by the page's own JavaScript, a fetch through its page's `request`, an AI verb that navigates and a navigation in a popup the handler's page opens are counted and gated like its `goto`, each held until its response arrives (a browser-tier page until its load event); on the Proving ground's arrival times, a handler's navigations of one domain come the delay apart, the first included when it is the Session's first request through its egress relay (whose delay counts from the send after the relay's `407`), and two pages going at once to a host whose robots.txt answers slowly come no sooner than its Crawl-delay after the robots.txt request, and that delay apart; the load of the page handed to the handler takes no second permit; a page a program opens is never counted in per-domain Politeness; and on a Collie engine a key only Foxhound honours fails with `not_in_product`.
  _Why:_ a handler's own navigation is how a crawl would press a site past its limits unnoticed (ADR 0086).
- **robots.txt reading cases** ([ADR 0086](docs/adr/0086-a-crawl-obeys-robots-txt-and-sends-one-request-at-a-time-per-domain-by-default-and-pressing-harder-is-recorded.md), [ADR 0098](docs/adr/0098-both-products-share-one-goodfetch-home-and-one-daemon-per-major-run-by-the-fullest-product-installed.md)) run in the page suite (`robots_txt`) on Collie's executable against the `access-policy` family's first host, whose robots.txt disallows `/private/` in its `*` group and asks for a Crawl-delay of 2 s: a program's `goto` to a disallowed page loads with its `200`, and the Run trace's Provenance records it not allowed, naming the `*` group and the rule `Disallow: /private/`, and an allowed page allowed; two tabs of one Run each load a page of the site, the second arriving less than the Crawl-delay after the first; each of two Runs sends the site one robots.txt request however many pages it loads there, and no file of the Goodfetch home outside the Runs' folders holds the file, where Foxhound's shared copy would be. The parser (`goodfetch-engine`'s `robots_txt`) is tested in-process with RFC 9309's examples as committed fixtures and `proptest` over generated rules, and fuzzed (`robots_txt`).
  _Why:_ a program's own navigation is not a crawl, so robots.txt must never gate it, yet what a program read must stay attributable to what the site said (SC-01).
- **A handler's page past its work item's lease sends nothing more** (ADRs 0003, 0087): once the lease ends, a command on that page or a popup it opened fails before reaching the browser, raising the lease check's error (Foxhound's `attempt_ended`) and moving the page to `Closing`, so its later calls raise `closed`, and a raw CDP attach bound to it ends with `attach_ended`; a page a program opens is fenced by the run epoch alone.
  _Why:_ the lease check reaches Collie's page only through the value Foxhound sets, so a missing value would drop the fence silently.
- **Proxy provider cases** ([ADR 0021](docs/adr/0021-standard-access-by-default-and-escalation-by-new-session.md), [ADR 0101](docs/adr/0101-a-model-is-named-provider-slash-model-its-capabilities-and-prices-are-declared-and-every-ai-result-carries-its-priced-usage.md) and [ADR 0129](docs/adr/0129-what-every-implementation-of-a-contract-shares-lives-once-in-a-driver-above-them-and-each-implementation-fills-only-narrow-primitives.md)) run in process against the Proxy-provider driver with a fake provider, then on Collie's executable against the Proving ground's forward proxies, judged by its connection record: the driver refuses a `proxy.kind` or `proxy.country` the provider lacks, keeps the provider's longest sticky lifetime unless `proxy.sticky_lifetime` asks for less, has the provider build the Session's sticky username, warns `proxy_exit_may_change` past the lifetime, refuses an unpriced provider under a currency Limit and charges a price rounded up once; through the engine, each Session of a `list` provider leaves from one of its servers for its life and the next Session of the kind from the next server, a kind or country the list lacks, `proxy.server` beside `proxy.provider` and an unknown provider are `setting_invalid`, the bytes `run.usage` counts for a kind are the bytes the Proving ground's proxies of that kind carried, priced at the configured price, a currency Limit stops the Run with `limit_reached` on the call that met it, and an unpriced provider under one is refused naming its price. The `bright_data`, `oxylabs` and `apify` adapters each build their provider's sticky username and lifetime, in process from a fixed Session ID and through the engine against the Proving ground's residential proxy, which reads each provider's format: every Session's requests carry one sticky session and leave from one exit, each Session its own, and a Session that outlives a `proxy.sticky_lifetime` it set is the one `proxy_exit_may_change` names. Each adapter's translation point (ADR 0127) has one named case per failure it knows (`quota_exhausted`, `sticky_session_expired`, `bad_credentials`) and one for an unknown failure, whose text is in neither the failure's `Debug` nor the `proxy_provider_unavailable` it becomes; the relay keeps the proxy's answer to the latest `CONNECT` it refused, and clears it once a tunnel is taken.
  _Why:_ a proxy whose bytes go uncounted escapes the Limit meant to cap proxy spend (pain 13), and a provider that picks a new exit under the same cookies is itself a bot signal (ADR 0021).
- **Session ownership and proxy limit conformance cases** ([ADR 0088](docs/adr/0088-collie-carries-every-capability-except-those-only-many-pages-need.md)) run against the engine on Proving ground hosts. A 403, a 429 and a Challenge met by a Session the program or an Agent opened, with `stealth` allowed, never retire it or open another Session, on a Saved identity, a Profile directory used in place and a Borrowed browser alike. The Challenge goes to the Challenge solver when `solve_challenges` is on, then to a Human pause when a human channel is configured, otherwise to `challenge_unsolved`; the 403 and the 429 are reported on the call that met them, with no Human pause; and in every SDK the program's Session and page stay usable afterwards. Once the Challenge solver has cleared a Challenge on a domain, the program's next Challenge there starts at the solver, with no `stealth` Session opened. The same 403, 429 and Challenge on a Session of a crawl's Session pool retire it and open its replacement, or, on a Borrowed browser, raise a Human pause. A Challenge a pool Session meets on a tier short of the browser (`/corpus/interstitial` answered over HTTP, or one the program's code names on a page served over HTTP, as an after-response Hook's `block(cause)` will) moves its request to the browser in the same attempt and Session, and its domain's next request starts there; under `fetch.tier: http` forced it ends `blocked` with its cause and the browser never loads it; and a Challenge met on the browser ends `blocked`, since no Challenge ladder runs for a pool Session yet (`escalation`, Foxhound's spec #600). Every Session a crawl's pools open, a Label's own pool's and a replacement included, leaves from a proxy of the Run's `proxy.provider` and `proxy.kind`, and a Label setting a `proxy.*` key fails the crawl's start with `setting_not_applicable` (ADRs 0093 and 0117). A 429 from a Collie program's proxy itself is a proxy error, never a Block cause, and lowers that proxy's ceiling for the user's other runs; two Collie runs whose `politeness.proxy.concurrency` is 1 take turns through that proxy instead of failing; and in every SDK a Collie Run accepts `politeness.proxy.concurrency`, `politeness.proxy.rate` and `politeness.sharing`.
  _Why:_ a replaced Session would lose the login of the golden scenario "Log in once with a person, reuse the login headless" and show the site a different browser (its failure point 6), and an Agent's page in "Reach a goal from one instruction, with limits and a verdict" would lose its state; and runs sharing a proxy plan would fail on the vendor's refusals instead of taking turns (ADR 0088).
- **Block detection corpus cases** ([ADR 0035](docs/adr/0035-stealth-is-a-settable-catalog-led-by-a-goodfetch-built-chromium.md)) classify, in process through `block_detection`'s `classify`, every page captured from the Proving ground's block-detection corpus and challenge pages (`fixtures/` beside its tests, each with its `.source.toml`) to the Block cause its corpus records, by the rule written for it (one captured page per rule, with the status and headers it was served with), and the corpus's pages a rule must not take for a block (a vendor's script or a captcha widget on an ordinary page, an article, a 404, a challenge's or a refusal's words served as content) to none; each rule's evidence names it, its source and its license, and the outside sources the rules name are exactly the inputs of `block_detection`'s `third-party.toml`, which `xtask notices` lists. The same pages are then met by a Session in the engine.

- **Trap link cases** ([ADR 0035](docs/adr/0035-stealth-is-a-settable-catalog-led-by-a-goodfetch-built-chromium.md); spec #600) run a crawl on Foxhound's executable against the Proving ground's `traps.test` honeypot pages (`/hidden-links`, `/cards` and every page's four honeypots), robots.txt not obeyed where the honeypots' requests are what is checked, so nothing else keeps them away (`tests/it/trap_links.rs`). On the browser tier a handler's `enqueueLinks` answers each link a person cannot see `not_added` with `trap_link`, and the Proving ground receives no request for one: no box, off the page, `visibility`, `opacity: 0`, `aria-hidden` (trimmed, any ASCII case), a `clip` or `clip-path`, a cut overflow (`hidden` or `clip`) on its chain of containing blocks, and an invisible `nofollow` link anywhere, a menu included. A link a person reaches is added and fetched: one a scroll container holds, one below the first screen when the page's overflow goes to the viewport, a fixed or absolutely positioned link out of a holder that cuts its overflow, a `display: contents` link, a visible `nofollow` link, and one in an element a cosmetic rule hides, left out of the judgement. A link hidden in a menu, a tab or a disclosure a person opens is added and fetched, marked `trap_link_suspected` with `revealable` in Provenance. On the HTTP tier every link is added, and each one its markup hides is marked, `revealable` when a menu, tab or disclosure holds it; a request marked otherwise ends `blocked` with `decoy` on the browser tier before anything is sent, unless a link a person sees reached it too, or it is a sample's browser run; a page marked `noindex` (header or `meta`, a value naming one crawler first left out) that fails its Page check ends `decoy`, never `page_check_failed`. Marks and their Provenance commit with their attempt, so a discarded run writes none. The visibility rules, the markup reading and the marks' clearing are tested in process too (frontier `trap_links`, fetch-tier `document`, page `noindex`); the page's own reading of a layout runs only in the browser cases, since it needs one.
  _Why:_ a rule that fires on a vendor's mere presence would turn a site owner's ordinary pages into blocks, and a page no rule decides must stay a page, never `Unrecognized` (ADR 0127).
- **Lending conformance cases** ([ADR 0089](docs/adr/0089-a-page-is-lent-through-an-endpoint-that-shows-only-its-session-and-collie-joins-other-crawlers-by-borrowing.md)) run each supported library (Playwright in TypeScript and Python, Puppeteer, Browser Use) through its helper against the binary of the same commit: at its newest pinned release in the Pre-merge suite when the SDK or the endpoint is touched, and at both ends of its supported range in the Release suite. They check that the library sees only the lent page, the pages it creates and the targets opened from either, never another Session or a page the program opens during the attach; a second lend on the same Session is refused with `session_reserved`; `Browser.close` ends only the attach and the browser lives; a new browser context, an extension load, ignoring certificate errors and a browser-level command the table does not class are refused with `cdp_command_refused`, and a file read by path on a Daemon with accounts; a download stays the Run's; device emulation and permissions are back after the attach ends; the lease's end raises `attach_ended` in the helper and a reconnect with the old token is refused; in a crawl handler, a library's navigation on the lent page and on a page it creates passes ADR 0087's navigation gate once, and a navigation robots.txt disallows is refused; and lending a Firefox or WebKit page raises `capability_unavailable`.
  _Why:_ they are the failures the driver interop research measured on a page another program owns (ADR 0089).
- **Borrowed page turn-taking conformance cases** ([ADR 0114](docs/adr/0114-a-program-and-collie-take-turns-on-a-page-other-code-drives-and-an-ai-verb-fails-when-a-navigation-it-did-not-start-replaces-its-document.md)) run in every SDK against the binary of the same commit on Proving ground pages, on a page borrowed from Playwright through `run.borrow()` (TypeScript and Python) and on a shared `cdp_url` (Rust), on a Lent page while Playwright is attached, and on a page with an open CDP session. While an `extract`, an `observe` and an `act` each run, compiled and replayed from the Plan, the program's own driver navigates the page to another document: each call raises `page_navigated_during_call`, a retryable `PageError` whose details carry both addresses, and no Plan entry is written, no Repair runs, and the `act` sent no input. An `act` whose own click navigates returns normally; a `waitForURL` and an `expect(page).toHaveURL()` armed before the program's driver navigates pass; a plain locator click during the program's navigation acts on the new document; an Agent's task records the navigation as a step's outcome and goes on, a task replayed from the Plan hands over to the live loop there, and under `plan.frozen` it raises `page_navigated_during_call`; a Lent page's library and a CDP session's commands are passed at once while a Collie call runs, never queued behind it; on a page only the engine drives, a page script's own redirect during an `extract` does not raise `page_navigated_during_call`.
  _Why:_ it is failure point 3 of the golden scenario "Add an AI step to an existing Playwright program", which no leader reports (ADR 0114).
- **The crawler guides' examples** ([ADR 0089](docs/adr/0089-a-page-is-lent-through-an-endpoint-that-shows-only-its-session-and-collie-joins-other-crawlers-by-borrowing.md)) (Crawlee for JavaScript and Python, Scrapy with scrapy-playwright, on Chromium) run as [docs code samples](#docs-code-samples) at pinned crawler versions, each borrowing pages from two browser contexts in one Run and checking one Session per context.
- **Catalog mapping conformance cases** ([ADR 0099](docs/adr/0099-every-surface-spells-each-catalog-name-by-its-own-rule-shapes-come-from-declared-kinds-and-call-is-the-way-down.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine: `call` on the connection sends the wire's snake_case params unchanged and returns the result unchanged, `call` on a value fills its own parameter and refuses another namespace's operation with a `ProgramError` before sending, a closed value's `call` fails with `closed`, its timeout and cancellation apply, an unknown Error code arrives as its group's class, and nothing is retried; a Collie program's `call` to a Foxhound operation on a Foxhound engine succeeds; each SDK declares its own form in `hello`, shows the message the engine rendered, and keeps an error's `operation` field canonical; a deprecated name raises its runtime warning once per process, and pyright strict, mypy with `--enable-error-code deprecated`, oxlint's `typescript/no-deprecated` and rustc each flag a use of it in a type test, except a Python plain-data field or settings `TypedDict` key, which PEP 702 cannot mark; in Rust, a Foxhound Error code read from the wire equals Foxhound's constant with `==` and in a `match` in a Foxhound program, an unknown code matches no constant and keeps its string, and `details::<D>()` returns a code's typed details; after a fixture catalog moves a code from Foxhound to Collie, a Foxhound program matching the code's old constant still compiles, with a deprecation warning, and matches the code, and the catalog marks the code as Collie's; a function typed for `Connection` accepts both Products' connections.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the way down (G8) and of the drift between ports the research found, and they show that every language names what the catalog names (ADR 0099).
- **The engine's name renderers** are tested once per form (`typescript`, `python`, `rust`, `cli`, `tool`, `catalog`) in the engine's own tests: a property test over every catalog entry checks that each renderer spells an operation, parameter, setting key and event exactly as `xtask codegen` emits it for that form, since both use one naming crate (ADR 0099).
- **AI verb conformance cases** ([ADR 0091](docs/adr/0091-collies-ai-verbs-live-on-the-page-replay-from-a-plan-file-the-run-names-and-expect-runs-a-success-check.md)) run in every SDK.
