Against the Scripted engine: each code raises its class under its group with its details; `StepResult` carries `output`, `step` and `usage`, and an unknown step source is kept; `observe` with no match returns an empty list; a placeholder nothing fills, an unused variable or a name that is both a variable and a bound Secret placeholder fails with `variables_mismatch` before any request; the Plan file is read and written on the program's side, as with a Daemon. Against the binary of the same commit with the testing kit's Scripted model: a step the Plan holds replays with no model call and says `plan`; a missing step compiles once and says `model`, and two calls for the same entry at once compile it once; a drifted step is repaired once and says `repair` with its drift, while a Drift after that Repair fails with `plan_drift`; under `plan.frozen` no model is called and nothing is written, a missing step fails with `plan_step_missing`, and `extract` with `mode: "model"` fails with `setting_not_applicable`; a new Plan version is written only after a `verified` Run, kept pending otherwise, and written once accepted by the next Run that names the file; two Runs that change different entries of one file both keep theirs, and an entry both changed is kept pending, never overwritten; a variable's value never appears in a request the Scripted model recorded, in the Plan file or in a log; `expect` passes when its condition holds and fails with `success_check_failed` once `timeout.wait` passes, a Run whose last `act` or `observe` call no passing Success check follows is `needs-review`, and a Success check evaluated before an `act` does not make it `verified`; a required field not on the page fails with `extraction_incomplete` carrying the fields found, and a model that never answers validly with `extraction_invalid`; an `act` whose Effect was sent before a failure raises `effect_uncertain` and is never re-sent; an `ai` aria snapshot past `observation.max_size` collapses and says so; `getByRef` on a removed element fails with `target_not_found` at once; with `site_preferences.respect_use_signals` on, a compile on a page whose AI-use signal refuses the declared Purpose fails with `site_preferences_refused` (`use_signals`) before any model request, while a replayed step runs. In Rust, typed details are read through `Error::details()`, and Python raises no `AssertionError`.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Extract a typed object with a chosen model", "Repeat AI steps with no model call", "Extract with AI inside a crawl, learning once per page type", "Add an AI step to an existing Playwright program" and "Reach a goal from one instruction, with limits and a verdict", and the server-only caches, false successes and token-heavy observations competitors shipped (ADR 0091).
- **Value transform conformance cases** ([ADR 0123](docs/adr/0123-a-typed-value-read-from-page-text-comes-from-an-engine-transform-under-a-stated-locale-and-time-zone.md)) run against the binary of the same commit with the testing kit's Scripted model, on the Proving ground's geo and language variants pages, which serve the same values in several locales. Each transform has its own engine tests over CLDR's data for every locale it ships. "1.099,98 €" under `de-DE` and "1,099.98" under `en-US` both read 1099.98, "1.099,98 €" also reads the currency `EUR` from the same element, "Price: 1.099,98 € incl. VAT" reads 1099.98 through a `match` and then a `number` while a `number` alone does not parse it, and `€` under `de-DE` reads the currency `EUR` while `$` reads `USD` under `en-US` and `CAD` under `en-CA`; a value of a string field reads as the text `"1099.98"`, and `12345678901234567890.12` and `0.1` keep every digit, never passing through a float; a grouping separator out of place, such as `2,109,998` under `de-DE`, does not parse, while a plain space, a no-break space or a narrow no-break space each match a locale's space grouping. `13/12/10` under `en-US` does not parse and is never reordered, and a two-digit year resolves within 80 years before and 20 after the fetch time. A replayed transform whose text no longer parses is Drift, repaired once and then `plan_drift` naming the field, the text and the transform, and under `plan.frozen` it fails at once; in model mode the failure is fed back and ends in `extraction_invalid` naming the text. A Scripted answer naming text absent from the page fails Grounding, and so does, with `extract.locale` unset, one choosing a locale whose language differs from the element's `lang`; with `extract.locale` set, a transform under any other locale fails Grounding, and an unknown tag fails with `setting_invalid` naming the closest one; `scriptedExtraction` with a typed date answers with the page's "01.02.2026" and its transform. A page that serves "1,099" to an `en-US` Session and "1.099" to a `de-DE` one compiles a variant for each, and never replays one Session's parse for the other, and `/en/` and `/de/` pages of one layout each compile once and then both replay, under `plan.frozen` too; changing `extract.locale` or `extract.timezone_id` between two Runs compiles a new variant too, and under `plan.frozen` fails with `plan_drift` naming the changed input; a replay that finds a field's element under another `lang` while the document's language is unchanged is Drift, repaired once for a call and handed to the live loop for an Agent's task, whose output field whose transform does not parse is given back once and then ends `needs-review` naming it. A compiled `map` reads "Three" as 3 from its table, and a text that is not one of its keys does not parse; an entry whose `map` table is new is kept pending after a `verified` Run and reaches the Plan file only once a person accepts it; in model mode or an Agent's output, an answer naming `map` fails Grounding. A `<time datetime>` and a `<data value>` read the same whatever the locale. A date-time with no offset takes `extract.timezone_id` when set, and otherwise the Session's `browser.timezone_id`, never the zone of the engine's machine, which the case sets to a third zone; one with an offset keeps it. "3 hours ago" replayed from a Response recording reads the recorded fetch time minus three hours on every run. The Run trace holds, for each field, the text as read and the transform with its resolved locale, zone, pattern and anchor, and the output is the schema's type with no wrapper.
  _Why:_ they are pitfall D05 of the pitfall catalog, numbers and dates misread under the wrong locale, zone or clock, which Babel, price-parser and dateparser leave to a hint or a guess (ADR 0123).
- **Crawl conformance cases** ([ADR 0093](docs/adr/0093-a-crawl-declares-its-labels-and-one-handler-each-and-a-handler-reads-collies-page-on-every-fetch-tier.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine, and against the engine on Proving ground hosts where a tier or a site is involved. A second Label of one name, or a second handler, fails at once with `label_duplicate`; a Label left without a handler fails the start with `label_unhandled`; an enqueue to another crawl's Label, and a resume whose pending requests name an undeclared Label, fail with `label_unknown` before any work; a missing or wrong Payload on `enqueue`, `seed` or `addSeed` fails `tsc`, pyright and mypy. `skip()` ends the request `skipped` with the reason `handler` and its message, and `retry()` ends the attempt with `retry_requested`, counted against `crawl.retry.max`, even when the handler swallowed either; every later call on that context fails with `attempt_ended`, and in Python `except Exception:` does not catch it. A handler raising its own exception ends `failed` with `handler_failed`, carrying the exception's type, message and stack, and is not retried unless `crawl.retry.handler_failed` is on, when it is retried `crawl.retry.max` times; a retryable `GoodfetchError` raised inside, such as a navigation timeout, keeps its code and is retried. A handler process killed mid-attempt, or an Attached engine that died with it and is resumed, leaves a `handler_lost` attempt that counts as a retry, while the program's own `close()` or a dropped Rust `serve` future counts none, a Rust handler's panic ends `handler_failed`, and a Hook's retry draws on the same `crawl.retry.max`. Enqueues, seeds and Records an attempt staged are discarded when it fails, retries or escalates. A 5xx or a 408 is retried and then fails `response_unavailable`; a 404 fails at once with `response_not_ok`, unless `crawl.handle_statuses` lists it, when the handler gets it. On the Proving ground's HTTP and JavaScript-rendered pages with `fetch.tier: auto`, one handler reads both: a wait for content the static document lacks, a click, and a page failing its `expectedPage` each escalate the request to the browser, none counting as a retry, and a `page.request` never escalates; under a forced `http` tier the same click fails with `browser_required`. Two `enqueue` calls to one URL with two POST bodies add two requests, and the same body twice is a `duplicate`; an `enqueueLinks` Payload function gives each link its own Payload and skips a link it returns `null` for; a `sitemap(url, rules)` seed, read from robots.txt, follows a nested index, gives each entry the first matching rule's Label, and reports the entries matching none, under `no_rule`, and those outside the scope, under `out_of_scope`, in each sitemap's counts in the Run's report; a robots.txt seed admits its own host's entries from the sitemap it cross-submits on another host and no other host's; and a sitemap's own fetches, nested ones included, run no handler, are never sent to a host the domain policy refuses or a path robots.txt disallows, and reach the site `politeness.domain.delay` apart from its pages (#903); a gzip sitemap and a plain one both seed, while one past sitemaps.org's 50,000 URLs (the `sitemaps` family's index), one past its 50 MB uncompressed, a decompression bomb refused by `network.max_decompression_ratio`, broken XML, a 500 and a 404 are each listed in the seed's `sitemaps` counts with their URL, code, status and why, in the progress and the report, and the other sitemaps still seed (#909). A link outside `crawl.scope.strategy`, or beyond `crawl.max_depth` or `crawl.max_requests`, is answered "not added" with its reason; a seed, an `addSeed` or an enqueue the domain policy refuses is answered "not added" with `domain_policy`, judged after the scope, so an off-site link the policy refuses too stays `out_of_scope` (U15), and before the caps, and the Proving ground receives no request for it, while an allowed one is added; an `egress.*_domains` entry that is no host fails the start with `setting_invalid`; a redirect leaving the scope ends `skipped` with `out_of_scope`, and one to a host the domain policy refuses (spelled in punycode or as the WHATWG parser leaves it), or to an address the address check refuses at that hop on the HTTP tiers, dotted or in hex, ends `skipped` with `domain_policy`, never connecting, each beside an in-scope chain that reaches its handler, live in `run.outcomes({ kinds: ["skipped", "blocked"], after: "now" })` and counted by reason in the Run report; a Hook's rewrite is judged as an enqueue is, by the scope then the domain policy (in process until Hooks exist, #1144); a handler's `goto` that robots.txt disallows, to a host whose Crawl-delay is above `site_preferences.max_crawl_delay`, or, with `site_preferences.respect_use_signals` on, that an AI-use signal refuses for the Run's Purpose, before the request or in its response's `Content-Usage` header, fails with `site_preferences_refused` and its reason (a refusal from the response leaving the page blank), and escaping the handler it ends the request `skipped` with `robots_txt`, `crawl_delay` or `use_signals`, while a Collie program's own `goto` to the same page is not refused, nor a handler's with `site_preferences.respect_robots_txt` off. A Label takes `locator.test_id_attribute` and `session.page_check` without a pool of its own, gets Sessions apart from the Run's with its own `session.source`, and refuses `politeness.*` and `browser.*` with `setting_not_applicable`, as a Collie Run on a Foxhound engine refuses `fetch.*` (a Collie engine answers `not_in_product`). A handler's `goto` over the HTTP tier is fetched over it through the gate; a page's own redirect that robots.txt disallows is aborted and reported as a `requestfailed` event, failing only a waiter armed for it; a handler waiting for a permit on a domain with a long delay is not timed out, and eight handlers going to one domain at once reach it `politeness.domain.delay` apart, judged on the Proving ground's arrival times, none timed out though the last waits longer than its lease (the Run's browser and its egress relay's credentials are started by a first seed's handler, so the eight pages open within the first one's lease, which nothing renews while its handler only holds its page; the lease cases whose handler opens its pages within a 1 s timeout's lease likewise run after a first seed's handler started the Run's browser, and every navigation gate case's home sets the governor's `http` and `browser_page` floors above what it holds at once, since a governor above normal on a loaded host admits nothing past a floor, ADR 0040); and a TypeScript `enqueue` left unawaited after the handler returned fails with `attempt_ended`.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Crawl two page types into a Dataset", "Crawl with HTTP and a browser only where needed" and "Extract with AI inside a crawl, learning once per page type", and the tier rewrites, faked skips and blind retries competitors shipped (ADR 0093).
- **Tier table cases** (spec #582; [ADR 0040](docs/adr/0040-one-resource-governor-per-engine-sized-from-measured-load.md), [ADR 0093](docs/adr/0093-a-crawl-declares-its-labels-and-one-handler-each-and-a-handler-reads-collies-page-on-every-fetch-tier.md)) run against Foxhound's executable on the Proving ground's `tiers.test` and Chrome for Testing. Under `fetch.tier_race`: a static listing the Proving ground holds 3 s is won by browser-fetch, its handler given the `http` page, and the navigation started 500 ms later, its Label having no browser-fetch latency yet, is cancelled; the next request of that Label waits at least its first's latency before its navigation; a script-rendered listing whose Label's Page check expects its entries is won by the navigation, its handler given the browser's page only; in each, the Proving ground receives both requests, Usage counts each navigation sent beside the crawl requests, and Provenance's `tier_race` entry names the delay, how each run ended and the winner, its `fetch_tier` entry `race`. The median that times a navigation is a pure function of a Label on a domain's latest browser-fetch latencies, tested in process, and a race switched on while a domain admits one request at a time is refused with `setting_not_applicable`.
- **Fetch edge conformance cases** ([ADR 0118](docs/adr/0118-every-fetch-tier-follows-redirects-as-the-browser-does-fails-a-cut-body-and-resends-only-what-a-site-never-processed.md)) run against the engine on Proving ground hosts, on the `http` and `browser` tiers alike unless a case names one, with short durations set. Redirects: a redirect loop ends the request `failed` with `too_many_redirects` after 19 hops, at its 20th request, and after 5 with `fetch.redirect.max: 5`, while `fetch.redirect.max: 20` is refused with `setting_invalid`; a site that sets a cookie and redirects back to its first URL reaches the handler; a POST answered with 301 or 302 arrives as a GET with no body, a 303 turns a PUT into a GET, and a 307 or 308 keeps the method and body; `Authorization` set on the first hop is absent once the chain reaches another origin; a 300, a redirect with no `Location` and one to an `ftp:`, `file:`, `gopher:` or `data:` URL fail at once with `response_not_ok`, while a 301 listed in `crawl.handle_statuses` reaches the handler unfollowed; two hops to one domain start at least `politeness.domain.delay` apart, and a hop to a path robots.txt disallows ends the request `skipped` with `robots_txt`; a `Retry-After: 2` on a 302 delays its next hop by 2 s. Refresh stubs: a refresh to another URL, by `<meta http-equiv="refresh">` with a delay of 0 or 5 s or by a `Refresh` header, hands the handler the target document at once on both tiers, with the same Records; a refresh with no URL, to the document's own URL, inside `<noscript>` or with a delay of 200 s is not followed; and a Label whose page redirects by script keeps the browser as its tier. Cut bodies: a body shorter than its `Content-Length`, a chunked body missing its last chunk, an HTTP/2 stream reset after its headers and a gzip body cut before its end each fail the attempt with `response_truncated`, retried and never handed to the handler, and a Collie `goto`, `response.text()` and `page.request` on the same pages raise it; a Response recording keeps such a response with `WARC-Truncated: disconnect` and replays it as `response_truncated`. Resends: on the `http` tier, a GOAWAY whose last stream ID is below an in-flight request, a REFUSED_STREAM, a 421, an `HTTP_1_1_REQUIRED` and a keep-alive connection closed as it is reused each end with the page in one attempt and one Politeness start, with the resend in the Run trace; a resend that fails again ends the attempt with `navigation_failed`; a POST that a handler's `page.request` sends on the `http` tier, through the Session's HTTP transport, is not resent after a reused connection closed; and an `ENHANCE_YOUR_CALM` backs off the domain. `Retry-After`: a 503 with `Retry-After` in seconds, and in each of the three HTTP-date formats against a skewed `Date` header, holds the domain for that time; one past `politeness.retry_after.max`, set to 3 s, sends nothing more to the domain, ends its request `failed` with `response_unavailable` and no retry spent, and ends each request the domain then holds the same way with `status` null and `retryAfter` set, after which `retryFailed()` re-admits them; and a Collie page's 429 raises `blocked` carrying `retryAfter`. Happy Eyeballs: a Proving ground host whose IPv6 address drops packets is reached over IPv4 within a second, with no `navigation_failed`. On the `http` tier (#763), `goodfetch-fetch-tier`'s `tests/it` (`fetch_edge`) runs the cut-body and resend lines through the fetch driver, their HTTP/2 forms over TLS included, and the fetch suite's `fetch.edge.http` cases run the crawl's side over plain HTTP/1.1 (`conformance/README.md`), the redirect and refresh lines included (#762), whose method, `Authorization`, loop-cost and refresh-delay lines also run through the fetch driver in `tests/it` (`chain`), over TLS for the cross-origin hop; the 421 line uses the page `/misdirected`, since the tier never coalesces hosts on a connection. The Response recording and Run trace lines join them with those features.
  _Why:_ they are rows U12, U13, H02, H05, H07, H15 and H24 of the pitfall catalog: loops, refresh stubs, cut bodies stored as pages, drained connections failing requests, ignored or endless `Retry-After`, synchronized backoff and stalls on a broken IPv6 path (ADR 0118).
- **URL identity conformance cases** ([ADR 0120](docs/adr/0120-the-frontier-parses-urls-as-browsers-do-fixes-only-safe-rules-learns-the-rest-on-evidence-and-caps-a-urls-shape.md)) run against the engine on Proving ground hosts, and the key's rules also as a pure function in-process, over a table of URL pairs. The parser and the key: an `href` written with a backslash, a tab, a newline or surrounding spaces is enqueued as a browser resolves it; a relative `enqueue` resolves against the document's base URL, and a relative seed is not added with `invalid_url`, as is an `href` that fails to parse; `http://bücher.example/` and `http://xn--bcher-kva.example/` share one Politeness key, one robots.txt copy and one scope host, and `http://0xc0.0.2.1/` and `http://192.0.2.1/` give one key and one host; the domain policy reads that host too, so a refused name spelled in Unicode, capitals or punycode, and a refused address in hex, decimal, octal or shortened form, is not added with `domain_policy`, while one leading `www.` is ignored by the scope alone: a `www` seed's bare host, which the scope admits, is refused by a policy allowing the `www` host alone; `%7e` and `~` and `%7E` give one key, while `%2F` and `/`, `+` and `%20`, `/APPLE` and `/apple`, and `?a=1&a=2` and `?a=2&a=1` give two; `utm_source`, `gclid`, `fbclid`, an empty query, empty pairs, parameter order and `;JSESSIONID=…` give one key, and the request is fetched at its own URL, with its parameters in their order and its tracking parameters kept. Fragments: `/page#a` and `/page#b` are one request; on a Label with `crawl.keep_url_fragment` on, the Proving ground's hash-routed app gives one request and one Record per route, served by the browser under `fetch.tier: auto`, while forcing `http` on that Label fails the start with `setting_not_applicable`, and a resume that changes the setting raises it too. Learned forms: on a host that redirects `http` to `https`, the bare host to `www.` and `/a` to `/a/`, each form is fetched once and then merged, a pending request it merges ends `skipped` with `duplicate`, and the Run report lists each `equivalent_form` rule with its evidence; on a host where `/a` and `/a/`, or `www.` and the bare host, serve different pages and never redirect, both are fetched and kept, while a scope seeded on the bare host still admits `www.`; a page whose `rel=canonical` names the home page merges nothing, while pages whose canonical drops a parameter that their signatures show has no effect yield a rule whose evidence lists the canonical; a redirect to a URL already admitted for another request ends the request `skipped` with `duplicate`, its handler not run, while a redirect that sets a cookie and returns to the request's own URL is followed and reaches its handler, and a later enqueue of a redirect's final URL is `duplicate`; under `same_origin`, an `http` seed whose site redirects to `https` stays in scope. Caps: a URL of 2,084 characters, a path of 21 segments, `/a/a/a/` and `/a/b/a/b/a/b/` are not added with `max_url_length`, `max_path_segments` and `max_segment_repeats`, while `/a/a/` and `/page/1/tag/1/sort/1` are added, and an over-long link to another host is `out_of_scope` and leaves the Run `complete`; the Proving ground's relative-link loop ends with the crawl `unverified` and its Completeness reason naming `crawl.max_segment_repeats`; each cap set to `null` admits its URL. Facets: on the Proving ground's faceted listing, a robots.txt Disallow on its facet parameters keeps a crawl with nothing set to the product pages, and so does a `crawl.scope.exclude` pattern on them with robots.txt off. Sitemaps: a sitemap seed whose entries name another host leaves those entries not added with `out_of_scope`, counted in its totals; a robots.txt seed whose `Sitemap:` line names a sitemap on another host admits that sitemap's entries on the robots.txt's host and no others; a relative `<loc>` resolves against its sitemap's URL.
  _Why:_ they are the URL identity and crawl-space failure modes the pitfall catalog found unanswered (rows U02, U05, U06, U07, U09, U10 and U20): merges RFC 3986 allows only on evidence, lost hash routes, one host split by its spellings, traps no learned rule closes, and sitemaps that widen the scope (ADR 0120).
- **Crawl-space cases** ([ADR 0005](docs/adr/0005-learned-frontier-rules-and-completeness.md)) run against Foxhound's executable under `fetch.tier: http` on the Proving ground's `learning` family, whose engine learns from each page's signature, and the learned rules' thresholds also in process as a pure function of what a crawl showed the Frontier (`learn` and `admit` over a run file in memory), with a model-based test of the learned parameters' and the trailing slash's merges, their contradictions and the pending requests they end, against a reference model under kills. Each threshold met and one short of it: 3 pairs of URLs differing only by a parameter, matching, teach it has no effect, and a URL that differs from an admitted one only by it is a `duplicate`, on its host alone; an equivalent form is learned on 1 redirect between its two forms, 2 matching pairs or 3 pages whose `rel=canonical` differs from their URL only by it, a canonical to another path or registrable domain teaching nothing, and a request admitted before and still pending that it merges ends `skipped` with `duplicate`, its handler never run; a pair of pages differing only by a parameter or a form whose signatures differ keeps it from being learned, and stops a learned one, which then merges nothing more and is reported with its contradiction; 100 pages under a path pattern in a row that bring no new page signature and no URL outside it close a trap, whose next URL is not added with `trap_path`, the Run ending `unverified` with the count; an offset listing gives way to a cursor its page links, its next offset URL a `duplicate`, and a listing paged only by offset ends the Run `unverified`. The Run report's `learned_rules` lists each rule with its evidence and every URL it merged. `crawl.path_budgets` is tested in process (`admit` over a run file in memory) and on Foxhound's executable against the Proving ground's `facets` family: the first entry whose glob a URL's path and query match counts it, one past its count is not added with `path_budget`, a later entry the URL also matches does not count it, and the Run ends `incomplete` with a reason naming the pattern and the counts; with no bound the faceted listing is crawled whole and `complete` (no default rule, ADR 0120), and a `crawl.scope.exclude` pattern on its parameters or `crawl.max_requests` bounds it. Completeness reasons ([ADRs 0005](docs/adr/0005-learned-frontier-rules-and-completeness.md), [0045](docs/adr/0045-payment-required-is-a-block-cause-and-paid-fetches-are-opt-in.md), [0048](docs/adr/0048-consent-prompts-are-refused-by-default-and-a-consent-wall-is-a-block-cause.md) and [0107](docs/adr/0107-a-runs-model-allowance-refuses-its-model-calls-once-spent-while-the-run-goes-on.md)) run on Foxhound's executable: a crawl with no reason is `complete`; pages left `blocked` with `payment_required` or `consent_required` leave it `unverified`, a bound's cutoff or a stop `incomplete`, and reasons of both kinds `incomplete`, each reason listed with its code, message and `count` (a stop's counting the requests it left), the same when another engine reads the report back; a crawl none of whose seeds reached a handler reports `nothingDone`'s `no_seed_handled` with its seeds counted by why. The Model allowance's reason, which no tier or AI verb raises on the executable yet, is tested in process over a run file.
  _Why:_ they are pitfalls U01, U02 and U03, traps that drain a crawl's budget and offset pagination that loses rows, which no competitor's fixed rules end (ADR 0005).
- **Tier table cases** ([ADR 0093](docs/adr/0093-a-crawl-declares-its-labels-and-one-handler-each-and-a-handler-reads-collies-page-on-every-fetch-tier.md); spec #582's served-call table) run in process, the table being a pure function of the call, the tier and whether it was forced (`goodfetch-fetch-tier`'s `served_calls`): each row on each tier, forced and under `auto`, and every call of the catalog's page entries with a row; and on Foxhound's executable against the Proving ground's `tiers.test` and Chrome for Testing (`tests/it/served_calls.rs`): one handler's waits for a visible heading, for elements its markup hides, for its link, URL and load state, its `formats`, and its `goto`, `reload`, `goBack` and `goForward` are answered on a forced `http` tier as on `browser`, while its `evaluate` fails with `browser_required` there and under `auto` escalates the request once, in the same attempt; a wait for a script-rendered listing's entries escalates under `auto` and fails with `browser_required` under a forced `http`, so the page never ends `succeeded` empty, neither counting a retry (pitfall H22); and the Page formats cases read every Markdown page of `extraction/7` on `http` and on `browser`, each verified by the oracle (ADR 0094). On the light-browser tier, with Lightpanda (`tests/it/light_browser.rs`), each row's call on a script-rendered listing is taken in turn, forced and under `auto` on a Label its samples moved there: `formats`, a wait for the entries its script added, `page.request` and `close` are served from the document Lightpanda's run left, with no layout, while a wait its document does not meet, `waitForResponse`, `evaluate`, a click and `goto` fail with `browser_required` when forced and under `auto` escalate the request once, served in the browser in the same attempt. On `discovered_api` (`tests/it/discovered_api.rs`), under `auto`, a handler that arms `waitForResponse` for its list's first two calls and reloads is answered from the replayed calls on the Proving ground's infinite lists, by page number and by cursor, committing the Records a crawl on `browser` commits at a fraction of its requests, and a list whose call carries a token taken once stays on `browser`; its other network waits and kept responses join with #1659. The layout and gesture rows join with #1091; a Label split by path segment is #926's, and `fetch.tier_race` its own ticket's.
- **Kept responses conformance cases** ([ADR 0110](docs/adr/0110-a-crawl-handler-reads-the-responses-its-labels-settings-kept-from-its-own-navigation.md)) run in every SDK, against the Scripted engine for the shapes and against Foxhound's executable of the same commit on a Proving ground page that loads a JSON call while it loads: with `fetch.kept_responses.urls` naming that call, a handler's `keptResponse` returns it with its body, whether it arrived before the handler started or while it runs, the site receives the page and the call once each, and no reload is needed; `keptResponses` lists those kept so far in arrival order, a predicate filters them in the program, and a call on a popup or a page the handler opened is not kept; a body past `fetch.kept_responses.max_size` raises `body_unavailable` naming that setting; a matcher no kept response meets raises `deadline_exceeded` naming `timeout.wait` and the matcher; on a Label that keeps nothing either call raises `responses_not_kept` at once; under `fetch.tier: auto` a read on `http` or `browser_fetch` escalates the attempt to the browser, under a forced `http` tier the Run's start fails with `setting_not_applicable`, and on `discovered_api` the call is answered from the replayed JSON; the setting on a Collie Run on a Foxhound engine fails with `setting_not_applicable`, and on a Collie engine with `not_in_product`; a call after the attempt ended fails with `attempt_ended`; `runHandler` with `tier: "browser"` and a recording returns the recorded call, on `http` ends the attempt `escalated` naming the call, and under a Label's forced `http` tier fails it with `browser_required`.
  _Why:_ it is the failure point golden scenario 06 found, a page's own JSON missed by a waiter armed too late and fetched twice by the reload every leader needs (ADR 0110).
- **Response decoding conformance cases** ([ADR 0119](docs/adr/0119-every-fetch-tier-decodes-a-crawls-document-as-the-browser-does-a-label-names-the-content-types-its-handler-takes-and-exports-are-utf-8.md)) run in every SDK, against the Scripted engine for the shapes and against Foxhound's executable of the same commit on the Proving ground's encodings family, its PDFs and downloads pages and its content-coding pages, each tier-dependent case run on every fetch tier in turn, forced, and under `fetch.tier: auto` where it says so: every encodings page reads the oracle's text on every tier, with where Provenance says its encoding came from (`bom`, `header`, `meta` or `utf8`), the UTF-16 page with a BOM by its BOM, the charset that contradicts its `<meta>` by its header, `latin1` and `ascii` labels as windows-1252 (0x80 reads as €), a `<meta>` UTF-16 label as UTF-8 and `x-user-defined` as windows-1252, a `<meta>` after the first 1,024 bytes but still among head tags read, and one after a body tag not read, on every tier; an unlabelled valid UTF-8 page reads as UTF-8 in the browser too, where Chrome alone would show mojibake, both as the crawl request's document and through the handler's own `goto`, while `ctx.response.headers()` keeps the site's `Content-Type`; an unlabelled page in a legacy encoding escalates to the browser under `fetch.tier: auto`, recorded as `detected`, and fails with `browser_required` naming `fetch.encoding` and `fetch.tier` under a forced `http` tier, while `fetch.encoding` set on its Label reads it on `http` with `setting` as where it came from; a BOM still beats `fetch.encoding`, and an unknown label is refused with `setting_invalid`; a sample comparison whose browser sample reports another `document.characterSet` keeps that Label on the browser; `ctx.response.text()` equals the text `page.content()` was decoded from, `page.request`'s `text()` honours a Shift_JIS charset and turns bad bytes into U+FFFD without raising, and `json()` reads UTF-8 whatever the charset and ignores a leading BOM; a body labelled gzip that is plain, and one whose gzip header is invalid, fail with `navigation_failed` carrying `net::ERR_CONTENT_DECODING_FAILED` on every tier, retried under `crawl.retry.max`; `gzip, br` decodes in reverse order, raw deflate decodes, an unknown coding such as `utf-8` passes the body through, and an HTML body compressed twice with gzip, or with zstd, but labelled once fails as a decoding failure; with the built-in `crawl.content_types` a PDF, a ZIP and a JSON response end `skipped` with `content_type` and a message naming the type, their bodies unread, never retried and with no Download in the Run; a 404 that `crawl.handle_statuses` hands to the handler meets the same check; with `application/pdf` or `application/*` listed, the handler's `ctx.response.body()` holds the PDF's bytes on every tier, a locator or `content()` on its page fails at once with `response_not_document` without escalating, and the browser tier starts no download, while a handler's click on an `<a download>` link still yields a `Download`; a response with no `Content-Type` is typed by MIME sniffing as Chrome types it; a Collie program's own `goto` to the same PDF still emits `download`; every text Export format of a Dataset holding the Unicode record is UTF-8 with no BOM, JSON and JSONL write `é` as itself rather than `\u00e9`, and CSV starts with a BOM only under `export.csv.bom`.
- **Transport conformance gate** (pitfall H13's acceptance test; spec #579) runs each HTTP transport under the fetch driver against the Proving ground, in `goodfetch-fetch-tier`'s `tests/it`: reqwest and `wreq`, each case on both, each asks for `gzip, deflate, br, zstd`, decodes the content-coding pages labelled `gzip`, `deflate` with its zlib wrapper and raw, `br`, `zstd` and `gzip, br`, passes an unknown coding through, and fails a plain body labelled gzip, an invalid gzip header and a body compressed twice but labelled once with `navigation_failed` carrying `net::ERR_CONTENT_DECODING_FAILED`; it answers a 301 as it came, its `Location` unfollowed, with one request to the site; a page that answers after 8 s ends a 1 s `timeout.first_byte` with `deadline_exceeded` naming it; and a received head holding a NUL, a bare CR or a line without a colon fails with `navigation_failed` carrying `net::ERR_INVALID_HTTP_RESPONSE`, while a folded line is kept. A proxy's answer to the tunnel request is its own transport failure, in process: a refusal, a demand for credentials and a proxy nothing answers on; a certificate no trusted authority issued is `CertificateInvalid` on both; on both, pitfall H03's acceptance test (`tests/it/certificates.rs`; spec #565) ends each of the `certificates` family's invalid certificates with `certificate_invalid` naming its failure, nothing sent, passes it under its domain's exception, and stops a pinned host presenting another key with `certificate_changed` after a handshake the Proving ground's `tls_handshakes` records and before any byte of the request, and for every later hop; on `wreq` the same file shows its trust scopes present one JA4 fingerprint, and on reqwest that each domain's client certificate goes to its own hosts alone, while Foxhound's executable fails such a crawl request after one attempt and marks the Records fetched under the exception in their Provenance (`goodfetch-foxhound`'s `tests/it/certificates.rs`); an unknown failure is the generic case. `wreq` also shows the Proving ground the TLS ClientHello and HTTP/2 settings of the Chrome for Testing build Collie pins, loaded beside it in the same test, but for the extensions and signature algorithms its BoringSSL fork cannot send, named in the test; and its translation point's pin check fails when `Cargo.lock` moves one of the forks it reads. Browser-fetch, the `http` tier of a Session that may move to the browser, runs the same cases on Foxhound's executable with Chrome for Testing (`goodfetch-foxhound`'s `tests/it/browser_fetch.rs`), since it sends from a Session's browser context: the browser asks for its own codings, the same on the `http` tier as on `browser`, and decodes each page as its own navigation does; over plain HTTP, the only scheme on which the browser can reach the Proving ground, Chrome decodes `gzip` and `deflate` alone, so the `br`, `zstd` and `gzip, br` pages fail there with `net::ERR_CONTENT_DECODING_FAILED` as on the browser tier; a `page.request` asking for no redirect gets the 301 with nothing sent to its `Location`; and a 1 s `timeout.first_byte` ends the 8 s page with `deadline_exceeded` naming it.
- **Tier parity of cookies** (pitfall H20's acceptance test, Foxhound's half; spec #579) runs on Foxhound's executable with Chrome for Testing: under `fetch.tier: auto`, a cookie the browser run of a Label's first sample set is sent by the `http` run of that Label's next request, in the same pool Session, and by no request of a Label whose pool is its own; and a Session that may move shows the site one TLS `ClientHello`, the browser's, on its `http` and `browser` runs (the Proving ground's `tls_handshakes`, which records a handshake the browser abandons at the Proving ground's untrusted certificate), while a Session forced onto `http` shows reqwest's.
  _Why:_ they are the [pitfall catalog](https://github.com/goodfetch/goodfetch/blob/prototype/sdk-api/sdk-api-sketch/pitfalls.md)'s rows H16, H17, H25 and D04: mojibake from one encoding signal, a PDF parsed as HTML, a mislabelled `Content-Encoding`, and a tier that reads a page differently from the browser it is compared with.
- **Sessions, Human pause and secrets conformance cases** ([ADR 0092](docs/adr/0092-sessions-open-from-their-source-page-pause-waits-for-a-person-and-a-secret-is-a-placeholder-bound-to-domains.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine: `run.newPage()` twice shares one default Session, reached from each page, and after it is closed `run.newPage()` fails with `closed`; `source` on `newSession` beats `session.source` from the Run, `session.source` in a Session's `settings` fails `tsc`, pyright and mypy, and in Rust is refused with `setting_not_applicable`; `saveIdentity`, the cookie calls and `gf.identities` send their operations and map their results, a cookie's expiry and a Saved identity's times arriving as the language's point-in-time type (`Date`, an aware `datetime` in UTC, `SystemTime`); `page.pause()` sends its reason, `until` and timeout, returns who ended it and how long it waited, throws `human_pause_declined` on a decline, throws `deadline_exceeded` naming `timeout.human_pause`, and sends `$/cancel` when the call is cancelled; the `login` function (a closure in Rust) runs on the program's side when the engine asks; `blocked` arrives as a `PageError` carrying its Block cause, URL and status, an unknown cause kept as received; `page.localStorage` and `sessionStorage` send their operations; a Secret placeholder outside the positions that take one is refused before sending with `page_value_invalid`; `resolveSecret` (Rust: `secret_requests()`) answers a `caller:` request, and none or a throw maps to `secret_unavailable`; each new Error code raises its class; `keyboard.type(secret(…))` fails `tsc`, pyright and mypy, and a literal with no provider where a Secret reference is expected fails `tsc`; in Rust, a placeholder passed to `evaluate` fails to compile (a compile-fail test); cookie and storage operations appear on no CLI command, MCP tool, skill, Framework adapter or Code mode global (a catalog completeness test).
  _Why:_ they are the SDK failure points of the golden scenario "Log in once with a person, reuse the login headless" (ADR 0092).
- **Sessions, Human pause and secrets engine cases** ([ADR 0092](docs/adr/0092-sessions-open-from-their-source-page-pause-waits-for-a-person-and-a-secret-is-a-placeholder-bound-to-domains.md)) run against the engine on Proving ground hosts, with short durations set: a Saved identity kept by `saveIdentity` is reused by a later Session, and `saveIdentity` on a Borrowed browser fails with `session_not_savable`; cookies outside a bound source's `domains` are refused; a pause stops every enclosing timeout, holds the page's later Gestures while reads run, ends when `until` holds or on "Done" (refused while `until` does not hold) or "Hand back", ends for a Live view grant holder only through `until`, ends past `timeout.human_pause`, and leaves Usage counting toward Limits; a page the Page check judges a login page runs the login step, skips the Human pause when `session.recovery.human_pause` is off, and fails with `logged_out` listing each step; on a Session the program opened, a rate limit or IP block fails the `goto()` that met it, or a `click()` that waits for the navigation it started, or else the page's next call after a navigation no call waits for (a script's redirect), with `blocked` naming the cause, and the Session is neither replaced nor closed, while a `page.request` answered with a rate limit returns its response; with `session.recovery.human_pause` off, a Challenge the solver cannot pass fails with `challenge_unsolved` without a Human pause; a Secret placeholder typed by `fill` or sent in a header resolves only when the field's frame or the request is on its bound domains (`secret_domain_not_allowed` otherwise, a payment frame on an allowed page included), an unbound one fails with `secret_unbound`, and a missing value with `secret_unavailable`, as does a `code_source` with no code while the pause is off; a header secret sent by `page.request` is invisible to page scripts and a Service Worker, and a redirect to a domain outside the binding drops it (a header and a resent JSON body) with `secret_domain_not_allowed`; a resolved value, and the Basic `Authorization` and `Proxy-Authorization` headers built from credentials, never reach a `request`, `response`, `console`, `pageerror`, `dialog` or WebSocket event, where `%name%` stands instead (the setting's key, `%session.http_credentials%` or `%proxy.password%`, for those headers), except a one-time code, which is masked where it was typed and in what the engine keeps; a placeholder typed into or sent from a Borrowed browser other code drives, or a page with a raw CDP session, fails with `secret_input_refused`, as does an HTTP authentication challenge there or on a Lent page; `secrets.*`, `session.http_credentials`, `proxy.server` and the proxy's credentials set by a Client acting for a model fail with `setting_locked`, and set by a Recipe are refused, while a Recipe's pool kind is kept; a `saveIdentity` from a Client acting for a model or a Recipe run waits for a person's approval; `browser.permissions` with a clipboard permission in a headful window on the person's desktop fails with `setting_not_applicable`; storage outside a bound source's `domains` is refused; a Saved identity from a login bound to its machine stays bound, and its export bundle used on another machine fails with `session_bound_to_machine`; a value that is not a Secret reference is refused with `setting_invalid`; `session.http_credentials` answer only a listed domain's challenge. The Page check's own lines run in the page suite (`sessions.page_check`) on Collie's executable: with `session.page_check` set, the portal's login page answering 200 and the login form its expired login answers with a 200 raise `logged_out`, a page that is neither the expected page nor the login page raises `page_check_failed` and an expected page answers its response, each by every form of page match (a URL glob and regular expression, a CSS and XPath selector, a text); without one, the built-in detector judges a redirect to the login page a lost login, lets a `goto` to a login page through, and Provenance marks each page it judged as weakly checked. The `blocked` lines, with the Fetch edge cases' Collie 429, the Session ownership cases on the program's and an Agent's Sessions and the block-detection corpus met by a Session, run there too (`sessions.blocked`): a 429 with a `Retry-After` in seconds or as an HTTP-date raises `blocked` from its `goto` with `rate_limit` and `retry_after`, the same URL then loads at once and the next navigation sends the Session's cookie; a 403 behind a Block cause raises it from its `goto`, from a `waitForURL` waiting for the navigation that met it, or else, after a click on a link, from the page's next call, once; every corpus page raises its cause or answers its response; on a Run opened over a Connection of the MCP server as on the program's, a challenge, a 403 and a 429 raise `blocked` and `page.request` answers the 429; and the access-policy shelves' `402`, `403` and challenge served with `200` answer on the same page. Each Run's CDP trace shows one browser context, so no Session was replaced, and the Politeness file names none of the sites' domains.
  _Why:_ they are the engine failure points of the golden scenario "Log in once with a person, reuse the login headless", and the leaked secrets, unbounded pauses and lost logins competitors shipped (ADR 0092).
- **Human pause ending cases** ([ADR 0113](docs/adr/0113-a-human-pause-whose-page-closes-raises-call-cancelled-naming-what-closed-and-a-lost-live-view-ends-no-pause.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine and against the engine on Proving ground hosts with a headful browser. Against the Scripted engine: `call_cancelled` raises its class with `CallCancelledDetails` (`by`, `closed`, `person`) in every SDK, Rust through `Error::details`, and an unknown `by` or `closed` value is kept as received. Against the engine: closing a paused page's tab ends `page.pause()` at once with `call_cancelled`, `by: "browser"` and `closed: "page"`, never `human_pause_declined`, as does the page's own `window.close()`; a Gesture held back by that pause and a `waitFor` in flight on the page fail the same way, a later call on the page fails with `closed`, and the Session keeps its cookies and opens a new page; quitting the browser fails calls in flight on every page of its Sessions with `closed: "browser"` and ends those Sessions; another Client stopping the Run and the program closing the page from another task give `by: "client"` with `closed: "run"` and `by: "program"` with `closed: "page"`; an Agent's task whose page closes returns `abort` with `by: "browser"`; closing the Live view, or dropping its connection, leaves the pause waiting until `until` holds or a person answers; the Live view offers no close for a paused page; a crashed renderer fails the pause with `page_crashed`; the Run trace holds how each pause ended, and the Audit log holds the pauses ended by "Done", "Hand back" or "Decline" with the person and those ended by a closed page with `by: "browser"`, and none ended by its condition, its timeout or the program's own cancel.
  _Why:_ they are failure point 1 of the golden scenario "Log in once with a person, reuse the login headless", which Playwright reports with one `TargetClosedError` for a close and a crash and resumes as if done when its Inspector closes (ADR 0113).
- **Agent conformance cases** ([ADR 0100](docs/adr/0100-collies-agent-is-one-bounded-call-on-a-page-judged-by-the-programs-success-check-and-replayed-whole-from-the-plan.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine, with the Scripted model behind it (ADR 0065): a task whose model reports done while the program's Success check fails gets the failure back and goes on, and ends `rejected` if the Success check never passes; a task that reaches `maxSteps`, the stall rule, `timeout.agent` or a Limit returns `ended: stopped` with that reason and its Grounded partial answer, with `reported` absent when the model claimed nothing, and only a Limit stops the Run; an enclosing handler's timeout fails it with `deadline_exceeded`; a task another Client stops returns `abort`, while the caller's own cancellation keeps its language's form; `steps()` called after the task began still starts at step 1 and loses none, and raises the task's error when it failed; an answer whose value appears on no observed page and in no tool's result reads `needs-review`; a replayed task makes no model call; a Drift, or a failed Success check after the replay, hands over to the loop from that step; under `plan.frozen` a Drift raises `plan_drift`, a missing entry `plan_step_missing`, and a failed Success check after the replay finishes the task `rejected`; with no model configured a missing entry raises `model_not_configured`, while a task its Plan holds whole replays and finishes with no model configured; an Agent tool's gesture on the task's page runs while the task waits for it; a tool that raises a `GoodfetchError` ends the task with that code, any other exception with `agent_tool_failed`; two tools with one name, or one named like a built-in action, fail with `agent_tool_invalid` before any step; a `PageError` from the Success check function goes back to the model, another `GoodfetchError` ends the task with its code, and any other exception raises `success_check_threw`; a replay checks before an Effect only the targets the recorded run saw before it, so a target that appears after an Effect on the same page is no Drift; a sent Effect left uncertain ends the task with `effect_uncertain`, and a declined confirmation with `effect_not_confirmed`; a navigation outside `agent.domains` is refused and given back to the model, while the page's own subresources load; `session.recovery.agent` off, or no model and no login Plan, skips the AI agent login step, and `logged_out` names why; an error raised after steps carries `agentTask` with the steps, the partial answer and any tool left uncertain; with `agent.human_pause` on, a person's answer, a decline and an expired pause each go back to the model; a tool with a `category` asks for confirmation unless the run allows it, and always for payment and accepting terms; a replayed task's `reported` is absent and its `ended` is `finished`; the tabs a task opened are closed, except the page it ended on, and on a Borrowed browser only released.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenario "Reach a goal from one instruction, with limits and a verdict", and the false successes, silent loops, hidden step caps and off-site navigation competitors shipped (ADR 0100).
- **Dataset and Record conformance cases** ([ADR 0103](docs/adr/0103-a-handler-pushes-records-staged-with-its-attempt-into-datasets-its-crawl-declares-and-a-reader-never-slows-the-crawl.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine, and against the engine on Proving ground hosts where a commit, a Sink or a remote engine is involved; on Collie's executable for the Key-value store outside a handler and a Collie engine's `sinks.*` cases, and on Foxhound's for the rest (ADR 0094):
  - **Declaring.** A second Dataset of one name fails at once with `dataset_duplicate`, and a key on a field the schema does not require with `dataset_key_invalid`. A resume whose crawl no longer declares a Dataset holding Records fails with `dataset_unknown`, and one whose key changed with `dataset_key_invalid`, before any work; one whose schema changed resumes.
  - **Pushing.** A Record missing a field of its Dataset's schema fails `tsc`, pyright and mypy. A push to another crawl's Dataset fails with `dataset_unknown`. A Record that fails its schema natively or in the engine, is not JSON, or lacks a key field of a Dataset with a key and no schema fails `push` with `record_invalid` and is not staged. Caught, the attempt's other Records commit; escaping the handler, the request ends `failed` and is not retried.
  - **Committing once.** Records, enqueues and Key-value store writes through `ctx.run` that an attempt staged are discarded when its handler fails, or when it retries, escalates, skips, times out, loses its lease or ends with the Run's close. A `push` left unawaited until after the attempt ended fails with `attempt_ended`. A request retried twice commits its Records once, numbered with no gaps. A sample comparison commits only the Records of the browser attempt whose blocking setting the route keeps, and a Label whose browser attempt enqueues links the `http` attempt does not find is not moved to `http`. A crash in mid-commit, and a zombie copy with a stale run epoch, commit no Record twice. A Key-value store write through the program's own Run value inside a handler lands even when the attempt fails.
  - **Hooks.** A "Record before commit" Hook sees only the committing attempt's Records. Its drop or reject leaves the request `succeeded`, counted per Dataset on its outcome. A transformed Record that no longer fits its schema is rejected, and `rejectedRecords()` reads it back as JSON with its number. A Hook that fails discards the attempt's staged outputs.
  - **Reading.** `records()` called after Records have committed starts at the first, `after` resumes past a number, and the iteration ends when the Run ends. With a `records()` iterator open and never read, the crawl reaches `finished` with every request committed, and no commit waits on the reader. Through a handler's `ctx.run`, `records()` reads what has committed and ends. A reader on another engine than the one whose writer works the Run, on Foxhound's executable, is sent each Record as it commits and ends only once the Run ended. A Record its native parse rejects ends the loop with `record_invalid` and its number.
  - **Provenance and Secret values.** Every Record `records()` reads names in its Provenance its crawl request, its tier and how it was chosen, its document's encoding and where that came from, and the Clean-up rules the Run holds to; a Record whose page met no Wall, payment, Response cache or Response recording names none, and one read through a Value transform names its text, locale and zone. A Record pushed holding a value the Run resolved is kept as `%name%` in its Dataset and in its rejection table, and the Secret canaries are found in no Record, Export, dead letter or WARC file (ADR 0067).
  - **Code-free crawls.** A `crawl.start` with `extraction` and no handler, on Foxhound's executable over the protocol against the Proving ground, is never sent a `crawl_request.handle`: from the Markdown task's index under `follow: scope`, `pages` holds one Record per page, keyed by its URL, holding exactly the formats asked for, `status` and `fetched_at`, each committed against the schema the engine generated from the request, and the oracle verifies each page's Markdown; `follow: none` sends the Proving ground only the seeds; `follow: sitemap` fetches the seed and its site's own entries of the sitemap its robots.txt names on another host, and none outside the scope; a start that also declares a Dataset fails with `invalid_params`. The schema and the sitemap seeds are also checked in process as functions of the request. With `fields` on the `http` tier, the Proving ground's `extraction.test/values` page gives a text, a link resolved against its base URL, a `match`'s group, a number, a date and two date-times (one taking its `time_zone`'s offset, one keeping its own) from its machine-readable values, typed as the generated schema holds them, the Record's Provenance naming each Value transform with its text, locale and zone, and a locator that finds nothing leaves its field null with `no_element`; an `XPath` field escalates the page to the browser under `auto`, which reads every field alike. Each field's value, Provenance and null reason, its schema and its refusal at the start are also checked in process as functions of the element found, and a number, a date and a date and time printed for people are read under each of several locales' CLDR forms, any other form being no value, as functions of the text.
  - **Exports, the Key-value store and Sinks.** `export()` writes the Records committed so far, and `saveAs` puts the file on the program's machine, against a Daemon too. `get` on an absent key answers null; `tsc` rejects `set(key, null)`, and in Python and Rust it fails with `value_invalid`, as bytes read with `get` do. Through `ctx.run`, `get` sees the attempt's own staged `set`, another attempt's stays invisible until it commits, and of two commits to one key the later wins. A Sink set on the Run naming an undeclared Dataset, in a keyed mode with no key of its own while one of its Datasets has none, or with a key naming a field one of its Datasets' schemas does not require, is refused at start with `setting_invalid`; one inherited from the connection is inactive for a crawl that declares none of its Datasets; `sinks.*` set on a Label, or on a Collie Run on a Foxhound engine, fails with `setting_not_applicable`, and given to a Run or a connection on a Collie engine with `not_in_product`. A Sink set on a Foxhound connection is inactive for the Collie Runs it opens. A Collie program starts in a project whose file holds a Sink for its crawls, and `config explain` lists that Sink as Foxhound's. Two Datasets delivered to one Sink each keep every Record, though both number from 1.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Crawl two page types into a Dataset", "Resume a killed crawl and retry its failures" and "Run a crawl inside a web service", and the partial and duplicate results competitors shipped (ADR 0103).
- **File saving conformance cases** ([ADR 0116](docs/adr/0116-a-saved-file-is-written-whole-or-not-at-all-and-a-failed-write-says-whether-its-path-or-its-storage-failed.md)) run in every SDK against the engine of the same commit, attached and through a Daemon, on Collie's executable for a Download, a Checkpoint, a Run trace and a screenshot's `path`, and on Foxhound's for an Export (ADR 0094):
  - **Path errors.** `saveAs` and a screenshot's `path` fail with `path_not_writable`, a `ProgramError` that is not retryable, when the path is in a folder the user cannot write, on a read-only filesystem, an existing folder, or under a parent that is a file. Its `reason` and `osCode` name the case, its `path` is the one given, its `fix` names the path argument, and, when the SDK wrote the path itself, the native error is its chained cause.
  - **Storage errors.** A full filesystem (a size-capped tmpfs on Linux, a small disk image on macOS and Windows) fails `saveAs` with `file_write_failed`, side `program`, reason `storage_full`, an `UnavailableError` that is not retryable, and a second `saveAs` after space is freed succeeds from the same `Export`. The engine's storage filled before `run.export` and `run.checkpoint()` fails them with side `engine`, returns no value, and leaves no unfinished file in the Run's folder.
  - **Never partial.** A `saveAs` failed mid-write, timed out or cancelled leaves the path as it was: absent, or the earlier file byte for byte, with no temporary file left beside it. A completed one replaces an existing file, creates missing parent folders, and matches the Export's size and `sha256()`. A Foxhound handler whose `ctx.run.export` lets `file_write_failed` escape ends its request `failed` with no retry, and scenario 05's program with an unwritable `books.json` fails with `path_not_writable` in every language. A `screenshot()` whose `path` cannot be written raises and returns no bytes, and `run.trace()` with the engine's storage full fails with side `engine`. A Plan file or a Response recording whose path cannot be written, or whose disk is full, makes `finish()` raise `path_not_writable` or `file_write_failed` once the Run has ended. The file at the path is left as it was, `openRun(id).report()` carries a warning naming the code, and the Plan's new versions stay pending. A Run that itself failed raises its own error instead. A start naming a Plan file with a version a person accepted, whose path cannot be written, raises `path_not_writable`, does no work, and writes the version at the next start.
  _Why:_ they are failure point 7, "the export fails", of the golden scenario "Crawl two page types into a Dataset", and the raw file errors, partial files and logged-only failures competitors shipped (ADR 0116).
- **Storage conformance cases** ([ADR 0122](docs/adr/0122-a-run-stops-resumably-before-its-disk-fills-and-a-daemons-logs-rotate-at-a-bounded-size.md)) run in every SDK against the engine of the same commit, attached and through a Daemon, on a size-capped filesystem (a tmpfs on Linux, a small disk image on macOS and Windows) holding the Goodfetch home and the run folder, with `storage.low_free` and `storage.critical_free` set against its size and a filler file to cross them, on Collie's executable for a Collie Run and on Foxhound's for a crawl with WARC output (ADR 0094):
  - **Low.** Crossing `storage.low_free` leaves the Run working: its Run trace records no more video while its snapshots and HAR go on, the Response cache stores no new entry, a periodic Checkpoint set to a folder on that filesystem is skipped while one on another filesystem is written, `progress()` reads `storage` at `low` with the folder, and the report carries `storage_low`. Freeing space brings `storage` back to `normal`.
  - **Critical.** Crossing `storage.critical_free` stops every Run whose run file is there at its next safe point: `finish()` returns the report `stopped` and `incomplete` with `stopReason` `resources`, `resource: "storage"` and the folder, no Checkpoint copy is written onto that filesystem, a new Run's start there raises `resources_exhausted`, an `UnavailableError` that is not retryable, whose `fix` names the folder, and `run.export` and `run.checkpoint()` fail with `file_write_failed`, side `engine`, writing nothing. After space is freed, a start with the Run's ID resumes it and commits no Record twice. Through a Daemon with its ops listener, `/readyz` fails while the run folder's filesystem is at `critical` and succeeds once space is freed, and the storage metrics read its level; in the per-install layout, with the run folders on a tmpfs of their own, `/readyz` and `holds="run_state"` follow the install database's filesystem instead.
  - **WARC only.** With the WARC output on a second size-capped filesystem and the run folder on one with room, crossing `storage.critical_free` on the first stops the crawl the same way.
  - **A failed write.** With both levels set to `0`, a commit the full filesystem refuses stops the Run as at `critical`; the commit recording the stop fails too, so the call waiting on it raises `resources_exhausted`, and once space is freed a start with its ID resumes it as after a crash.
  - **A failed write.** With both levels set to zero, a crawl that fills the filesystem stops at its first failed commit, with the same report, and resumes with no Record twice; its WARC file, cut back on resume, holds each request's capture once and no torn record, and reads as a Response recording (ADR 0105).
  _Why:_ they are the pitfall catalog's row O03, a disk that fills under a running crawl, and the corrupt state, silent stops and missing guard competitors ship (ADR 0122).
- **Hook conformance cases** ([ADR 0104](docs/adr/0104-a-crawl-declares-its-hooks-per-stage-in-code-a-hook-returns-its-change-and-a-program-registers-its-own-plugins-by-name.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine, and against the engine on Proving ground hosts where a fetch or a site is involved: Hooks named in settings run before a crawl's Hooks in code, which run in declaration order; a Hook scoped to Labels never runs for another Label; a Label or Dataset of another crawl fails the declaration with `label_unknown` or `dataset_unknown`, an empty `labels` or `datasets` list or a second Hook of one name on the crawl with `hook_ineffective` or `hook_duplicate`, and a navigation Hook whose Labels all force `http`, `discovered_api` or `browser_fetch` fails the Run's start with `hook_ineffective`; a before-request Hook of a POST request sees the method and body it was enqueued with, and its change keeps them; a before-request Hook runs again on a retry, an escalation and a sample-comparison run, seeing `previousError`, and its `skip` ends the request `skipped` with the reason `hook` and every failed attempt's error kept; a forbidden header fails with `hook_failed` wrapping `header_forbidden`, a Secret placeholder in a header reaches only its bound domains and never the program, and a rewritten URL outside the crawl's scope or disallowed by robots.txt is skipped, never fetched, from a Hook in code and a Script Hook alike; a navigation Hook runs on the `browser` and `light_browser` tiers, never on `browser_fetch`, and a page-ready Hook's browser-only call escalates as the handler's does; an after-response Hook's `block` ends the attempt `blocked` and retires the Session; a Script Hook at `request_ended` receives each request's outcome; a Hook past `timeout.hook` fails with `deadline_exceeded` naming it; `hook_failed` carries the stage, the Hook's name, the Label, the exception's type, message and stack and the wrapped code, and names the exception in its message; a Hook's own exception is not retried unless `crawl.retry.handler_failed` is on, and a wrapped retryable code is; a Hook that raises at the Record stage discards everything its attempt staged; the only Proxy provider registered becomes the connection's value with the origin `registered`, which `connect()`'s own `settings` or a Run's replace, a configuration file does not, and an Operator policy lock wins over with no refusal; two Proxy providers fail `connect()` only when nothing else sets the key, and a handler named as a built-in or an installed Plugin fails it too; a setting naming an unknown handler fails when its scope opens, a `type` beside a Sink handler's `sinks.<name>` entry fails the Run's start, and detaching a Run that uses a handler is refused with the key in its `fix`; a resume without a handler its Run used is refused before any work; Rust reading an undeclared name, or a second stream for an open one, fails with `setting_invalid`, and a call dropped unanswered fails as an unreachable implementation; a Rust `caller:` reference without `ConnectOptions::resolve_secret()` raises `secret_unavailable`; a handler's own exception is its kind's `<kind>_handler_failed`, a Sink's retried with the same delivery ID, and in a Collie Run not retried; a program's Fetch backend is never handed a logged-in Session's request, one whose Hook set a Secret placeholder, one whose Session has `session.http_credentials` or one whose proxy has a password, and is handed every other request with its method, body, an anonymous Session's cookies and its proxy server, the Run trace marking each request the engine sent in its place; a route on the handler's Session sees a before-request Hook's header and rewritten URL, its own header override wins, and the cookies and Secret values the engine adds reach neither; on the request's own document fetch, a route's method or body override fails with `page_value_invalid` and aborts the request, and its new URL outside the crawl's scope ends the request `skipped` with nothing sent; and while one domain's Politeness permit is taken, its requests are not handed to a Hook in code, so another domain's requests keep the leases.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenario "Crawl two page types into a Dataset" with Hooks, and the integer orders, unbounded hooks and lost retries competitors shipped (ADR 0104).
- **Rust crawl `for_each` conformance cases** ([ADR 0111](docs/adr/0111-a-rust-crawl-hands-what-it-subscribes-on-its-page-to-serve-which-reads-it-for-the-rest-of-the-attempt.md)) run in the Rust SDK's async and blocking APIs, against the engine on Proving ground hosts: a route a before-navigation Hook hands to `ctx.for_each` answers the page's requests through the navigation and the handler, on a single-threaded executor, and stops with the attempt; its requests are answered concurrently, while events and locator-handler turns arrive one at a time in order; a route handler that returns an error or panics aborts its request, is removed, and raises `page_handler_failed` from the attempt's call waiting on that request or its next page call, and a listener's error raises `listener_failed`; a dialog or locator-handler turn dropped unanswered gives its default; `for_each` on a context read from `run.hook_requests()` or `run.crawl_requests()` fails with `setting_invalid`; a route `runHandler`'s handler hands to `for_each` answers that attempt's later requests, and a crawl started on a Response recording answers a Hook's route; and an init script kept with `Registration::keep()` runs in the navigation after the Hook returned, while one dropped is removed.
  _Why:_ they are the failure point of the golden scenario "Crawl with HTTP and a browser only where needed" in Rust, whose Hook's route first ran on an untracked task of the program's own that lost its errors and needed Tokio (ADR 0111).
- **Run conformance cases** ([ADR 0102](docs/adr/0102-a-program-names-its-run-by-an-id-that-a-start-resumes-ends-it-with-a-run-report-and-reads-what-waits-and-why.md)) run in every SDK against the engine: a crawl killed part-way and started again under its ID resumes with no Record committed twice; a third start under the ID returns the Run `ended` with `nothingDone` set and calls no handler; `retryFailed` re-admits only the failed requests in the same Run and answers how many; a second program starting a live Run's ID fails with `run_in_use`, other seeds with `run_conflict`, a dropped Label with pending requests with `label_unknown`, a changed `crawl.scope.*` with `setting_not_applicable`, and a Limit above the one standing with `limit_raise_refused`; a deleted or corrupted run file fails with `run_state_unavailable` and starts nothing afresh; a scope left without `finish()` ends the Run `stopped`, and `stop()` returns its report within `timeout.close`, while a handler that never returns makes it raise `deadline_exceeded` naming the setting and the Run still stops at its next safe point; an unread `outcomes()` iterator never slows the crawl, and one with `after: "now"` yields only later outcomes; `progress()` gives its first value at once and names the wait reason and setting of a domain held by its delay, and on Foxhound's executable against the Proving ground each wait reason the engine builds (a domain's concurrency, delay and backoff, the handlers' concurrency and the governor) names its setting; `openCrawlRun` reads a Run without resuming it, and on a Run whose writer died its sequences end and `report()` fails with `run_interrupted`; a `page.pause({ reason, until })` appears in `progress()`'s `pauses` with its reason, its `until` and a `deadline` 24 hours after it began, the first of the two timeouts in a scheduled run, and leaves it when its condition holds; a sitemap seed whose entries match no rule and one of whose nested sitemaps answers 404 counts those entries under `no_rule` and lists that sitemap with its status, in the progress and the report; a drained keep-alive crawl says `idle` and works again on `addSeed`; a resume advances `progress()`'s `epoch`; a resume of a Run stopped by a daily Limit raises `limit_reached` naming the Limit's scope, the scope's ID and `resetsAt`, and the report's `usage` gives each applied Limit its period; `trace()` copies the Run trace with `saveAs`, and with the trace off returns none while the report's `weakened` names the setting; an Agent task with no Success check leaves the Run `needs-review` with a reason naming its `callId`; on a view of a Run whose writer died, `records()` also ends after the last committed Record; an inherited Sink that names none of the crawl's Datasets is `inactive` in the report's `sinks`, listing the names it gave; a Collie Run that `finish()` ends `verified` writes its promoted Plan version to its Plan file before `finish()` returns, while a stopped one promotes nothing; `openRun` with an unknown ID fails with `run_not_found`, and a Run that ends `failed` makes `finish()` raise its error; and every point in time the Run carries is a `Date`, a time-zone-aware `datetime` in UTC or a `SystemTime`.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenario "Resume a killed crawl and retry its failures", and the run-state surprises and silent hangs competitors shipped (ADR 0102).
- **Model and usage conformance cases** ([ADR 0101](docs/adr/0101-a-model-is-named-provider-slash-model-its-capabilities-and-prices-are-declared-and-every-ai-result-carries-its-priced-usage.md)) run in every SDK. Against the Scripted engine: each Model provider code raises its class under its group with its details, `model_unauthorized` and `model_funds_exhausted` not retryable and `model_unavailable` retryable with its `retryAfter`; every AI result carries `ModelUsage`, an error from a call that made model calls carries its `usage`, a `null` price stays `null`, and `run.usage()` reads `RunUsage` with its Limits and the uncapped mark; unknown strategies and Limit kinds are kept as received. Against the binary of the same commit with the testing kit's Scripted model as the program's own provider: it answers through `modelProviders` (TypeScript, Python) or a name declared with `ConnectOptions::model_provider` and read with `model_requests` (Rust, where an undeclared name, or a second stream while one is open, fails with `setting_invalid`), only for the Runs its connection opened, gets the `prompt` strategy unless its models declare others, a name taken by a built-in fails with `setting_invalid`, an exception from it, or in Rust another error given to `fail`, is `model_handler_failed`, a dropped Rust call fails as an unreachable provider, and past `timeout.model` an async handler's task is cancelled, a sync Python handler's late answer is discarded and a Rust call's `cancelled()` resolves. Against the binary with the Fake model and `httpmock` stubs at declared provider addresses (ADR 0065), which see every request: a Model reference whose provider is unknown fails where its scope opens with `setting_invalid` naming the closest, and a call's `model` beats `ai.model`; a price set in code or by a Recipe, and a provider's `api_key`, `headers` or `base_url` set by a Recipe or a Client acting for a model, fail with `setting_locked`; a key and secret headers reach only their provider's address, and a built-in pointed elsewhere sends no default key; `auto` steps down from native to tool to prompt when a stub rejects a strategy and never on an invalid answer, which is retried, a rejected forced strategy fails with `model_request_rejected`, and `extraction_invalid` and `model_output_invalid` name the model and the strategy; an attempt past `timeout.model` is retried, and `deadline_exceeded` names `timeout.model` or the enclosing `timeout.ai`, whichever ended first; a step replayed from the Plan reports no call and a zero price. The price rules (a local built-in at its default address priced zero, a redirected one unpriced, `model_price_unknown` for a missing price or rate, a `null` total rather than a partial sum) are pure functions of the table, the address and the Limit, tested in-process. Model recordings of each format, `via gateway` where ADR 0076 records so, and `httpmock` stubs for the caps no recording holds, map each provider's error type and message to its code (OpenAI's `insufficient_quota` sent as a 429 and Anthropic's spend limits sent as a 400 or a 429 to `model_funds_exhausted`, never retried; a rate limit to `model_unavailable`) and sum Anthropic's cached input into `inputTokens`. In TypeScript, a bare model ID as a call's `model` fails `tsc`.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenarios "Extract a typed object with a chosen model", "Extract with AI inside a crawl, learning once per page type" and "Reach a goal from one instruction, with limits and a verdict", and the provider churn, opaque structured-output errors and hidden spend competitors shipped (ADR 0101).
- **Model allowance conformance cases** ([ADR 0107](docs/adr/0107-a-runs-model-allowance-refuses-its-model-calls-once-spent-while-the-run-goes-on.md)) run in every SDK. Against the Scripted engine: `model_allowance_reached` raises its class under the `refused` group, not retryable, with its `limit` naming `ai.allowance.<kind>`, its value and layer, and its `usage`; an Agent's task returns `ended: stopped` with the reason `model_allowance`; `RunUsage.modelAllowance` reads one entry per kind set, kept apart from `limits`, and an unknown kind or skip reason is kept as received. Against the binary of the same commit with the Fake model (ADR 0065): `ai.allowance.*` set on a Label, a Session or a page fails with `setting_not_applicable`; with `ai.allowance.price`, `tokens` or `calls` set where the Run starts, concurrent handlers reserving each call's worst case never spend past it, the first call that does not fit is refused, and every later model call of the Run is refused, a smaller one included, while a step replayed from the Plan is still extracted with no model call; a crawl handler that lets the refusal escape ends its request `skipped` with `model_allowance` and the error's message, its staged Records discarded, one that catches it pushes its Record, and the Run ends `finished` and `incomplete`, its reason naming the allowance and the count; the Run never stops for the allowance, a Limit reached at the same call still stops it with `limit_reached`, the uncapped mark is unchanged by an allowance, and under `price` an unpriced model fails with `model_price_unknown`. The calls counted (compiles, Repairs, extraction retries, a discarded attempt's calls, the program's own provider, the parts before a resume) are pinned by one test each, and a simulation test (ADR 0060) checks that a Partitioned crawl's Partitions never pass their shared allowance. The testing kit's `runHandler` (ADR 0105) returns `skipped` with `model_allowance` for a refusal that escapes the handler.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points "The budget runs out part-way" and "Cost or usage is not reported" of the golden scenario "Extract with AI inside a crawl, learning once per page type", and a cap that refuses calls must never be passed under concurrency, as LiteLLM's check-then-charge is ([#40095](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/issues/40095)) (ADR 0107).
