- **Writability shape cases** ([ADR 0117](docs/adr/0117-what-coding-agents-misread-is-reshaped-an-ai-result-holds-its-answer-in-how-it-ended-resource-types-are-blocked-by-a-setting-and-a-start-names-its-run-id.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine, and against the engine on Proving ground hosts where a page or a crawl is involved:
  - **How AI work ended.** A task stopped by `maxSteps` returns `ended` `stopped` holding its reason and the Grounded `partial`, and a task whose model gives up `stopped` with `gave_up` and `reported` holding the claim; a finished task's `ended` holds a complete `output`. Type tests: reading `output` before narrowing on `ended` fails `tsc`, pyright and mypy, a Rust `match` on `Ended` with no wildcard arm compiles, and a Python `StepResult[Author]` or `AgentResult[Author]` passes where `StepResult[object]` or `AgentResult[object]` is expected.
  - **Resource blocking.** With `browser.block_resources: ["image", "font"]` on a crawl's start, the Proving ground receives no image or font request from any browser fetch of the crawl, a sample comparison of an HTTP Label's page included; set on any Label, it fails with `setting_not_applicable` (a type error in TypeScript and Python), and a type other than `image`, `media`, `font` and `stylesheet` fails `tsc`, pyright and mypy, and through `call` is refused with `setting_invalid`.
  - **Run IDs and failures.** `runId` names the Run in the Run index; after `retryFailed`, `failedRequests()` lists none of the requests that then succeeded, while `outcomes()` still yields both of their endings, and with `blocked: true` it adds the blocked ones.
  - **Scripted extraction.** `scriptedExtraction(data)` answers an `extract` in the default `compiled` mode with locators that read `data` from the saved page, and in `model` mode with `data`; a value not on the page raises `extraction_incomplete` naming its field.
  - **Start-time refusals.** A Run whose `ai.model` has no price fails its start with `model_price_unknown` under a currency Limit or `ai.allowance.price`, and starts under `plan.frozen`; a Session source or a secret binding naming `www.example.com` in `domains` fails with `setting_invalid` naming `example.com`.
  _Why:_ they are the shapes the Agent-writability check saw coding agents get wrong more than once: a stopped task's lost partial answer, images blocked too late, an ended Run treated as new, a compiled step scripted with data, and an unpriced model met only at its first call (ADR 0117).
- **Effect conformance cases** ([ADR 0106](docs/adr/0106-a-program-declares-an-effect-as-a-keyed-block-whose-output-the-effect-journal-keeps-judged-by-what-the-engine-sent.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine and against the engine on the Proving ground: a body that returned is not run again by a retry, an escalation, a sample comparison, a re-run after a lost lease, `retryFailed` or a Collie program started again with its Run's ID, and the call returns `journal` with the output it kept; a body that fails before anything was sent propagates its own error and runs again at the next declaration; one that fails after a click or a `page.request` raises `effect_uncertain` with the original as its cause, every later declaration of that key raises it without running the body, and the crawl request ends `failed`, not retried, with the Run `needs-review` and the Effect in `uncertainEffects`; a repeatable one runs again instead; a process killed after the first command went out leaves the Effect uncertain, and one killed before it leaves it not performed; a cancellation (Python's `CancelledError`, a TypeScript abort, a dropped Rust future) and an attempt's end inside a body propagate unchanged, and the key's next declaration acts on what was sent; while a raw CDP attach or a lent page is open, a body that fails before any command of its own counts as sent; a key declared again while its body runs, or after its answer in one attempt, fails with `effect_key_reused`, and two overlapping attempts of one crawl request run its body once; a person's "it happened" makes the next declaration return `reconciled` (in a crawl, through `retryFailed`), and "it did not happen" performs it once more with the same `idempotencyKey`; a resume from a Checkpoint written while the Run was running makes a key the Effect journal lacks uncertain; `idempotencyKey` is the same across attempts, resumes and machines and differs between Runs; an empty or overlong key fails with `effect_key_invalid`, and a non-JSON or oversized output fails with `effect_output_invalid` when nothing was sent and with `effect_uncertain` caused by it after a send; a Rust body's own error type comes back unchanged; no request sent inside a body is hedged, coalesced or cached; an `enqueue`, a `ctx.push`, or an `addSeed` or Key-value store write through `ctx.run` while a body runs in the attempt fails with `effect_staging_refused`; a crawl request enqueued with POST and a body is retried and escalated like a GET, and never hedged or coalesced; on a Run that borrows a Crawlee page, a retried handler's second declaration of a done key returns `journal`; a `repeatable` Agent tool that raised ends the task with no uncertain Effect, and a replay of the task calls it again, and a replay of one tool call gets the same `idempotencyKey`. A command a Response recording answers counts as sent; `runHandler` given a prior `journal`, `reconciled` or `uncertain` answer for a key answers that key's declaration without running its body (returning `journal` or `reconciled`, raising `effect_uncertain`) and lists it in the attempt's `effects`, and the test engine's `reconcile` makes the next declaration return `reconciled` or perform it again; on a Run that `startedAs` `ended`, a key the Effect journal holds returns its answer and any other raises `run_ended`. The Collie Run and Agent tool cases run on Collie's executable and the crawl cases on Foxhound's (ADR 0094).
  _Why:_ an order placed twice after a crash or a retry is the failure a declared Effect exists to prevent, and Crawlee's retried handlers repeat what they sent (ADR 0106).
- **Rust program-error conformance cases** ([ADR 0109](docs/adr/0109-a-rust-programs-own-code-returns-any-error-as-a-box-error-read-as-the-other-sdks-read-an-exception.md)) run in the Rust SDK against the Scripted engine, and through `run_handler` and `run_hook` in the testing kit: a handler returning a `ParseIntError` with `?` ends its attempt `failed` with `handler_failed`, whose type name reads `BoxError`, whose message is the error's `Display` followed by each `source()`, whose stack is empty, and whose `source()` in the process is the boxed error; a `&str`, a `Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync>` and an `anyhow::Error` returned with `?` do the same; the SDK's own `Error` returned with `?` keeps its code and retryable flag (a retryable one is retried under `crawl.retry.max`), while one wrapped in the program's own error, or held in an `anyhow::Error`, is `handler_failed`; `return Err(ctx.skip(…))` and `ctx.retry(…)` end the attempt `skipped` and `retry_requested`, and a Hook's `skip`, `block`, `retry`, `drop_record` and `reject` likewise; a panic in a handler, a Hook or a login step inside `serve` and the blocking `serve` is `handler_failed`, `hook_failed` or `logged_out`'s cause with the type name `panic` and its payload as the message; a Hook's own error is `hook_failed` with no wrapped code, a login step's is chained to `logged_out`, an Agent tool's is `agent_tool_failed`, the Success check's is `success_check_threw`, an error given to an SDK handler call's `fail` is its kind's `<kind>_handler_failed`, and any error a `for_each` function returns, the SDK's own included, follows its item kind's rule (`page_handler_failed` for a route, with the original as its cause), each carrying the same details; a Goodfetch code a Hook returns stays wrapped in `hook_failed` naming it.
  _Why:_ a Rust program fails with its own errors through `?`, as TypeScript and Python programs throw, and a code the program did not mean to keep must not leak through a wrapper (ADR 0109).
- **Connection and provisioning error cases** ([ADR 0115](docs/adr/0115-a-cdp-url-the-engine-cannot-use-and-a-missing-browser-build-fail-with-one-error-code-per-remedy.md)) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine, and against Collie's executable with Proving ground hosts and a prepared Browser cache: each code raises its class under its group with its `retryable` flag, `fix` and details; a `cdp_url` on a closed port raises `cdp_url_unreachable` with `failure: connection_refused` at once, not after a timeout, and an unresolvable name `name_not_resolved`; a plain web server, a page target's WebSocket and Node's inspector each raise `cdp_url_not_browser` naming what answered, and the `fix` gives the browser endpoint the same host's `/json/version` lists; a 401 and a 403 raise `cdp_url_unauthorized` naming the header and its Secret reference, never its value; a private address from the program and a loopback address from a Client acting for a model raise `cdp_url_not_allowed` before any connection is opened; an address that never answers raises `deadline_exceeded` naming it. A build absent from a read-only cache raises `browser_not_installed` naming the build, the Product version that pins it and that Product's install command; the same under `offline` raises `artifact_unavailable_offline` with `offline` and its layer in `limit`; a download served truncated, with a 404 or with a wrong digest raises `artifact_download_failed` with its `failure` and leaves nothing in the cache. `connect()` raises none of them; `run.newSession`, the default Session's first `newPage`, a crawl's Session pool opening a Session on a closed `cdp_url`, and a crawl request's escalation under `fetch.tier: auto` do, the request retried under `crawl.retry.max` for the retryable `cdp_url_unreachable` and `artifact_download_failed`, and otherwise ending `failed`. The crawl case runs on Foxhound's executable (ADR 0094).
  _Why:_ they are failure point 4 of the golden scenario "Add an AI step to an existing Playwright program" in Rust and the setup of every scenario, and the untyped connection and missing-browser errors competitors shipped (ADR 0115).
- **Completeness tests** check that every catalog operation, Error code and event has its generated counterpart in each SDK, in the package of its Product: Collie's entries in the Collie client package, Foxhound's in Foxhound's (ADR 0095), and that each SDK's manifest of hand-written members names a catalog entry or a reason for each, a member with no entry is spelled from the canonical words and form its line declares, two members of one receiver listed for the same entry fail, and a hand-written member's words appear on the same receiver in every SDK that has the receiver unless its line says why only that SDK has it (so a Rust-only `label_with` beside `label` fails), carries its entry's doc text and markers, and holds no line the generator could express (ADR 0099). Codegen refuses a name a language cannot use (`self`, `super`, `crate`, `Self`; TypeScript's `then` and `constructor`; Python's dunders; the Rust trait methods `clone`, `eq`, `fmt` and `hash`; `call`; a built-in class name), two entries with one member on one receiver, a prefix used against its meaning, an MCP tool name over 39 characters, and a value of an open value type over 63 bytes.
- **Product conformance cases** (ADR 0095) run in every SDK against the Scripted engine: Foxhound's `hello` carries Foxhound as the Product it needs, and when the Scripted engine refuses it with `not_in_product`, `connect()` raises that error with its details; Collie's `connect()` succeeds against a Scripted engine whose reply names Foxhound; the connection's `engine` value reports the engine's Product and Product version; and a Collie Client reading a Usage kind, a Limit scope, a Notification event, a Plugin kind or a Hook stage only Foxhound carries from a Scripted engine that names Foxhound keeps it as an unknown value, with no decoding error (ADR 0079). Type tests in each language check that a Foxhound handler's Page and a Collie program's Page are one type, and that the connection type both share accepts a `Collie` and a `Foxhound`.
- **Testing kit conformance cases** ([ADR 0105](docs/adr/0105-a-programs-tests-replay-a-response-recording-in-a-test-engine-that-reaches-no-site.md)) run in every SDK, in both of its modes, against the executable of the same commit on Proving ground hosts: Collie's kit cases on Collie's executable, and `runHandler`, `runHook` and a crawl's recording, which Foxhound's kit holds, on Foxhound's (ADR 0094). Replaying a Response recording, a recorded page is answered while the Proving ground receives no request; a page the file lacks raises `response_not_recorded` with its method, URL and nearest recorded URLs, a crawl request it lacks ends `failed` and is not retried, and a subresource it lacks fails in the page and is listed in the Run report; a recorded login form replays though its Secret is masked, and a response's `Vary` headers are matched; a fulfilled route answers before the recording, a host `resolve` maps to loopback is reached, a private address is answered from the file, and no replayed or missing request makes a DNS query; robots.txt is read from the file alone, leaving the copy shared across runs untouched, one the file lacks reads as no rules, and a crawl of one domain replays without Politeness delays; a replayed browser page reads its recorded time from `Date.now()` and the same `Math.random` sequence on every run. Recording replaces the file atomically when the Run ends, a resumed Run's file holds both halves, and the file holds no `Cookie`, `Authorization` or `Proxy-Authorization` header, no `Set-Cookie` value and no Secret value (a Secret canary). A test engine's Run that names no recording raises `response_not_recorded` for any site, the engine reads no project file unless named, finds browsers, and in Rust its Product's executable, in the browser cache of the caller's home (the one `GOODFETCH_HOME` names in the environment map its `env` option passes) without writing to it, or through `GOODFETCH_BROWSERS_PATH` and its Product's engine-path variable, never the other Product's, and a model call with nothing configured raises `model_not_configured` before any request. `runHandler` reports each ending (`returned`; `skipped` with its reason; `retry_requested`; `escalated` naming the call, with no browser process started on `http`, under `fetch.tier: auto`, and `failed` with `browser_required` under a forced `http` tier; `blocked` for a Block cause; `failed` with `handler_failed`, `response_not_ok` or `response_unavailable`) with the Records, enqueues, seeds and writes the attempt staged, kept even when it ended early, each Record as its Dataset's schema parses it, and a Record failing its schema raising `record_invalid` without being staged; a swallowed `skip` or `retry` still ends the attempt, and a later call fails with `attempt_ended`; two enqueues of one URL with two POST bodies stage two requests, the same body twice is answered `duplicate`, and a link an `enqueueLinks` Payload function returns `null` for is reported `skipped_by_payload`; a `tier` other than the Label's forced one raises `setting_invalid`; `runHook` with a name the crawl does not hold raises `hook_unknown`; a Scripted model answers a retry of a retryable error with its next reply, raises `scripted_model_exhausted` past its replies, and the engine's close raises `scripted_model_unused`; `fakeError` builds each code's class with its group, retryable flag and details. An Agent's task started with its Plan, `plan.frozen` and a recording replays with no model call and no request, ending `finished` with `reported` absent. The kit's own sample test runs unchanged under Vitest, Jest and `node:test`, pytest (asyncio and trio, which never collects `TestEngine`) and `unittest`, and `cargo test`.
  _Why:_ they are the failure points of the golden scenario "Test a crawl offline", and the silent misses, cassettes that go live and runner coupling competitors shipped (ADR 0105).
- **Generated code is type-checked** by `tsc` with `skipLibCheck: false`, pyright strict and mypy `--strict`.
- **Mapping and decoding are property-tested** (fast-check, hypothesis): user-data values with any keys, `__proto__` included, come back unchanged.
- **Type tests:**
  - TypeScript: `*.test-d.ts` files with `expect-type`'s `expectTypeOf` and `@ts-expect-error`, compiled by `tsc --noEmit` against the built `.d.ts`. Not Vitest's `typecheck` mode, tsd or TSTyche.
  - Python: `tests/types/` files with `typing.assert_type` and `# type: ignore[code]`, under pyright strict with `reportUnnecessaryTypeIgnoreComment = "error"` and under mypy `--strict`, whose `--warn-unused-ignores` reports an unused ignore.
- **Python:**
  - the test-support plugin's `anyio_backend` lists `asyncio` and `trio` explicitly, and a missing trio fails the session;
  - every Client conformance case runs on asyncio, on trio and through the sync API;
  - free-threaded builds run the whole suite with `PYTHON_GIL=0`, and fail if the GIL is on; the sync API's unit tests also run under pytest-run-parallel. Every Linux platform in the Release suite; macOS and Windows in a nightly-only job.
- **TypeScript runtimes:** Vitest runs on Node with the `forks` pool. The `smoke/*.mjs` scripts (only `node:assert`) each run spawned by a Vitest test:
  - on Bun and Deno 2, with an Attached engine and with a Daemon, covering the runtime-dependent Client conformance cases, whose IDs the script declares;
  - on `workerd` (Miniflare) and Vercel's edge runtime, against a Daemon only, since they cannot start a process. Deno 2's `daemon` run stands for Deno Deploy (ADR 0049).
- **Installed packages:**
  - npm packages install from Verdaccio with no uplink, with npm, pnpm, Yarn, Bun and Deno, into a path with a space and a non-ASCII character, and the smoke scripts check the installed version; `--omit=optional` must fail at first use with Goodfetch's error;
  - each install keeps its manager's release-age gate on and passes it through ADR 0030's exclusion snippet, read from the source the release notes use; one run per manager without the snippet must not install the packed version (uv's snippet is not tested: `--no-index` has no gate);
  - wheels install into a fresh environment (`uv pip install --no-index --find-links`, and `pip`) and run `tests/integration` unchanged, on glibc and Alpine;
  - each Product's crate builds a temporary consumer from `cargo package`'s output;
  - one environment per package manager installs both Products' packages, and both executables run there.
- **Models are scripted only through the testing kit's Scripted model** (`goodfetch_collie::testing`, `@goodfetch/collie/testing`, `goodfetch_collie.testing`, and Foxhound's `goodfetch_foxhound::testing`, `@goodfetch/foxhound/testing` and `goodfetch_foxhound.testing`). The kit's test engine helper is an Attached engine in TypeScript and Python and the linked engine in Rust.

### CLI

- **The binary runs under assert_cmd and is snapshotted with insta**, through `goodfetch-test-support`'s helper. One snapshot holds stdout, stderr and the exit code.
  - `cli(&env, executable).args([…]).env(name, value).run()` (with `.stdin(text)` for what it reads on standard input, empty otherwise) runs the executable the test gives, by path (its own crate's `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_goodfetch-collie")` or `…-foxhound`), in the test's folder with the cleared map (whose `LANG=C.UTF-8` and `TZ=UTC` are the fixed locale and zone unless the test named others) plus `NO_COLOR=1`, `COLUMNS=100` and `TERM=dumb`. It returns a `CliOutput` (`exit`, `stdout`, `stderr`), which `assert_cli_snapshot!(output)` (or with a name, or an inline `@"…"`) snapshots as one value under the shared filters. It fails with `CliError::EscapeByte` when piped stdout or stderr holds `0x1B`, and with `CliError::NotUtf8` on bytes that are not UTF-8.
  - `--json` runs: `cli(&env, executable).args([…, "--json"]).json("SettingsExplanation")` runs the command as `run()` does, then checks that stdout holds exactly one JSON document, valid against that type of the committed bundle's `$defs` (read as a file, so the test names the type and test support stays on values); `.json_lines("EventEnvelope")` checks that each line of stdout is one document of the command's declared event type. They fail with `CliError::SchemaViolated` (each violation, and the line among JSON Lines), `CliError::SecondDocument`, `CliError::NotJson` (a stray line, a blank line or no document) and, before anything starts, `CliError::SchemaUnknown`. The `CliJson` they give holds the `documents` and an `output` whose stdout is those documents pretty-printed, for `assert_cli_snapshot!`; that leaves out stdout's layout, key order and number spelling, so a test running one command in several modes also compares `printed`, stdout as the command printed it, across them.
  - Both modes: `.mode(Mode::Daemon)` starts the executable's Daemon in the test's home (`Daemon::start_program`), adds `--daemon` after the arguments, runs the command, then stops the Daemon and fails with `CliError::Daemon` when it still runs or left its runtime file; `.mode(Mode::Attached)` adds `--attached`, so even a command whose value outlives it, which reaches the Daemon by default, runs in the executable's own process and starts none. With no mode, the arguments run as given, on the engine the executable chooses: a test of a value-bound command or an opener that is about something else passes `--attached` or a mode, or it starts a Daemon. Each command that runs the engine has one rstest case per mode, `#[case::attached(Mode::Attached)]` and `#[case::daemon(Mode::Daemon)]`, snapshotted under one name (`assert_cli_snapshot!("config_explain_json", checked.output)`, or one inline snapshot); the engine's dispatch records, which an Attached engine writes on the command's stderr and a Daemon in its own log, are left out of what both snapshot.
  - `pty(&env, executable)`, with the same `.arg`, `.args` and `.env`, runs the executable on a pseudo-terminal under expectrl (ConPTY on Windows) with the test's map, `COLUMNS=100`, `TERM=xterm-256color` and no `NO_COLOR`. `.run()` gives a `PtyOutput` (`exit`, `text`: the one transcript, `\r\n` line ends and escape sequences included); `.spawn()` gives a `PtyRun` whose `expect(text)` waits for a prompt, failing with `PtyError::NotShown` when the executable ends or 60 seconds pass first, whose `send_line(answer)` types the answer, and whose `finish()` reads to the end. `PtyOutput::stripped()` drops the escape sequences and `\r`; `has_colour()` finds an SGR sequence other than a reset. A pseudo-terminal run never refuses an escape byte, and records its command as `cli` does.
  - The colour properties of [Accessibility](#accessibility-localization-and-visual-regression) are `ColourProperty` cases any command can run: `ColourProperty::StrippedColourEqualsNoColor.check(&env, executable, &args)` fails with `ColourError::ColourCarriesInformation`, `EmptyNoColorKeepsColour` with `ColourError::EmptyNoColorTurnsColourOff`, and `ColorAlwaysOverridesNoColor` (which adds `--color always` after the arguments) with `ColourError::ColorAlwaysIgnored`. Write one rstest named case per property.
  - The locale cases: `cli(&env, executable).with_locale("tr-TR").with_time_zone("Asia/Kathmandu")` sets `LANG=tr_TR.UTF-8` and `TZ` in that child's map only; a value outside the shared table fails the run, before it starts, with `CliError::LocaleUnknown` or `CliError::TimeZoneUnknown`.
  - Each run appends `{"target": "goodfetch::test::command", "fields": {"command": …, "exit_code": …}}` to the test's command record, `commands.jsonl` in its folder, which teardown copies into its exercise record ([Feature coverage](#feature-coverage)). The command is the longest run of the arguments whose names, joined by dots, are a `command:` Feature of `features/inventory.json` (`browser install --json` is `browser.install`); arguments that name none are recorded as `unmatched`, which the exercise audit reports. A test that starts the executable itself, such as an Attached engine's, calls `record_command(&env, &["attached-engine"], status.code())` once it exited; a handle kept beyond a borrow of its `TestEnv`, such as `daemon run`'s foreground Daemon across a Journey's steps, keeps the test's folder and calls `record_command_in(&folder, …)`.
- **Every `--json` test validates stdout against the command's schema** from the bundle, checks that stdout holds nothing else, and snapshots it, through `cli(…).json(type)` or `.json_lines(event type)`.
  _Why:_ `--json` is under semver (ADR 0030), and only a schema puts it in the breakage check.
- **Warnings, hints, deprecations and the age notice never reach stdout.**
- **Every exit code in `goodfetch-protocol`'s table has a test through the binary**, one rstest named case per exit code, in `tests/it`. An in-process unit test checks that the table maps every Error code. A case that needs an Error code the engine cannot be made to raise plays the corpus's `daemon/cli-call-failed.json` with that code and its group's class in its reply, the Scripted engine run as a Daemon answering `config explain --daemon`.
- **Test a command both executables carry through Collie's.** Foxhound's `--help` snapshot and the containment checks of [Which Product's executable a test runs](#which-products-executable-a-test-runs) show it unchanged there.
- **Human output is snapshotted under `NO_COLOR=1`, a fixed `COLUMNS`, `TERM=dumb` and a fixed locale.** The `--help` tree is one snapshot rendered in-process from clap's `Command`; one `tests/it` case checks the binary prints the same. A unit test calls clap's `Command::debug_assert`.
- **Terminal-only behaviour is tested in a pseudo-terminal with expectrl,** through `pty`: colour, progress, and person-only prompts, which must refuse when stdin is not a terminal.
- **Interrupts are tested through the binary:** a running Crawl sent `SIGINT` (Unix) or `CTRL_BREAK_EVENT` (Windows) checkpoints and exits with its exit code; a second interrupt ends the command at once, exit `130`, while on a Daemon the Run still stops with its Checkpoint through the first interrupt's `run.stop` (ADR 0069's #1180 note): its test plays the Daemon on the Scripted engine, which holds that `run.stop` unanswered, sends the second interrupt once it is in flight, and checks that the command exits `130` within 10 seconds. Test support's `Process::interrupt()` sends a spawned executable `SIGINT` (Unix), and `Process::terminate()` `SIGTERM`, which exits `143`. A cancellable command's interrupt shows in the engine's dispatch records as `cancel received`, before its call's `call_cancelled`.
- **A command that runs the engine is tested in both modes**, as rstest cases `attached` and `daemon` through `cli(…).mode(mode)`, against one snapshot.
- **Daemon log cases** ([ADR 0122](docs/adr/0122-a-run-stops-resumably-before-its-disk-fills-and-a-daemons-logs-rotate-at-a-bounded-size.md)): `daemon run` writes JSON lines to stdout and no log file; a Daemon started by `daemon start` writes them to the Goodfetch home's log folder, rotating at `log.file.max_size` and keeping `log.file.max_files`, so a test that sets both small and logs past them finds that many files, each at most the size plus one line; a crawl of 1,000 requests at the default level writes no line per request; on a full filesystem the Daemon keeps serving, and once space returns the next line says how many were dropped; `log.output: stdout` on an Attached engine fails with `setting_not_applicable`, and with `log.output: file` it writes the JSON lines ADR 0072's exercise audit reads into the test's Goodfetch home.
  _Why:_ they are the pitfall catalog's row O10, debug-level logs and files that never rotate on long runs (ADR 0122).
- **Client faults** (a refused protocol version, a dead PID, a dropped connection) come from the Scripted engine run as a Daemon.
- **What only Foxhound carries is refused through Collie's executable,** table-driven from the generated list of Foxhound's names: each operation over the protocol, each setting key as a `settings` value, each CLI command with its exit code, enabling each Plugin kind and Hook stage, and each value Foxhound adds to a type both Products share (a Limit scope, a Usage kind, a Notification event) where a program or a Recipe sets it answer `not_in_product`, with one message snapshot per kind. A configuration file holding a Foxhound key or such a value loads, and `config explain` lists it as Foxhound's (ADRs 0087 and 0095).
- **A Recipe is held to the Product its manifest names, on both engines** (ADR 0096). `init` writes the Product of the executable that ran it. Collie refuses a Recipe that names Foxhound with `not_in_product` before its run starts. A Foxhound-only operation, Plugin kind or Hook stage from a Recipe that names Collie answers `not_in_product` on both engines, its `fix` saying to name Foxhound in the manifest, and on a person's own Collie engine also to run it with Foxhound. On Foxhound's engine, the same call on that run from a Client answers the same, the Client's connection and other calls still succeed, and a crawl setting key on that run answers `setting_not_applicable`.

### Where they run

| Suite | SDKs | CLI |
|---|---|---|
| Quick | unit tests, type tests, completeness and property tests, script validation, the case-list check | in-process unit tests, `--help` rendered in-process, `debug_assert`, the exit-code table maps every Error code |
| Pre-merge | touched SDKs on Linux with the newest Node LTS and CPython: integration, Client conformance, Scripted engine process, one installed-package run | every `tests/it` case, both modes: exit codes and `--help` through the binary, `--json`, pseudo-terminal, interrupts |
| Release | every Node line, Bun, Deno and CPython on Linux x64, free-threaded builds on every Linux platform; oldest and newest Node and CPython on every other platform; every install path on every OS. Free-threaded builds on macOS and Windows: a nightly-only job | everything, on every platform |

## MCP, framework adapters and Integrations

Decided in [ADR 0070](docs/adr/0070-mcp-adapters-and-integrations-are-tested-through-their-platforms-own-clients-against-a-real-engine.md).

### Every surface

- **Drive each surface with its platform's own client, against a real engine or Daemon serving the local Proving ground.** No mocking library or hand-written fake of Goodfetch's API: no `nock`, fake HTTP API or stub listener.
  _Why:_ a fake of the engine passes where the real one fails, and misses the token, `Host` and `Origin` checks.
- **For Goodfetch's side, use only the doubles ADRs 0062 and 0069 define:** the in-memory adapter of the protocol in Quick-suite unit tests, and the Scripted engine for faults a real engine can't produce on demand.
- **Stand in for the platform in this order:** its real runtime run locally, its official test harness, a written adapter of its context.
- **Keep a Quick check over everything `xtask codegen` generates:** no person-only operation or setting, and no `page.lend` ([ADR 0089](docs/adr/0089-a-page-is-lent-through-an-endpoint-that-shows-only-its-session-and-collie-joins-other-crawlers-by-borrowing.md)), on any MCP profile, Framework adapter, Integration or skill, and each surface's operations map to catalog operations of the same class. No surface of Collie's carries an entry marked Foxhound (ADR 0095); that Foxhound's carry every entry of Collie's under the same name is the containment check of [Which Product's executable a test runs](#which-products-executable-a-test-runs). The same check lists the public items of `goodfetch-collie-client` and `goodfetch-collie` and refuses any of Foxhound's.
- **Assert outcomes through the oracle and Error codes**, never a model's wording or a platform's message text.

### MCP server

- **Test the server with `rmcp`'s client:** in-memory in its crate, over stdio and Streamable HTTP in the binary's `tests/it`. MCP Journey variants run there too, on nextest.
- **Read stdio stdout with the strict framing reader**, `StrictStdout` ([Shared helpers](#shared-helpers)), logging at `trace`. Any line that is not a JSON-RPC message fails the test.
- **Test two Streamable HTTP sessions at once:** each keeps its own Limits and its own cancellation.
- **Run the conformance suite at its pinned version, once per supported revision**, with `--expected-failures conformance/mcp/expected-failures/<revision>.yaml`:
  - `--requirements <revision>` for a revision with a frozen requirement set (today `2025-11-25` and `2026-07-28`), `--spec-version <revision>` for an older one;
  - against the shipped binary, whose expected-failures file lists only scenarios that need fixture tools or resources, each with a reason (a Quick check refuses other entries);
  - against the fixture server, with an empty expected-failures file;
  - once more for the extensions the server implements, each scenario by name with `--scenario` and without `--spec-version`, against `conformance/mcp/expected-failures/extensions.yaml`, since `--requirements` doesn't score extensions. These run at `2026-07-28` only; test an extension the server supports under an older revision with `rmcp` in `tests/it`.
- **Change the suite's version only in a pull request that updates the expected-failures files.**
- **Run the pinned Inspector CLI with `--format json --stored-auth-only`:** boot smoke of every packaged artifact over stdio and Streamable HTTP (the OCI image on Linux only), `--strict` on every profile's `tools/list`, and `--verify --require-digests` on skills once the server serves them.
- **Cover every supported revision in the client matrix:** TypeScript SDK v1 and v2 clients, the Python SDK and `rmcp`, pinned, over stdio and Streamable HTTP, plus the negotiation cases.
- **Add a revision to the supported list only with its expected-failures file and its matrix cells, in the same pull request.**

### Framework adapters

- **Drive each tool through the framework's own agent loop with its own scripted model**, never by calling the tool's `execute` alone: `MockLanguageModelV4` for the Vercel AI SDK and Mastra, `FakeListChatModel` or `FakeStreamingChatModel` for LangChain.js, `GenericFakeChatModel` through test support's `bind_tools` subclass for Python LangChain.
- **Script a read, an Effect and a failing call** in each adapter's tests.
- **Run `ToolsUnitTests` on every generated Python LangChain tool**, and `ToolsIntegrationTests` on one tool per operation class and every tool a Journey uses, from a pinned `langchain-tests`.
- **Test the lowest and highest version of each declared peer range, and one of each major inside it**, in the Release suite. Pin the lowest in a test package (pnpm's `lowest-direct` never lowers peers); use uv's `--resolution lowest-direct` in Python.
- **Give each framework that connects only through MCP a model-free test** of its docs example: its own MCP client connects, lists tools and calls one tool.
- **Check Dataset loaders against the oracle's records**, including metadata and lazy streaming.

### Integrations

- **Start the Daemon through `goodfetch-test-env`** with its HTTP listener, a token and port 0.
- **Cover, for each Integration:** starting a run and a Recipe, fetching Records, one trigger per record range, no repeated trigger after a resumed run, a refused token, a refused `hello` version, and every Error code it maps.
- **Produce the refused `hello` and the Error codes the Proving ground can't cause with the Scripted engine run as a Daemon with an HTTP listener**, one script per case; test every other case against the real Daemon.
- **Run each platform's offline validator in the Quick suite**, and the Integration in its platform's local runtime or official harness in the Pre-merge suite:

| Integration | Quick | Pre-merge |
|---|---|---|
| n8n | `n8n-node lint` (strict); verification rules | pinned n8n image, `n8n execute --id` |
| Zapier | `zapier-platform validate --without-style` | `createAppTester`, real HTTP |
| Make | Make Apps Editor JSON schemas; module-to-operation map | none (Live check) |
| Pipedream | Pipedream ESLint rules, build, key checks | `run` with the `$` adapter |
| Activepieces | lint, build | `createMockActionContext`; pinned Activepieces image |
| Power Automate | `ConnectorPackageValidator.ps1`; OpenAPI 2.0 validation | every operation through an OpenAPI 2.0 client |
| Airbyte source | offline QA checks; manifest validation | CDK standard suite; incremental reads from saved state |

### Journey variants

- **Run `adapter-<name>` and `integration-<name>` variants in the package's own runner, on Linux:** Vitest in `tests/journeys/J-NNN.test.ts` for TypeScript adapters and the n8n, Zapier, Pipedream, Activepieces and Power Automate Integrations; pytest in `tests/journeys/test_jNNN.py` for Python adapters, the LlamaIndex reader and the Airbyte source. Make has no automated variant.
- **Run `http-listener` variants on nextest** in the binary crate's `tests/it/journeys`, against a Daemon's HTTP listener with a token and port 0.

### Live checks and manual tests

- **Run unpinned releases only as Live checks:** the newest conformance suite, Inspector and MCP SDK clients, and each framework's `latest` and prerelease tags, nightly; the n8n scanner after each publish.
- **Keep real platform accounts out of every suite.** They run weekly and at release candidates, on free developer plans only, through a Cloudflare Quick Tunnel (`cloudflared tunnel --url`, a new `trycloudflare.com` URL each run, no account or domain), and only once the owner gives the go-ahead at M4, because the job exposes a Daemon (ADR 0076); a repository variable keeps the job off until then. Paid accounts are declined while there is no revenue.
  - **Re-point the platforms to each run's URL before its cases:** the Power Automate connector's host, and Make's base address wherever the app fixes one, change every run with the Quick Tunnel's address.
  - **The job's ADR 0043 endpoints are the tunnel's** as well as each platform's API host: `api.trycloudflare.com:443`, which creates the Quick Tunnel; the run's `*.trycloudflare.com:443`; and Cloudflare's tunnel hosts `region1.v2.argotunnel.com:7844` and `region2.v2.argotunnel.com:7844`, over TCP and UDP. `cloudflared` runs with `--no-autoupdate`, so it needs no update host. ADR 0070's named Cloudflare Tunnel on a `goodfetch.dev` subdomain is declined (ADR 0076).
- **Test by hand at each release candidate that changes them:** Claude Desktop, ChatGPT, VS Code and Cursor connecting to the MCP server; `paconn validate`; each platform's listing submission.

## Performance budgets

Decided in [ADR 0071](docs/adr/0071-performance-budgets-are-counted-per-change-and-time-is-measured-on-the-reference-host.md) and [ADR 0134](docs/adr/0134-absolute-ceilings-and-performance-targets-join-the-per-change-budget-and-m1-ends-with-an-optimization-pass.md).

### What gates a change

- **Only counted figures fail a change:** instructions and heap metrics per benchmark, protocol and render counts per scenario, and bundle bytes. No check on a GitHub-hosted runner compares a duration with a threshold.
  _Why:_ shared runners vary by about 2.7% in wall time, and instruction counts of one binary on one machine repeat exactly.
- **No test asserts elapsed time, a rate or a duration.** Time is measured only by the Performance series and the benchmark, both Live checks.
- **Until M1's optimization pass, the counted figures inform and fail nothing,** a dated waiver of the [Quality register](#exceptions) (X20) ([ADR 0134](docs/adr/0134-absolute-ceilings-and-performance-targets-join-the-per-change-budget-and-m1-ends-with-an-optimization-pass.md), as amended on 2026-10-22). `xtask perf` lists each figure past its threshold or over its ceiling, with its change or value, as a `note[performance-change]` or `note[over-ceiling]` line in the gate's log, and no pull request needs a "Performance change" section or acceptance comment. A broken measurement still fails: a benchmark that fails its admission, or a ceiling whose figure the head lost. The switch is the constant `GATING` in `xtask/src/perf/budget.rs`; the optimization pass's pull request sets it back to `Enforced`, and the acceptance and ceiling rules below gate from then on.

### Benchmarks

- **Benchmarks live in the crate's `benches/`:** `scenarios.rs` holds the scenario functions, `instructions.rs` runs them under gungraun's Callgrind with mimalloc as the global allocator, `heap.rs` under gungraun's DHAT with the system allocator, and `wall.rs` under criterion for the Performance series. Scenarios that cross crates (startup, a protocol round trip) live in the binary crate.
  _Why:_ DHAT counts allocations by replacing `malloc`, which mimalloc never calls.
- **A benchmark measures one pinned scenario:** inputs from a literal, a fixture or a seed; no network, no sleep, no work whose amount depends on timing.
- **A bench target builds what it needs the way tests do:** a paused current-thread runtime through the helper tests use, `Clock::paused` and `Entropy::seeded` from the start time and seed `goodfetch-test-support` supplies (it supplies the values, never the handles), and the `tasks` module's inline blocking policy, so no thread waits on another. Its root opts in once with `#![expect(clippy::disallowed_methods, reason = "benchmark harness, ADR 0071")]`.
- **A scenario that times a crate's own private code compiles its pure modules in through `#[path]`,** each module that does no I/O and holds no task, such as the page crate's guard check, its guard world's call and the interception's steps; no `pub` item is added for a benchmark, as none is for a test (ADR 0071's note of 2026-10-24). The one exception is the fetch tiers' test seam, `FetchTiers::serving`, since the crawl path no pure module carries crosses several crates (ADR 0134's note of 2026-10-27).
  _Why:_ the scenario then builds the very command the engine sends, and the crate's interface stays what its callers use.
- **A new or changed benchmark runs 20 times on one build in its pull request.** Every gated figure must spread less than 0.5%, or the benchmark is fixed before it gates.
- **Changing what a benchmark measures gives it a new ID.** A benchmark with no base is reported, never gated.
- **Benchmarks build with the `bench` profile** (release settings, thin LTO, `debug = "line-tables-only"`) on the Linux gnu targets. Valgrind and gungraun's runner come with the pinned CI image on Linux x86-64, the only platform ADR 0135's image has; the Linux arm64 Release cells install them on their runner (`ci/host/perf-tools.sh`).
- **The `perf` job is one job while every selected bench target fits it;** it becomes a matrix over `bench-targets`, sharded by bench target within the Pre-merge budget, once they do not.

### The comparison with the base

- **`xtask perf` measures the base and the head in the same job, on the same runner:** on a pull request, the merge commit against its first parent, the base branch's head, so the head adds exactly the pull request's change; the group's base in the merge queue. Never a stored figure from another machine.
- **The gate trips at +5%** on instructions, total allocations, total bytes or bytes at the global maximum of any benchmark, and at **+5 kB brotli** on any size-limit entry.
- **Once the budget gates again, a tripped pull request passes only with a "Performance change" section** in its body, listing each tripped figure with its measured change and reason, accepted explicitly by the owner when merging ([ADR 0133](docs/adr/0133-agent-work-follows-the-matt-pocock-skills-as-written-and-the-fresh-review-is-retired.md)).
  - Each line names the figure as the table does, with a change at least the measured one: `- goodfetch-frontier/instructions::admission::admit/one_heavy_domain_and_a_thousand_small instructions: +5.10%, <reason>`, or `- size-limit/<entry> brotli: +6.20 kB, <reason>`.
  - The owner accepts with a comment saying "Performance change accepted" and naming the head SHA, then re-runs the `perf` job with `gh run rerun <run-id> --job <job-id>`. Its steps are not test steps and its figures repeat exactly, so the re-run measures the same change and is no retry: nothing flaky is masked ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md), as amended on 2026-10-19). The same holds for `size` where it gates. "all green" still refuses a run whose earlier attempt failed a `Test` step.
  - No section passes a ceiling: each Product's whole Console build is the size-limit entry `collie-console` or `foxhound-console` of `.size-limit.json`, held under 1 MB brotli, and each one's initial route the entry `collie-console-initial-route` or `foxhound-console-initial-route`. size-limit reads the brotli files `xtask console build` writes as they are (`"brotli": false`), so an entry counts the bytes the executable embeds; `xtask perf build` builds the Console of the head and of the base, each from its own packages.
- **Run `just perf` before pushing a change to code a benchmark covers.** It uses CI's thresholds; off Linux it runs in the CI image through a container engine, or lists itself as skipped.
- **The figures reach the results store** as `perf-figures.json` in the job's JUnit artifact, beside gungraun's and size-limit's reports. The daily test-health job's drift check reads them from there: `main`'s latest per platform against the last release's.

### Absolute ceilings

- **`xtask perf` also checks absolute ceilings,** in the same job as the comparison with the base. A "Performance change" section never passes a change over one; only the owner moves a ceiling. Until M1's optimization pass, a figure over one is noted and fails nothing ([What gates a change](#what-gates-a-change)).
- **The fixed ceilings:** at most **8 heap bytes per URL in the Frontier** at 100,000 URLs (DHAT's bytes at the global maximum, less the same scenario with no URL, over the URL count), and at most **twice its body's bytes allocated per crawled page**, in a crawl fed by an in-memory fixture (DHAT's total bytes of the crawl over the total body bytes its fixture records as `body-bytes` in its `.source.toml`). Each Console build's 1 MB brotli is a ceiling too.
- **The crawl fed by an in-memory fixture is `goodfetch-foxhound`'s `heap::crawl::in_memory_pages`:** a code-free crawl, on an engine composed as the crawl's tests compose it, of an index and twelve pages of about 1 kB to 1 MB, served from memory through the fetch tiers' test seam, `FetchTiers::serving`, which sends every Run's HTTP hops with the transport it is given. Each page goes through admission, the fetch, parsing, link discovery (`follow: scope`) and extraction to a Record. Its bodies are borrowed from a static fixture, so serving them allocates nothing, and DHAT counts the whole process, as the Frontier's benchmark does. The integration test `in_memory_crawl` runs the same scenario and checks that the site sends exactly the `body-bytes` its `benches/pages.source.toml` records.
- **The tuned ceilings:** instructions, allocations and bytes allocated per dispatched JSON-RPC message, each at 1.10 times its figure after M1's optimization pass, approved by the owner.
- **The ceilings are constants of `xtask perf`** that cite ADR 0134, like the thresholds, each keyed by the benchmark IDs or size-limit entries whose figures it reads.
- **A ceiling starts when its benchmark first runs, and never lapses:** once the base or the head measures a benchmark a ceiling reads, the head must have every figure the ceiling reads, so renaming or removing a benchmark fails rather than dropping its ceiling.
- **The summary lists every ceiling beside the comparison table:** its head figure, derived ones shown rounded up, and its verdict. One whose benchmarks neither side measured is listed as not run yet. The check itself compares exact counts.

### The fixed reference build

- **`xtask perf` also holds every counted figure to at most 1.10 times a pinned reference build** (`drift.reference`, [ADR 0140](docs/adr/0140-every-shared-per-unit-path-has-one-owner-module-a-cost-contract-of-counted-work-and-a-benchmark.md)), so that steps under +5 % cannot add up unseen. `ci/perf-reference.toml` names the build's commit or tag: #1577's tuned build, then each release tag.
- **It is measured in the same job, on the same runner,** in a worktree and target folder of its own: the selected bench targets it has, and size-limit when it has a `.size-limit.json`. Moving the pin selects every bench target.
- **A figure over 1.10 times its reference fails, naming it,** whatever `GATING` says and whatever a pull request's "Performance change" section accepts: only an ADR amendment the owner approves passes it. A figure the reference does not hold is listed as new since it. A reference holding none of the head's figures compared nothing, and fails.
- **With no revision pinned, the check reports that it has not run yet.** A missing `ci/perf-reference.toml` fails `xtask perf build`: the file is the gate's switch.

### Performance targets

- **A Performance target is measured on the owner's machine and never fails a change.** Startup to `hello` at most 10 ms (median); an idle Attached engine at most 8 MiB and an idle Daemon at most 16 MiB of resident memory, for each Product; the 1,000-page HTTP crawl at most 32 MiB at its peak, and at least as many pages per second as Spider in the same `just compare-spider` run.
- **`just targets` writes the targets report:** each target, its measured figure and, for a miss, the gap with the optimization pass's write-up from `ci/performance-target-gaps.toml`, in `target/targets/report.md`. It measures each Product's release build: startup to `hello` with hyperfine (10 warm-up runs, then the median of 100), the peaks of the idle Attached engine, the idle Daemon and the 1,000-page crawl with the resident-memory check's scenarios (median of 5 runs each), and takes the crawl's pages per second from the latest `just compare-spider` report. A figure with no measurement yet is listed as not measured. Each milestone's owner test links it under Reports, with `just compare-spider`'s report, labelled `local-controlled` and never published (ADR 0076).
- **`just compare-spider` counts a figure only after its same-work check.** Foxhound and Spider (pinned, its HTTP mode) each crawl the Proving ground's 1,000-page site, `scale.test/sites/1000/1`, in 10 trials interleaved in random order, each tool in a fresh process against a fresh Proving ground, at 16 requests at once; every trial must have fetched the site's own pages and been served the requests and bytes most trials were, as the Proving ground's observations record them, or it fails naming each trial that did other work and writes no figure. It reports each tool's median pages per second and median peak RSS in `target/compare-spider/report.md`.
- **Its Callgrind cell gives `foxhound.spider_instruction_ratio` under the same check** ([ADR 0140](docs/adr/0140-every-shared-per-unit-path-has-one-owner-module-a-cost-contract-of-counted-work-and-a-benchmark.md)). Each tool crawls the same site under Callgrind 3 times, each in a fresh process against a fresh Proving ground, Foxhound on scenario 1 of [ADR 0138](docs/adr/0138-a-page-is-analysed-once-costly-features-are-settings-and-run-state-commits-in-groups-at-a-chosen-durability-level.md)'s matrix. The runs are held to the same-work check among themselves, a run counting no instruction fails, and the ratio is the median of Foxhound's instructions per page over the median of Spider's, to three decimals. A host without Valgrind reports the cell as not run, with why.
- **A target is not a test.** No test asserts it, and `just compare-spider` is not a Live check.

### Counts as snapshots

- **Protocol counts are snapshots:** for each named scenario of a surface, the requests sent, the answers and events received, and their bytes after the shared redactions. Rust against the real binary in `tests/it`; TypeScript and Python against the Scripted engine of ADR 0069 in unit tests.
- **MCP scenarios snapshot the bytes of `tools/list` and of each tool result.**
  _Why:_ for an agent, bytes are context.
- **The Python SDK snapshots the modules `import goodfetch` loads.**
- **Console render budgets are snapshots in component tests:** React commits per action inside `<Profiler>`, and DOM elements rendered for a 10,000-row list. Never render durations.
- **A changed count is reviewed as a change of expected behaviour**, like every snapshot.

### Ceilings

- **Each Product's embedded Console stays under 1 MB brotli,** and its initial route under the ceiling area 12 sets, 1.25 times its first build's measure. Collie's is the page, its entry script and its stylesheet, under 139,779 bytes brotli, 1.25 times 111,823 (#937). Foxhound's adds Foxhound's English catalog, which its entry awaits before the first render, under 161,174 bytes, 1.25 times 128,939 (#1235). Fonts and the pseudo-locales' catalogs load after the first render. Only the owner moves a ceiling.
- **Resident memory stays under its ceiling per OS** for an Attached engine idle after `hello`, an idle Daemon, and a 1,000-page HTTP crawl of the local Proving ground. The check runs in the Release suite.
- **`just rss [scenario…]` runs the check on this host:** each scenario on each executable it runs on (`idle-attached-engine` and `idle-daemon` on both Products, `crawl` on Foxhound), each peak against its ceiling in `ci/rss-ceilings.toml` and its Performance target. Until the owner commits that file, it reports and gates nothing. `just rss-propose` proposes the file's lines from 20 runs.
- **Each Product's executable has its own startup benchmarks and its own ceilings** for an idle Attached engine and an idle Daemon; the 1,000-page crawl runs on Foxhound's.
- **The Console's JS heap after 10,000 streamed events,** after a forced garbage collection, is within 10% of its value after 1,000.
- **Resident-memory ceilings are measured on the tuned build,** after M1's optimization pass. A ceiling may sit above its target.

### Memory at scale

- **The Frontier at scale:** one process enqueues 100,000 synthetic URLs, then 1,000,000, with no network. Peak resident memory at 1,000,000 is at most 1.5 times its peak at 100,000. It is `goodfetch-frontier`'s `at_scale` test: the Frontier's admission in the test's own process, against a run file on disk opened as a crawl's is, read with `goodfetch-test-support`'s `peak_resident_bytes`; outside the Release suite, on 10,000 and then 100,000 URLs. It also checks the duplicate check is exact: every distinct URL added, a visited one a `duplicate`.
- **The Daemon soak:** one Daemon runs 20 consecutive 1,000-page Proving ground crawls. Its resident memory after the 20th is within 10% of its value after the 3rd. If the 20 crawls take more than about two minutes, lower the pages per crawl, never the crawl count. It is `goodfetch-foxhound`'s `soak` test: 40 code-free HTTP crawls of 300 of `scale.test`'s items each, up to 8 in flight, on one Daemon of Foxhound's executable, its Runs' stores on a memory file system; a least-squares line through the Daemon's live heap after each of crawls 26 to 40, once it is warm, climbs at most 32 KiB a crawl, a bound over thirteen times clean runs' measured climb and under a third of a planted leak of 110 KB a Run's (#1734, ADR 0134's note). The live heap is `daemon.status`'s `heap`: the bytes the Daemon's allocations hold, as its executable's global allocator, mimalloc wrapped in `goodfetch_engine::heap::Counting`, counts them, without the pages mimalloc keeps once freed, whose swings failed the private-memory checks this replaced on clean runs. The Daemon's private resident memory, read with `goodfetch-test-support`'s `private_resident_bytes_of` (`RssAnon` on Linux, the physical footprint on macOS, the private working set on Windows), is printed beside the heap when the check fails; the same in every suite.
- **An engine ending Run after Run keeps nothing of each:** `goodfetch-engine`'s `heap` test runs one engine in its own process over the in-memory network, on the paused runtime with its blocking work inline, so each Run allocates the same; the test binary's global allocator is the executables' `Counting`, around the system's. It sets the engine's window of kept lossless events to 16, so the window is full within the 40 Runs of its warm-up (the Daemon's 4,096 fill after about 2,000 Runs, past the soak's reach), then ends 100 more bare Runs, and a least-squares line through the live heap after each climbs at most 16 bytes a Run (#1768, ADR 0134's note).
- **The Daemon soak pins the resource governor's memory allowance far above the expected peak,** so it does not measure load shedding (ADR 0040); the Frontier at scale starts no engine, so no governor runs.
  _Why:_ a ratio gives one verdict on any machine, as the Console's leak check does, and both fit the five-minute cap per test.

### Profiling

- **CPU: samply,** on macOS and Linux (`samply setup` once on macOS).
- **Heap: gungraun's DHAT and heaptrack, on Linux,** with the gnu build and the system allocator `benches/heap.rs` uses; on the owner's machine, in the Lima VM. Instruments' Allocations on macOS when a question needs it.
- **Async: the ops listener's metrics and Tokio's stable runtime metrics,** through `tracing`. tokio-console is not used: it needs `tokio_unstable`, its own thread and its own TCP listener (ADR 0060).

### Where they run

| Check | Suite |
|---|---|
| Count snapshots in unit tests | Quick |
| Count snapshots against the binary, render and DOM counts, the Console leak check, the Python import set | Pre-merge (Linux x64); Release on every cell |
| `xtask perf` against the base, and benchmark admission | Pre-merge (Linux x64); Release adds Linux arm64 |
| Resident-memory ceilings | Release, every OS |
| Absolute ceilings | with `xtask perf` |
| The Frontier at scale and the Daemon soak | Release, every OS |
| Performance targets and the Spider comparison | the owner's machine, before each owner test; never a suite |
| Drift check against the last release | the daily test-health job |
| Performance series | a nightly Live check on the owner's machine when it is on, labelled with that machine; the rented reference host is declined while there is no revenue (ADR 0076). The Console's lab metrics: `just console-lighthouse`, with `GOODFETCH_PERF_MACHINE` naming the machine, appends a record per Product's Console, Collie's and Foxhound's, each served by its own executable, to `target/perf-series/console-lighthouse.jsonl` |

## Quality register

Decided in [ADR 0140](docs/adr/0140-every-shared-per-unit-path-has-one-owner-module-a-cost-contract-of-counted-work-and-a-benchmark.md), in the format of the `system-qualities` skill's register. It is the project's quality bar: the Floor every diff keeps, the limits that fail the merge gate, the figures that only ratchet down and the Exceptions that expire. The project has no `CONSTRAINTS.md`. Read it before writing code on a Shared path (ADR 0140's inventory: F1 to F6, C1 to C5, E1 to E6). Change a limit only by the rules below: tighten it in the pull request that earns it, loosen it with the owner.

Until the gate's spec builds them, one thing this section names doesn't exist in full yet: the counters of counted work (`F1.parses` has its own; a row whose "Checked by" says none yet has none). Until then, `code-review`'s Standards axis checks the Shared paths' contracts as a judgement, and each row of counted work stays in the Measured table. `just floor-guard` checks the Floor on every merge (`xtask floor-guard`, in `just merge`'s gate); it reads lines, not meaning, so `code-review` still checks the Floor too.

**`just counts`** (`xtask counts`, ADR 0140) is the "Checked by" of every counted row: it runs each row's benchmark with its default features on and once per switchable feature off, divides each count by the units of the outcome that did the work, reads the row's limit (its Today figure, else its Contract) and fails over it, naming the row, its figure and its limit. `just counts --family B1`, `--path F1` or `--row F1.parses` runs fewer; `--plant <row>` has the benchmark do one extra unit of the row's work, and `--prove` does so for every row and fails on a counter that does not see it. `just counts --valgrind` does the same for the rows read under Valgrind, at the merge gate: each figure is its scenario less the run of its benchmark that sends nothing, per unit, within 0.5 % of its limit, and a row with `1.10 ×` in its Contract is held only to its Today figure until #1577 sets its ceiling. Its figures are per unit and whole: `F1.parses`'s 8 includes each parse X1 waives. Its Page analysis rows other than the parses count from the crawl's first page: what its start does once is no page's work. A row of a path whose code does not exist yet (`F5.*`, `F6.*`) starts at ADR 0140's value: the command prints its limit and "not run yet" and passes, and it fails, naming the row, as soon as a benchmark counts any of its work, so the ticket that builds the path moves the row to `xtask counts`'s `ROWS` and gives it a plant.

### Floor

Always enforced on the diff. A breach is a hard violation in `code-review`.

- **No new suppression comment,** except these forms, which never expire and always name what they suppress:
  - Rust: `#[expect(lint, reason = "…")]` ([`STYLE.md`](STYLE.md)), never `#[allow]`;
  - a test run as a child process by another test: `#[ignore = "run as a child by <test>"]`;
  - the SDKs' type tests: `@ts-expect-error` and `# type: ignore[code]` ([SDKs](#sdks));
  - Python: `# pyright: ignore[rule]`;
  - TypeScript: `oxlint-disable-next-line <rule> -- <reason>`. [Oxlint's documentation](https://oxc.rs/docs/guide/usage/linter/ignore-comments) gives the comment and its rules and no reason syntax; its parser takes what follows ` -- ` as the description, so the guard asks for that form.
- **No unimplemented stub, or empty catch, standing in for the work.**
- **No skipped or deleted test without a `Quality-change` trailer.** A test replaced at a deepened module's interface names its replacement.
- **No secret in source.**
- **No register row weakened outside the rules below.**
- **No edit to the gate's own files** (its benchmarks, the floor guard, the register check) without a `Quality-change` trailer. The guard reads them from this line, at the merge base; a new file of the gate adds to the bar and passes: `xtask/src/floor_guard.rs`, `xtask/src/floor_guard/`, `xtask/tests/it/floor_guard.rs`, `xtask/src/register.rs`, `xtask/src/register/`, `xtask/tests/it/register.rs`, `xtask/src/counts.rs`, `xtask/src/counts/`, `xtask/tests/it/counts.rs`, `crates/foxhound/goodfetch-foxhound/benches/`, `crates/goodfetch-page/benches/`, `crates/goodfetch-engine/benches/`.

_Why:_ the floor is diff-scoped, so the suppressions the code already holds (412 `#[expect]`, 28 `#[ignore]`, 51 `oxlint-disable` on 2026-11-01) fail nothing, while a new bare one does.

### Recorded changes

- **A change the Floor would flag but these rules allow carries a commit trailer:** `Quality-change: <file or row>: <reason>`. That covers a test removed with its replacement named, a row raised within its Cost contract, and an edit to the gate's own files.
- **The floor guard accepts a flagged change that a trailer names,** and `code-review` checks each trailer against the spec and ADR 0140. A file's trailer names its path, a row's its name (`F1.parses`, `X1`), and a change to a section of rules names that section (`Floor`, `Recorded changes`, `Rules`). The guard reads the trailers of every commit from the merge base to the head, and lists what each accepted as a `note[quality-change]` line.

### Enforced

Limits that fail the merge gate. Every row has a number and a command.

| Dimension | Rule | Checked by | Runs at |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rust lints and types | 0 warnings | `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| TypeScript types | 0 errors | `pnpm exec tsc -p tsconfig.json` and `pnpm -r --if-present run typecheck` (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| Python types | 0 errors | `uv run pyright` and `uv run mypy .`, both strict (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| TypeScript and Python lints | 0 errors | `pnpm exec oxlint` and `uv run ruff check .` (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| Secrets in commits | 0 findings | `just commit-leaks` | merge gate |
| Secret canaries' rule | catches every leaked artifact, passes the clean one | `ci/tool.sh gitleaks ci/canary-rule.sh` (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| Dependency advisories | 0 | `just audit` | merge gate |
| Dependency bans, licenses and sources | 0 | `cargo deny check … bans licenses sources` and `ci/deny/products.sh` (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| Workflow security | 0 findings | `zizmor` and `actionlint` (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| Generated code | current | `xtask codegen --check` (`just lint`) | merge gate |
| Feature declarations | inventory current, every declaration known | `xtask features check` (Quick) and `just features-tests` (Pre-merge), [Feature coverage](#feature-coverage) | merge gate |
| Quality register | no drift from ADR 0140 or `xtask perf`'s ceilings ([Rules](#rules)) | `xtask register check` (`just lint`) | merge gate |

### Measured, not yet enforced

The ratchet. A figure is compared with its "Today" value, never with the previous commit. Counted work has zero tolerance: one more count per unit is a regression. The gate's spec builds each row's command, which promotes it to Enforced (a tightening). Each row's Contract is ADR 0140's cap: no row may allow more, and a gap below it is an Exception. "After #1583" means the row's first figure is taken on its benchmark once the gate's spec builds it, after [#1583](https://github.com/goodfetch/goodfetch/pull/1583) merges.

Some tickets name a row by what it counts: Hook stages are `F5.*`, the Politeness path `E6.*`, Sink delivery `F6.*`, an Extractor Plugin `F1.plugin_*`, Live view reads `C2.live_view_commands`, closed shadow roots `C2.closed_root_commands`, cache hits `C5.cache_lookups` and `C5.cache_file_opens`, record mode `C5.body_reads`, the second GET `C5.charset_refetches`, and validations and serializations `F4.validations` and `F4.serializations`.

The benchmarks are ADR 0140's: B1 is `goodfetch-foxhound`'s in-memory crawl (`crawl::in_memory_pages`), B2 a `goodfetch-page` Session against a scripted browser on the in-memory CDP end, and B3 `goodfetch-engine`'s `dispatch` with large params, Audit entries, charges and governor samples. Each runs with its path's default features on and once with each feature that can be switched off turned off. The gate's spec names each benchmark's ID.

- **Rows measured until `GATING` turns on** ([ADR 0134](docs/adr/0134-absolute-ceilings-and-performance-targets-join-the-per-change-budget-and-m1-ends-with-an-optimization-pass.md)): ADR 0071's +5 % comparison with the base, and the absolute ceilings of [Performance budgets](#performance-budgets). `xtask perf` reports them as notes until #1577's pull request sets `GATING` to `Enforced`, under Exception X20, which ends then or on its date. The rows of counted work above do not wait for it: each is enforced from the pull request that builds its command.
- **Project coverage** is compared by ADR 0072's weekly coverage job, which opens an issue when it falls, as `xtask budgets` does; no merge fails on it. `xtask register coverage` adds up the job's three lcov reports into one line figure, and its 0.5 % is a share of the row's figure, as for every figure that varies. While the row has no figure, the job opens one issue naming the first; `--write`, run in a pull request on the run's `coverage-report`, writes it in the row, and writes a rise the same way. It never lowers the row.

**Context, not a Today value.** A count is compared only with the same benchmark on the same input, so the profile's figures stand beside the rows, never in them. Profile of 2026-10-30, on a Hetzner CCX43 at tip `0fd15a3a7`, crawling the Proving ground's 1,000-page site at 16 requests at once, code-free and in `fast` on tmpfs unless noted:

| Figure | Per page |
|---|---|
| HTML parses | 8 |
| `Matcher::new` calls | about 672 |
| statement preparations | 135.5 |
| run-state commits | 7.4–8.0 |
| WAL fsyncs, `full` on ext4 | 7.5 |
| files opened (`openat`) | 7.9, of which 4.1 fail |
| system calls | 329 (Spider: 12) |
| configuration files read, whole crawl | 650 |
| `/proc/meminfo` opened, whole crawl | 177 |

### Exceptions

Each known gap between today's code and ADR 0140's contracts, recorded at adoption on 2026-11-01. A gap the first measure finds is added in the gate spec's pull request with the same expiry; that is adoption, not a loosening. On expiry, the gate fails again with no action from anyone. The spec that removes each gap writes the row's measured figure in the same pull request.

| ID | Rule | Path | Reason | Owner | Expires | Removed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | `F1.parses`, `F1.selector_compilations`, `F1.readability_runs` | F1 | 8 parses per page; the clean-up compiles about 614 selectors twice per page even with no format (`goodfetch-extractor/src/formats.rs:131`, `goodfetch-clean-up-rules/src/removal.rs:95`); readability runs again for formats; the gate's use signals (`goodfetch-fetch-tier/src/gate.rs:389`), each redirect hop's refresh stub (`refresh.rs:62`) and a document tier's served calls (`tiers.rs:2163`, `tiers/calls.rs:416`) each parse the page again. It also covers #767, #788, #715, #760, #762 and #994 if they land before #1577: each keeps today's parse, which #1577 moves onto the Page analysis | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | #1577 |
| X2 | `F1.file_opens`, `E5.file_opens`, `E5.spawns` | F1, E5 | each Provenance and request-log entry creates its folder and opens its file (`goodfetch-engine/src/run_trace.rs:80`, `:126`) | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | #1577 for the page's entries; the Shared paths spec for the rest |
| X3 | `F1.bytes_allocated` | F1 | 234.45 bytes allocated per body byte against ADR 0134's 2 | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | #1577 |
| X4 | `F2.transactions`, `F2.syncs`, `F2.writer_reads` | F2 | about 8 commits and 7.5 fsyncs (`full`) per page; `RunState::read` goes through the writer (`goodfetch-engine/src/run_state.rs:510`, `:735`, `:814`) | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | #1577 |
| X5 | `F2.prepares`, `F2.configuration_reads` | F2, F3, F4 | 135 statement preparations per page; settings read again at each admission (`goodfetch-engine/src/settings.rs:253`) | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | #1577 |
| X6 | `F3.invariant_reads`, `F3.compilations`, `F3.prepares`, `F3.payload_serializations`, `F3.validations` | F3 | the Scope is read and its globs compiled per links batch (`goodfetch-crawl/src/enqueue/links.rs:460`, `goodfetch-frontier/src/scope.rs:385`); a validator is built per payload, and each payload is serialized only to measure it (`goodfetch-frontier/src/payload.rs:155`, `:328`); each redirect hop reads the Scope in a writer transaction (`goodfetch-fetch-tier/src/gate.rs:790`), and each sitemap read reads it again and compiles its rules (`goodfetch-crawl/src/work_items.rs:289`, `goodfetch-frontier/src/sitemap.rs:551`) | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | the Shared paths spec |

| X8 | `F4.deep_copies`, `F4.constant_lookups` | F4 | each Record's data is cloned and the Run's schema hash looked up per insert (`goodfetch-dataset/src/records.rs:93`), and the Clean-up rules' digest read again at each commit (`goodfetch-crawl/src/work_items.rs:794`); confirmed by B1's first figure | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | the Shared paths spec |

| X12 | `C4.detection_reads`, `C4.compilations`, `C4.provenance_entries` | C4 | 4 detection reads per navigation on B2's first measure: the Block markers' (`goodfetch-page/src/pages/blocked.rs:72`), the Page check's or the built-in detector's (`recovery.rs:266`), the Consent prompt runner's first pass (`consent.rs:212`) and its start in each document (`consent.rs:412`), which cannot join the others (ADR 0140, open question 3); the Page check compiles its globs per judgment (`recovery.rs:182`); a Consent prompt's outcome writes a Provenance entry of its own beside the built-in detector's (`consent.rs:534`) | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | the Shared paths spec |

| X20 | ADR 0071's +5 % comparison and ADR 0134's ceilings | all | `GATING` is `Informational` (ADR 0134): a waiver, which ends at #1577's merge or on this date, whichever comes first | Jean-Baptiste Manillier | 2027-01-30 | #1577 |

### Rules

- **"Runs at" names one of two moments:** implementer done (`tdd`) or the merge gate. The rows of counted work run at implementer done, at most 60 seconds per benchmark family; the rows read under Valgrind (instructions, allocations, bytes) run at the merge gate, through `xtask perf`.
- **Every enforced row has a number and a command.** A dimension with a number and no command stays in the Measured table until its command exists.
- **Counters only.** An enforced row counts counted work (ADR 0140), or instructions, allocations, bytes or system calls. Wall time and resident memory stay [Performance targets](#performance-targets), each figure with the setup it was measured on, and compared only with a figure from the same setup. Work is counted per unit of the outcome that does it, and each benchmark's input produces every outcome its path has.
- **A ceiling is the measured value, or the value an ADR sets,** never a round number above it. For work ADR 0140 plans and no code does yet, the row starts at the ADR's value. Raising a row up to its Cost contract, in the pull request whose spec names the added count, is not a loosening: the commit records it with a `Quality-change:` trailer. Above the contract, it is a loosening.
- **The ratchet.** A measured figure is compared with its recorded Today value. The pull request that improves a figure writes the new value in the same change. Counted work has zero tolerance. A figure that varies (coverage, instructions, bytes allocated) has a tolerance of 0.5 %. In parallel runs, only the merger writes a new Today value.
- **Coverage is a ratchet,** "must not fall", with no per-changed-line threshold; test quality, when measured, is ADR 0072's mutation score.
- **Tightening is silent; loosening is loud.** These need no approval:
  - lowering a number, adding a row or promoting a measured row to enforced;
  - extending a benchmark's input or counters to a new outcome or feature.

  These need the owner, and an amendment of ADR 0140 when they raise a Cost contract:
  - raising a number (outside the contract case above), or removing or demoting a row;
  - changing a row's command or Runs at;
  - narrowing a benchmark's input or what its counters see;
  - adding or extending an Exception.

  A loosening never rides in the pull request of the feature that failed the limit.
- **Exceptions expire.** Each has an owner and an expiry date, 90 days by default.
- **No gate is informational without an owner and an expiry.** A waiver names its figures, its owner and a date or a named merge, and the gate blocks again by itself when it ends (ADR 0134, as ADR 0140 amends it).
- **Drift has a fixed baseline.** No counted figure passes 1.10 times its pinned reference build, #1577's tuned build and then each release tag, without an amendment the owner approves (ADR 0071, as ADR 0140 amends it).
- **The register is the source of the numbers.** The gate's code reads the register, or the register check compares the two and fails on a difference, as for `xtask perf`'s ceilings.
- **The register check, `xtask register check`, runs with the merge gate through `just lint`.** It fails when:
  - an enforced row's command does not exist: each code span of its "Checked by" must be a recipe of the justfile, a command of `xtask`, a file of the repository, or text a recipe runs;
  - an Exception has expired;
  - a path of ADR 0140 has no row, or a count in a Cost contract has no row: a row holds a count when its Metric names every word of the count, the words after the count's number up to the first word that says where or when it applies;
  - a row allows more than its contract's cap, or its Today figure is over its Contract with no Exception naming it;
  - a ceiling of `xtask perf` differs from the row whose Benchmark names it, or either has no counterpart;
  - a table it reads is missing or empty.
- **Each counter proves it can fail.** A benchmark's own tests plant one extra unit of each counted work it gates and expect a failure.
- **A row changes in the pull request that lowers its figure, raises it within its contract as its spec names, or amends ADR 0140.** Nothing waits for a separate maintenance task.

## Security

Decided in [ADR 0067](docs/adr/0067-security-is-tested-per-trust-boundary-with-secret-canaries-and-private-fuzzing.md). The Quick, Pre-merge and Release suites are those of [Suites, commands and time budgets](#suites-commands-and-time-budgets): the Pre-merge suite runs on Linux, and the Release suite on the full matrix in the merge queue once it exists, and until then on manual dispatch before each milestone's owner test and before any release, on the private-period matrix of [Suites, commands and time budgets](#suites-commands-and-time-budgets). Live checks are those of [Flaky tests and Live checks](#live-checks), in no suite.

### Trust boundaries

Every boundary has its tests. A decision that adds a boundary adds its row in the same change. The OSes column is the whole schedule: a test absent from an OS its row does not give is not skipped.

| Boundary | Tests | Suite | OSes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Untrusted decoders | corpus and crasher replay | Quick | every OS |
| | `cargo fuzz check` | Pre-merge | Linux x64 |
| | fuzzing | Live check, private repository | Linux x64 |
| Script sandbox | worker probes and inspection | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS |
| | V8 memory-corruption scripts, on the test build fetched by digest | Release | Linux x64 |
| Plugin confinement | rule-to-probe check | Quick | every OS |
| | probes generated from each policy; engine refusals | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS |
| Chromium's sandbox | renderer checks; startup probe cases | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS |
| | `sandbox_linux_unittests` on both builds (ADR 0074) | Chromium build gate | Linux |
| | renderer check on the signed bytes | release pipeline | every OS |
| Egress | SSRF and hostile-input cases in-process | Quick | every OS |
| | the same cases end to end | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS, rebinding end to end on Linux only |
| Endpoints and the peer link | endpoint table; protocol fuzzing; MCP conformance over HTTP (placed by ADR 0070) | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS (MCP conformance: as ADR 0070) |
| | ZAP baseline of the Console | Release | Linux |
| A Session's proxy relay | relay unit tests in process against fake SOCKS5 and HTTP proxies: a client without the relay's credentials getting a `407` and no relayed byte, a wrong one in the Audit log, its endpoint table column, a tunnel and a plain request through SOCKS5, credentials refused per connection, the accept loop's backoff | Quick | every OS |
