| The egress relay (`goodfetch-page`'s `egress_relay`) | relay unit tests in process: a client without the Session's credentials refused, the Scripted resolver's rebinding cases, the upstream proxy path, the domain policy | Quick | every OS |
| | the relay on Chromium (Collie's `egress_relay` tests): no private address reached, another local process refused, an upstream proxy chained, the switch off, the ClientHello's JA4 unchanged | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS but Windows |
| | the terminating mode in process (`egress_relay`'s `terminate_tests`): the ClientHello mirrored upstream, an exception host passing through trust on first use, a changed pin stopping the host before any byte goes up, each certificate failure named, the authority's name constraints read by a verifier; the request-head and ClientHello fuzz targets replayed | Quick | every OS |
| | the terminating mode on Chromium (Collie's `certificate_exceptions` tests): pitfall H03 refused and passed under its exception, a changed pin, no Session cookie to an exception host, a refused tunnelled certificate named, no authority key or certificate on disk | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS but Windows |
| | the relay path end to end through the Proving ground's SOCKS5 proxy, behind the egress relay and with the browser as its client, its password searched as a Secret canary; the page suite's SOCKS5 relay case | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS but Windows |
| | the local-address relay on Chromium (Collie's `extra_loopback` tests): HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2 and a WebSocket reaching the site from the Session's address with the switch on and off, no UDP from WebRTC in the Proving ground's UDP record; the binding's lookup and connection from that address (the engine's `extra_loopback` tests); a local address refused on a Borrowed and a third-party CDP browser (Collie's `borrowed_browser` tests) | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS with the loopback aliases, but Windows |
| Secrets | redaction property tests | Quick | every OS |
| | Secret canary searches | the suite of the test that produces the output | its OSes |
| | `gitleaks` over job outputs | a step of every CI job | every OS |
| File outputs | path traversal cases | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS, each OS's own cases |
| Audit log | tamper property tests | Quick | every OS |
| Reset gate | ADR 0001's planted Secret canaries: `conformance/reset/cases.toml` on Collie's executable, in both layouts | Pre-merge; Release | Linux; every OS with the pinned browser build |
| Unsafe code | ASan run of crates that opt into `unsafe` | Release | Linux glibc |
| Supply chain and workflows | ADR 0032's gates; image digest pins; CodeQL once public; `gitleaks` over commits until then | Pre-merge | Linux |

**Before the repository is public** (ADR 0032), the Release suite, with every part above, runs on manual dispatch before each milestone's owner test and before any release, on the private-period matrix of [Suites, commands and time budgets](#suites-commands-and-time-budgets). CodeQL, GitHub secret scanning and push protection, and the nightly watch jobs start when the repository becomes public. Fuzzing runs on the owner's machine, using no included minutes ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)); paid minutes are declined while there is no revenue (ADR 0076).

### Fuzzing

- **Every decoder of input Goodfetch did not write itself has a `cargo-fuzz` target.** Today: JSON-RPC, plugin-protocol, peer-protocol and Script sandbox pipe framing; catalog-call arguments; CDP messages; HTML and Markdown; sitemaps, robots.txt and Site preference files; run-state import; Checkpoint and session-export bundles; Plugin manifests, OCI image indexes and bundle unpacking; Recipes and settings files; Secret references; certificate and key files; the Console's sign-in endpoints' inputs (the `Cookie` header, the `POST /sign-in` body and the CSRF token header); the Console's `POST` fallback (its stream path and its body of messages); Filter lists; Session source formats, `Cookies.binarycookies` included; extraction schemas; URL canonicalisation and the resolved-IP classification.
- **Write each target's body as a plain function**, and replay its seeds and every past crasher from a `#[test]` on every OS.
  _Why:_ cargo-fuzz does not build on Windows, and path handling differs most there.
- **Replay a target only with `goodfetch_test_support::fuzz_replay!(body, "fuzz/<target>")`**, once per target, in its own module of the crate's unit tests, so it runs in the Quick suite. It writes a `#[test]` that runs `body`, the target's `Fn(&[u8])`, over every file of `fuzz/<target>/seeds/` and then of `fuzz/<target>/crashers/` (relative to the crate's manifest folder), and fails naming each file the body panicked on; it prints each file as `goodfetch-fuzz-replay=<path>` before running it, so an input that aborts the process is named too. A missing or empty `crashers/` passes; a missing or empty `seeds/` fails. Commit each minimised crasher in `crashers/`.
- **Check more than crashes where a pair exists:** round-trip for encoders and decoders, and the resolved-IP classification against the reference table built from the IANA registries.
- **Commit seeds and a dictionary per target.** The grown corpus stays private.
- **Never fuzz in a pull request.** Fuzzing is a Live check that runs only in a private repository, against the main repository's `main`: nightly, ClusterFuzzLite builds the targets of `main`'s head and runs `batch` for 3,600 s, then `prune` and `coverage`. It runs on the owner's machine, in a Linux VM recreated for the job, through a runner instance of the private fuzzing repository that never runs a job at the same time as the main repository's ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)). Push fuzzing (`code-change` after every push) and its continuous builds do not run.
  _Why:_ a public job's logs and artifacts would disclose a crash already on `main` before its fix.
- **Keep fuzzing's results in the private repository.** They never go to the results store or its published report.
- **The job that builds and runs fuzz targets holds no secret** and a token with no permissions; a later job stores its results.
- **Run ClusterFuzzLite's images by digest** from Goodfetch's workflow, never through its action's `:v1` tag.
- **Send every new crash to a private security advisory draft** on the main repository, never a public issue. Until the main repository is public, which advisories need, open an issue in the private fuzz repository instead. A crash that also crashes the previous continuous build joins its advisory, or opens one. Fix it from the advisory's private fork (before publication, an ordinary pull request), and commit the crasher, minimised with `cargo fuzz tmin`, as a regression input with the fix.
- **An open fuzz crash blocks the promotion of the next minor** until it is fixed or triage closes it as not a vulnerability. Patches are not held. Never post a private result (the Private component run or fuzzing) on a public commit. The release pipeline reads them from the private repositories when each step starts, with the read-only private gates App: the Private component run for candidates and patches, and also open fuzz crashes for promotion to `x.y.0`. A promotion held while every public gate is green does reveal that a fuzz crash is open; this is accepted, since it shows neither the target nor the input (ADR 0067).
- **Fuzz, and run the ASan tests, on one nightly toolchain pinned in its own file**, apart from `rust-toolchain.toml`: [`ci/sanitizer-toolchain.toml`](ci/sanitizer-toolchain.toml), with the cargo-fuzz version. **`just fuzz-check`** runs `cargo fuzz check` of every target with it, in the Pre-merge suite's `supply-chain` job; a fuzz crate's `Cargo.lock` must be current.
- **Run the tests of every crate that opts into `unsafe` under AddressSanitizer** in the Release suite, on Linux glibc: **`just asan`**, the standard library rebuilt with the sanitizer. It runs the crate's unit tests and its integration tests in a module named after a module on the opted-in file's path; the others drive the crate's own sanitized executables and compare their output byte for byte.

### Confinement

- **Generate Plugin confinement cases from the policy.** One probe per rule of each generated seccomp, Landlock, Seatbelt, AppContainer and broker policy, at each network level. A rule without a probe, or a probe without a rule, fails.
- **Assert the exact error** ADR 0033 gives for a call the seccomp, Landlock or Seatbelt policy refuses: a kill or an allowed call fails the test. Resource-limit probes expect the limit's own Error code.
- **Pair every refusal with a positive control:** the same probe first succeeds unconfined, in the same test.
  _Why:_ a probe that fails for an unrelated reason would otherwise pass as a refusal.
- **Assert the mechanism each runner uses** (Landlock ABI, user namespaces, mount-namespace fallback), so a silent fallback fails. A container under Docker's default seccomp profile on a kernel before 6.12 must refuse with `confinement_unavailable`.
- **Test the Script sandbox worker as if V8 were broken:** run the probes under the worker's own generated policy, and inspect a live worker (`Seccomp:` and `NoNewPrivs:` on Linux, an AppContainer token on Windows, the spec's method on macOS). In the Release suite, on Linux x64, run corruption scripts on a V8 build with `v8_enable_memory_corruption_api`; each must end as `script_crashed`.
- **Build the test V8 once per V8 version**, in its own required check on the pull request that changes the version, outside the Pre-merge suite's "all green" check. The job skips when that version's build is already published. A later job attests it and publishes it as a release asset, as the Goodfetch Chromium is. The author commits its digest, which a check matches against the attested asset. A fork's pull request cannot attest, so a V8 change from a fork is rebuilt from a branch of the main repository. The Release suite fetches the build by digest before the outbound deny and never builds it.
- **Check that every renderer is sandboxed** for each browser Goodfetch launches: `Seccomp: 2` and `NoNewPrivs: 1` on Linux (Ubuntu 24.04 with its AppArmor restriction on), `chrome://sandbox` on Windows, Seatbelt on macOS. Test ADR 0049's startup probe in its refuse and record cases.

### Egress and hostile input

- **Keep SSRF cases in one table, as data, and run it at every layer:** the resolved-IP classification in-process, the HTTP tiers, the egress proxy, browser Sessions for every request type of ADR 0014, raw CDP, a Lent page whose client also tries a browser context with its own proxy and an extension (ADR 0089), the relays of ADRs 0042 and 0044, a Borrowed browser, Plugin traffic, and Sinks and webhooks under "no private endpoints".
  - Collie's layers are data, in [`test-support/fixtures/ssrf/layers.toml`](test-support/fixtures/ssrf/layers.toml): each layer, the runners that apply the table to it and the families each runs, and why a layer never meets a family every layer meets (encodings, IPv6 forms, the IANA blocks, mixed answers, redirects, rebinding, the upstream proxy path); a layer not built yet is `pending`, naming the ticket that builds it. The Quick suite fails on a built layer with no runner, a family a layer skips without a reason, a family of the table no layer runs, and a runner its test file does not hold (`goodfetch-test-support`'s `ssrf_table::every_layer_collie_carries_has_runners_for_every_family_of_the_ssrf_table`). A new layer joins the file with its ticket.
  - Foxhound's HTTP tier runs it on reqwest and `wreq` (`goodfetch-fetch-tier`'s `tests/it/addresses.rs`), directly and through the Proving ground's open proxy, resolving through the DNS fixture in process on every OS: a case's lookups become the fixture's name that answers them (`mixed.dns.test` for one lookup, `alternating.dns.test` for one answer per lookup), a domain-policy case keeping its own host. The discovered-API tier is not built yet; it takes the same transports and joins the runner with its ticket.
- **Cover every family:** encodings; IPv6 forms (IPv4-mapped, NAT64, 6to4, Teredo, zone IDs); every IANA special-purpose block that is not globally reachable; names with mixed answers; redirects of every kind and to non-HTTP schemes; rebinding; the upstream proxy path.
- **Assert that no connection reached the target**, from the Proving ground's connection-recording fixture, and the typed refusal.
- **Test rebinding against the Proving ground's DNS fixture**: end to end on Linux in a network and mount namespace whose resolver points at it (`DnsNamespace`, in a module compiled for Linux only, so no other OS runs it and Linux never skips it); in-process on every OS with a scripted sequence of answers. Never use a public rebinding service.
- **Keep the IANA special-purpose registries committed with their date**, in `crates/goodfetch-protocol/iana/`, beside the classification table `xtask codegen` writes from them, so `xtask codegen --check` fails when the two drift. The classification's differential test reads the same files through `goodfetch-test-support`'s own parser. A nightly watch job, which runs none of the product, opens an issue when they change.
- **Test the hostile-input set where each case can happen:** CR, LF or NUL in header values (Recipes, Sinks, webhooks, CDP); a domain-bound Secret across a cross-origin redirect; path traversal into every file Goodfetch writes from outside input, with each OS's cases; external `$ref` in extraction schemas; no process spawned to compare page content (checked under a seccomp filter that refuses `execve`); decompression bombs, endless bodies, deep nesting and oversized messages ending in their typed limit errors.
  - A layer refuses through `goodfetch-engine`'s one check of each kind, whose unit tests run the whole table: `hostile_input::check_headers` before a header leaves, `hostile_input::check_path` before a name from outside input names a file, and `response_limits` (`Decoding`, `parse_json`, or `http_client::Response::body` and `json`) as a body is read. A layer's own test drives it end to end against the Proving ground's pages (`bombs/*`, `body-limits/*`) with a positive control, and asserts the memory bound from the Proving ground's record of the bytes it sent.

### Endpoints

- **Run the endpoint table against every listener:** the Console, its content origin, the Daemon's socket and WebSocket, the HTTP listener, the ops listener, MCP over HTTP, the Live view, raw CDP, the Attached engine's private endpoint, an Exposed Daemon and the peer link.
  - A Session's proxy relay (`goodfetch-page`'s `proxy::relay`), a listener outside `transport`, binds a port of its own on `127.0.0.1` for its Session's life and relays its client's connections to the Session's proxy, adding the proxy's credentials. It is an HTTP proxy that tunnels only for its per-Session credentials (`Endpoint::HttpProxy`: user `goodfetch`, the token as the password of a `CONNECT`'s `Proxy-Authorization: Basic`), which the engine answers the browser's `Fetch.authRequired` with and the egress relay sends. Its column (`proxy_relay`) runs in process in `proxy::relay`'s tests, against a fake SOCKS5 proxy, with the Run's Audit log on a test home as the verifier, since its token never leaves the engine. A request without credentials is answered with the `407` challenge every client meets first (`{ expect = "challenge" }`, no Audit log entry); a wrong token, another Session's relay's among them, is refused with a `407` and recorded.
  - The committed table, [`test-support/fixtures/endpoint-table.toml`](test-support/fixtures/endpoint-table.toml), is the one source of its rows and columns; nothing else lists them. A listener joins as a column with its ticket, and a rule as a row with the first listener it applies to. The Quick suite fails on a row that applies to no listener, a listener with no row, and a cell its listener's transport cannot try.
  - A listener's tests call `EndpointRunner::new(verifier).run(&listener)` from `goodfetch-test-support`, with the listener's kind, address, token and the token of another listener as a value, and the product's reader of its Audit log as the verifier. The runner first sends the valid `hello` or request, which must be accepted, then every row of the listener's column, never skipping one, and fails each row whose answer or Audit log entry is missing. The Console's WebSocket (`Endpoint::SignedInWebSocket`) carries its token as a sign-in's cookie on the handshake, so it refuses each token row there with a status. The content origin (`Endpoint::SignedAddress`) takes no credential but an address the Console signed for one file: its token is such an address's path and query, the other listener's token is the Console's sign-in cookie, which it never reads, and the row `token_for_another_file` carries the signature onto another file's path. An address's expiry is checked at `content_origin`'s interface on the paused `Clock`, since no test waits 5 minutes.
  - The rows presenting a token of their own take it from the listener's description (`with_other_listener_token`, `with_expired_token`, `with_revoked_token`, `with_scoped_elsewhere_token`), and a row whose token the test does not give fails. The Collie listeners' tests read their refusals from the Audit log through `tests/it/endpoint.rs`'s `audit_verifier`.
  - A regression test of an advisory calls `run_advisory(&listener, "CVE-…")`, which runs only the rows whose `advisories` cite it, after the positive control, and fails when no row of the listener's column does. The token comparison's timing is a property of `goodfetch-engine`'s `endpoint` interface: guesses sharing no prefix and all but the last letter, timed in a seeded interleaving, show no difference Welch's t finds.
- **Check every route's headers on both of the Console's origins** with an HTTP test in the executable crate, a table each new route adds its rows to (`goodfetch-collie`'s `tests/it/console.rs` and `tests/it/content_origin.rs`), and run the Proving ground's escape page (`adversarial/3`) on the content origin, in the Console's sandboxed frame and in a frame with no sandbox, and its page that calls the local Daemon (`adversarial/4`) against the Console, both from `adversarial.localhost`, in a served test (`console/collie/tests/served/content-origin.spec.ts`).
- **Cover every row:** missing, wrong, expired, revoked, out-of-scope and other-listener tokens, and tokens in a query string; a foreign `Host`; a foreign or missing `Origin` on the request and the WebSocket handshake; plain HTTP where TLS is configured; `X-Forwarded-*` from an unconfigured address; each listener's own rules (ADRs 0031, 0049, 0050, 0055, and MCP's audience and no passthrough). Check each refusal in the Audit log.
- **Fuzz the protocol from the committed JSON Schema bundle** with `proptest` and `proptest-state-machine`, over every transport. Every invalid call gets a typed error and changes no state.
  - A listener's test calls `ProtocolFuzz::new(ProtocolSchema::committed()?).with_valid_calls(&[…]).run(config, connect, state)` from `goodfetch-test-support`. It reads the bundle and the catalog file as files, so a change to the contract changes the calls with no hand edit. `connect` opens a new Connection, past its `hello`, for each sequence; the transport is the caller's `ProtocolTransport`, which returns the reply carrying a call's id. `state` reads the state the calls may change, such as the files of the engine's Goodfetch home.
  - Each sequence mixes invalid calls of every operation, one breach each (an unknown operation, params that are no object, a missing or unknown property, a value its schema refuses), with valid calls of the operations `with_valid_calls` names, which must answer at once. An invalid call fails the property unless it gets a typed error that is not internal and leaves `state` unchanged; a valid call fails it on an internal or untyped error or a result its schema refuses, and its state becomes the one the next calls are checked against. A failure is reported as the sequence proptest shrank it to.
  - Run it at `proptest_config()`'s size with the test's seed and with `source_file` set to `file!()`, so proptest writes its regression file under the crate's `tests/proptest-regressions/`, which is committed.
- **Run the MCP conformance suite against MCP over HTTP**, in the suites and on the platforms [ADR 0070](docs/adr/0070-mcp-adapters-and-integrations-are-tested-through-their-platforms-own-clients-against-a-real-engine.md) gives it.
- **Run a ZAP baseline scan of the Console in the Release suite:** image by digest, `-silent`, `allow_issue_writing: false`, FAIL rules committed (`ci/zap/rules.tsv`), the crawl bounded by depth rather than time, under ADR 0043's deny so its Egress canary catches a call home. `ci/zap.sh scan` scans the Console Collie's executable serves from a Daemon `goodfetch-test-env --daemon` starts in a home of its own.

### Secrets

- **Plant a Secret canary for every kind of secret** (typed password, TOTP seed, emailed or texted one-time code, proxy password, HTTP authentication password, a header or JSON value sent by `page.request` or a route, a value a `caller:` resolver returns, model API key, webhook signing secret, Sink credential, Session cookie, endpoint token, run data key), through the Proving ground's login flows and every Secret provider (ADR 0092).
- **Search every output for every Secret canary**, raw and JSON-escaped, URL-encoded, base64 and hex, and inside the Basic `Authorization` and `Proxy-Authorization` headers the engine builds. The outputs are:
  - logs, Run traces, HAR files and console captures, and screenshots and recordings where masking applies;
  - the OTLP export, read through an in-process OTLP collector (ADR 0132);
  - the Audit log, the Effect journal, and Checkpoints outside their encrypted fields;
  - Exports and Notifications;
  - JSON-RPC errors, error messages in run state, and model requests;
  - the Console's offline cache, `doctor`'s file, and the SDKs' `repr`, `toString` and errors;
  - what a call returns and an event carries: reads such as `inputValue`, page function results, extracted values, Records, response bodies, and page and network events, for every canary but a one-time code, masked only where it was typed, and a Session cookie, which `cookies()` and network events return by design;
  - Plans (ADR 0092).

  _Why:_ redaction is best effort against arbitrary transforms, but Goodfetch's own encodings are known.
- **Property-test redaction** with generated values that are hard to redact (quotes, backslashes, newlines, `%`, Unicode, very long, empty, common words, values that contain one another), through every formatter and serializer. Commit `proptest-regressions/`.
- **Scan each CI job's logs, test reports, snapshots and artifacts with the `gitleaks` binary** (pinned by checksum, Secret canaries added as a rule) before they upload. A hit fails the job. Each job that uploads runs `ci/leaks.sh` over every path it uploads, with `if: always()`, and each upload waits for that step's success; `xtask output-scans` refuses an upload without it. The scan runs gitleaks' own rules ([`ci/gitleaks.toml`](ci/gitleaks.toml)) and the canaries' rule; `just leaks <path>…` runs it locally. A job's live log reaches GitHub as each step runs, so no step scans it first: the canary searches of the tests that write it stand there.
- **Scan commits,** Model recordings included: with GitHub secret scanning and push protection once the repository is public ([`.github/security-settings.md`](.github/security-settings.md)); until then with `gitleaks` over each pull request's commits in the Pre-merge suite (`just commit-leaks`).

### Other rules

- **Property-test the Audit log's hash chain:** an edit, deletion, reordering or truncation of entries or of the tail fails verification, a Daemon group's single chain included.
- **Add one regression test per bypass** at the layer it slipped through, Goodfetch's own or a class a comparable project disclosed. Cite the advisory in a comment. A nightly watch job lists new advisories of comparable projects and opens an issue.
- **Never skip a security test where it is scheduled.** The OSes in the boundary table are the schedule, and a test's absence from an OS its row does not give is not a skip. On a scheduled host, never `#[ignore]` it, return early or gate it at run time; where the host lacks a mechanism, assert the refusal (`confinement_unavailable`, `local_address_unavailable`). A missing tool fails the job.
- **Pin every container image a workflow runs by digest.** `xtask image-pins`, in `just lint`, reads each job's and service's container, each `docker://` step and action, each Containerfile's `FROM`, each `image.toml` pin file under `ci/` and each literal image a `run:` script or `ci/` script names.
- **Run CodeQL** on Rust, TypeScript, Python and the workflows in the Pre-merge suite once the repository is public. A high or critical alert blocks the merge. `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` skips its jobs while the repository is private; the owner's checklist turns the merge protection on.

### Manual security tests

Only these stay manual. They are not Journeys: none has an automated step, and each runs on a release candidate rather than at a milestone's owner test. They are named by title.

| Manual test | When | What a person does | Recorded on |
|---|---|---|---|
| Threat-model review | each minor's release candidate | Walks the trust-boundary table against the minor's changes and confirms each new decoder, listener, Plugin kind, widening setting, Secret kind and output has its test | the release pull request |
| Vulnerability triage | each new fuzz crash, external report and advisory-watch issue | Judges it against `SECURITY.md`'s vulnerability definition; opens the private advisory or closes with a reason | the advisory or issue |
| Independent security assessment | before 1.0 and each later major; declined while there is no revenue (ADR 0076), the manual threat-model review stands in | An outside tester attacks the boundaries; each finding gets a fix and a regression test | a report linked from the release notes |
| Confinement where CI cannot run | each minor's release candidate | WSL1's refusal; Bridge Sinks under Docker Desktop and Podman machine on macOS and Windows; the documented host-firewall recipes for `direct` | the release pull request |
| Passkeys on real authenticators | each minor's release candidate | Sign-in, step-up and recovery on a platform authenticator and a hardware key | the release pull request |

## Console and docs site

Decided in [ADR 0068](docs/adr/0068-the-console-is-tested-in-real-browsers-and-served-by-the-real-binary.md). Which accessibility, localization and visual checks run on these runners is in [Accessibility, localization and visual regression](#accessibility-localization-and-visual-regression).

### Where each test lives

Each suite contains the one before, so the table gives the first suite that runs each test; [Browsers](#browsers) says which browsers each suite adds.

| Path | What it tests | Runner | First suite |
|---|---|---|---|
| `console/<package>/src/**/*.test.ts` | logic with no DOM | Vitest `unit` (Node) | Quick |
| `console/<package>/src/**/*.test.tsx` | one component | Vitest `component` (Browser Mode) | Pre-merge |
| `console/<package>/src/**/*.stories.tsx` | each state of a component, rendered as a test | Vitest `component` (Storybook's addon) | Pre-merge |
| `console/<package>/tests/screens/` | the whole Console against the in-memory adapter of the protocol | Vitest `component` | Pre-merge |
| `console/<package>/tests/served/` | the Console served by the binary, which embeds its Console build (`just console-build`, ADR 0057) | Playwright Test | Pre-merge |
| `console/<package>/tests/journeys/J-NNN.spec.ts` | the `console` variants of Journeys ([Journeys](#journeys)) | Playwright Test | Pre-merge |
| `conformance/console/` | the Console conformance suite | Playwright Test | Pre-merge |
| the live-view packages' `*.test.ts(x)` | `<goodfetch-live-view>` and its React wrapper | Vitest Browser Mode | Pre-merge |
| `website/src/**/*.test.ts` | the docs site's build code | Vitest (Node) | Quick |
| `website/tests/` | the built docs site | Playwright Test | Pre-merge |
| `docs/**/*.md(x)` code blocks, and the published packages' READMEs | the docs code samples, extracted by `xtask docs-samples` | `twoslash` in the docs build; the TypeScript SDK's Vitest, the Python SDK's pytest, rustdoc, trycmd, the MCP server's tests | Pre-merge |
| the files beside each migration guide, with their manifest | the guide's "before" programs, never run | tsc, pyright or rustc against the pinned competitor | Pre-merge |

### Components

- **A test that renders or needs the DOM is a `.test.tsx` file** and runs in Vitest Browser Mode with the Playwright provider. A `.test.ts` file runs in Node and never touches the DOM. Never jsdom or happy-dom.
  _Why:_ Base UI's positioning, focus and pointer handling need a real layout.
- **Mount with `vitest-browser-react`, find with `vitest/browser` locators, act with `vitest/browser`'s `userEvent`.** Never `@testing-library/user-event`.
  _Why:_ events are then real browser input sent by Playwright, and the locators are Playwright's.
- **Use the shared Vitest fixture of `@goodfetch/test-support`; it resets hover in `beforeEach`** (`userEvent.unhover(document.body)`). It is the `component` project's setup file, `@goodfetch/test-support/vitest-browser/setup`, whose `hoverReset` the root `vitest.config.ts` runs before every `.test.tsx` test.
  _Why:_ the Playwright provider keeps the pointer position and hover state between tests of one file, and tests run shuffled.
- **The engine is the in-memory adapter of the protocol from `@goodfetch/test-support`, passed to the Console's Client as its transport and body fetcher.** Never MSW, never a patched global `WebSocket`. A call the test did not answer fails the test, and every message is validated against the schema bundle.
- **Test data comes from builders with literal defaults.** Override only what the test asserts on; no random data.
- **Each state of a component has a story** (`*.stories.tsx`, Storybook 10 until Storybook 11 is stable and supports TypeScript 7). Stories hold no assertions and no play functions. Behaviour goes in `.test.tsx`, which may reuse a story through `composeStories`.
- **Storybook's docgen is `react-docgen`.** Never `react-docgen-typescript` or the experimental docgen server, which need the TypeScript compiler API.

### Browser state

- **Set colour scheme, reduced motion, locale, time zone and online status through Playwright:** context options (`colorScheme`, `reducedMotion`, `locale`, `timezoneId`), `page.emulateMedia` and `context.setOffline` in served tests; the provider's context options and the `@goodfetch/test-support` browser command in component tests.
  - In a component test, `browserState({ colorScheme, reducedMotion, locale, timeZone, online })` from `@goodfetch/test-support/vitest-browser` sets any of the five until the test ends, when the page gets the project's state back. Colour scheme and reduced motion work in every browser. Locale, time zone and online status are DevTools Protocol overrides, so only Chromium takes them per test; elsewhere the command fails with `provider_unsupported`, and a project sets them in its provider's context options.
  - Online status there is `navigator.onLine` and its events, never `context.setOffline`, which would also cut the page's own connection to Vitest and hang the run.
- **Pass page visibility to the Console's Client as a value**, like its transport. Playwright cannot emulate it. Served tests of the idle stop close the page instead.
- **Never redefine a property of `document`, `navigator` or `window`.** oxlint's `no-restricted-properties` refuses `Object.defineProperty`, `Object.defineProperties` and `Reflect.defineProperty` in test files, and `vi.stubGlobal` is banned ([Writing a test](#writing-a-test)).

### The served Console

- **Browser tests of the Console as users get it run on Playwright Test against the executable built from the commit.** Never against a Vite server.
- **Each test starts its own Daemon** through the `daemon` fixture of `@goodfetch/test-support/playwright` (`goodfetch-test-env --daemon`), with its own home, port 0 and fresh browser context.
- **Test each Product's Console build served by its own executable:** a screen both Products carry on Collie's, Foxhound's screens on Foxhound's, and a route walk on Foxhound's that checks every route of Collie's build is there and renders.
- **Sign in the way a person does,** through the `signIn` fixture: the executable's single-user sign-in link, Playwright's virtual authenticator for passkeys and step-up, a local OIDC provider pinned by digest. Never write a cookie or storage state yourself: the fixtures refuse `storageState`, and oxlint refuses `addCookies`, `setStorageState` and `document.cookie` in test files.
- **Make faults where they happen:** `routeWebSocket` with `connectToServer()` for a dropped or stalled connection, the real process for an engine fault. `routeWebSocket` replaces the page's `WebSocket` with a shim that forwards to the real binary; use it only to drop or delay real messages, never to invent one.
- **Find by role, label or text.** `data-testid` only where an element has no accessible role or name; never CSS classes or XPath.
- **Wait with web-first assertions and `expect.poll`.** Never `waitForTimeout` (oxlint refuses it) or `networkidle`.
- **A console error, an uncaught page error, a CSP violation or a failed same-origin request fails the test**, through the shared fixture's `failOnErrors`. A CSP violation is seen twice: by the page's `securitypolicyviolation` event and as the report the browser sends to the CSP report endpoint (`application/csp-report` or `application/reports+json`). A same-origin request fails when it gets no answer or an HTTP status of 400 or above; one the browser cancelled because the page navigated away or closed does not. A test tool's own fault is the one exception: an entry of `known-issues.ts` in `@goodfetch/test-support` names the browser and the Playwright version it was seen on, its upstream report, and the errors it raises, and the helper whose action raises them tolerates exactly those errors around that action alone (`PageErrorWatch.tolerating`): a CSP violation the entry matches, and beside each one console message and one report it matches. Tolerated errors fail nothing and are attached to the report as `known-issues`. A Vitest test fails when the installed Playwright moves, so each bump checks every entry again. The one entry: Playwright inserts an inline `<style>` before every WebKit screenshot, which the production CSP refuses, so the shared screenshot helper tolerates that refusal, from no source file, during its comparison.
- **`toMatchAriaSnapshot` and Vitest's ARIA snapshots follow [Snapshots](#snapshots).**
- **Tests run under the production CSP.** The binary has no switch that relaxes it, and Playwright's `bypassCSP` is never set: the shared fixture fails a test that sets it, and in test files oxlint refuses `newContext`, `browser.newPage`, `launchPersistentContext`, `launch`, `connect` and `connectOverCDP`, so every browser and context comes from the fixture. `context.newPage()` is allowed; API calls use the fixture's `request`.
- **Keep traces on failure** and upload them with the JUnit files: `playwright.config.ts` keeps a failed test's trace (`trace: 'retain-on-failure'`) in its output folder, `target/junit/playwright/`, beside the JUnit file.
- **The tools served tests rely on are proved under the production CSP's Trusted Types** on Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Firefox and WebKit in the Release suite, whose cells install every browser of the locked Playwright before the deny, `ci/host/fetch.sh`; until a Release run has passed, local runs show them there: #372's on macOS on 2026-10-19 and, with every test of `trusted-types.spec.ts`, `fail-on-errors.spec.ts` and the `bypassCSP` refusals, one on Linux x86-64 on 2026-10-22), by `test-support/typescript/tests/trusted-types.spec.ts`: `page.clock`, `routeWebSocket`'s shim with `connectToServer()`, axe's injected script and the virtual authenticator's `credentials.install()`, each its own test on a fixture page that sets `require-trusted-types-for 'script'`. A tool that fails there is replaced or reported to the owner; the CSP is never relaxed.

### The Console conformance suite

- **It runs the Console against a target given as configuration:** each Product's Daemon, serving its own Console build, and the Stub gateway here, Goodfetch Cloud's gateway from the private repository. The Stub gateway serves Foxhound's Console build and starts each run's engine from the executable of the run's Product (ADR 0096). It is `goodfetch-stub-gateway --console-build <folder> --collie <executable> --foxhound <executable> --token-file <file> --state <folder> --ready <file>`: it serves Foxhound's build from `xtask console build`'s output folder, the content origin beside it, both on loopback port 0, signs a browser in at `POST /sign-in` with the token file's token swapped for a cookie, gives each run an Attached engine in a home of its own under `--state`, and writes `{"console", "content"}`, its two origins, to the `--ready` file once it serves; it writes nothing to stdout. Its Connection answers `run.list` from its own index, starts a run (`run.start` or `crawl.start`) on a new engine of the Product the routing rule picks, and forwards every other message to the engine that owns the run its params name, or to an engine of the Client's Product when they name none. Its cases cover the rule that picks each run's Product: a Collie Client's run lands on Collie's engine and its `crawl.start` answers `not_in_product`; a Foxhound Client's run lands on Foxhound's; a Recipe that names Foxhound lands on Foxhound's whoever starts it; a run started from the Console lands by what its target names; Foxhound's screens show only for a run on Foxhound's engine, and a Foxhound-only call on a run of Collie's engine answers `not_in_product`, naming the run's Product; a Recipe that names Collie and calls `crawl.start` answers `not_in_product` on Collie's engine with the `fix` to name Foxhound in its manifest and no install command; a Recipe that names Collie and sets a crawl setting, started from the Console, lands on Collie's engine by its manifest, not its settings, and the key answers `not_in_product` there. The Stub gateway picks each run's Product with `goodfetch-protocol`'s `routing::product_for_run`, which Goodfetch Cloud's gateway calls too; its own Quick test is table-driven over the generated table of Foxhound's names, one case per row of ADR 0096's table, one per kind of name (an operation, a setting key, a Plugin kind, a Hook stage, a value Foxhound adds to a shared type) and one per known Run ID, which the table never routes again. Each target run writes its own result document with `kind = "conformance"`, like a Journey ([Journeys](#journeys)): its Playwright reporter derives each outcome, and the Rust reader recomputes it. It writes no case log.
- **Its three targets are three projects of `playwright.config.ts`,** each writing its own document: `collie-daemon`, Collie's Daemon serving Collie's build, runs every case but Foxhound's screens and the routing cases; `foxhound-daemon`, Foxhound's Daemon serving Foxhound's build, runs the Foxhound-only case, `foxhounds-screens-show-only-for-a-run-on-foxhounds-engine`; `stub-gateway` runs the whole suite on the Stub gateway, which `@goodfetch/test-support/playwright`'s `stubGatewayConsole` starts beside the test's Foxhound Daemon as its `consoleServer`, from the test tool manifest's `stub_gateway`, `collie` and `foxhound`, its state in the test's folder, its sign-in link its token in the fragment. A case reaches its target's runs through `conformance/console/target.ts`: on a Daemon with the executable's commands, behind a gateway with the protocol, as the Console sends a start (`startRun`, `finishRun`), or as a Client program does (`ProgramConnection`, signed in with the gateway's token). The `routing` capability's cases are the routing rows above that need no Recipe: `a-collie-clients-run-lands-on-collies-engine-and-its-crawl-start-answers-not-in-product`, `a-foxhound-clients-run-lands-on-foxhounds-engine`, `a-run-the-console-starts-lands-by-what-its-target-names` (a plain `run.start`, a `crawl.start` and a start setting a crawl key, by the generated `FOXHOUND_OPERATIONS`) and `a-foxhound-only-call-on-a-run-of-collies-engine-answers-not-in-product-naming-its-product`, each covering `story:12-231` and `story:12-232`; each tells which engine a run landed on as the Cloud-readiness suite does, by a Foxhound Connection's `run.seeds`. The Recipe rows join once a start carries a Recipe's manifest (#1691). A case that needs what the Stub gateway does not serve yet skips there, naming it, so its case stays `not_reached` in `stub-gateway.json`.
- **Write a case as a test of `@goodfetch/test-support/conformance`'s `test`, titled with the case's ID and carrying the annotation `capability` naming its capability**, as `conformance/console/cases.toml` declares them. The configuration gives each target its own project, whose `metadata.conformanceTarget` names the target, whose `product` is the Daemon's Product and which sets a `provingGroundSeed` (a test whose Daemon has no Proving ground fails with `proving_ground_unseeded`). Before the body, each test attaches its target's Product and version, as the executable's `--version` reports them, the platform, the browser's runtime (`chromium-141`) and the Proving ground's version and seed.
  - The configuration's reporter, `@goodfetch/test-support/result-document-reporter` (option `caseList`, `conformance/console/cases.toml` beside the configuration by default), writes one document per target, `<target>.json`, into `GOODFETCH_TEST_RESULT_DOCUMENTS`, through `conformanceDocument`: every capability and case the list declares, each run of a test an entry whose one check is whether the test passed. A skipped test leaves its case `not_reached`, and a capability no test reached stays `unproved`.
  - It fails the run when a test's annotation and title name no declared case (`conformance_case_unknown`), or two tests of one target report different facts (`conformance_target_mismatch`), such as one target's tests run in two browsers: give each browser its own target. A project that names no target writes no `conformance` document, and a target none of whose tests attached its facts gets none.
- **The Console's own Client is a case too:** the `client` capability runs `createConsoleClient` of the target's build, in Node, on the target's `/protocol` with the page's sign-in cookie as configuration, through the protocol suite's and the TypeScript SDK's lifecycle Client cases a browser's Client has, under their IDs there (story 12-138). Its screens' use of the catalog is checked twice: in the Quick suite, `console/collie/src/catalog-use.test.ts` fails a screen whose call names no catalog operation as a literal, or that names an absolute address, a request API or an engine path of its own outside the modules that take the endpoint as configuration; in the suite, the `catalog` case walks the screens and fails on any method outside the catalog or any request to another origin (story 12-137).
- **`just test` reads every `conformance` document a Playwright run wrote** through `goodfetch-conformance read` (`ci/read-conformance-documents.sh`), so the run fails when the harness refuses one.
- **Add a case when the Console starts to need something new from its target.**

### Browsers

- **One Playwright version for the whole pnpm workspace**, shared by Vitest's provider.
- **The Console's and the docs site's tests run on one Node line;** their matrix is browsers.
- **Pre-merge:** Chromium on Linux. **Release:** components, the live-view packages, served tests, Journeys and conformance on every OS runner with every browser Playwright ships for it, plus phone emulation on Chromium and WebKit for served tests; the docs site's build, lychee and Playwright on Linux, on Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; the docs code samples as [Docs code samples](#docs-code-samples) says. `playwright.config.ts` adds its `firefox`, `webkit`, `translation` and `elsewhere` projects beside Chromium's (and, in the pinned image, the shared visual config its `visual-firefox` and `visual-webkit` projects), and `vitest.config.ts` its `component-text-spacing`, `component-stories-en-XA` and `component-stories-ar-XB` projects, when `GOODFETCH_TEST_SUITE` is `release`; the CI image carries Chromium.

### Docs site

- **Docusaurus throws on every broken reference:** `onBrokenLinks`, `onBrokenAnchors`, `onDuplicateRoutes` and `markdown.hooks`' `onBrokenMarkdownLinks`, `onBrokenMarkdownImages` and `onUnusedMarkdownDirectives`.
- **Every kept version is built, and every page has its `.md` twin, an `llms.txt` entry and its content in `llms-full.txt`,** in its own Product's section (`/docs/collie/`, `/docs/foxhound/`), or for a root page in the root's; the root `llms.txt` lists both sections' files and the root pages ([ADR 0097](docs/adr/0097-one-docs-site-renders-each-products-section-from-one-source-and-neither-product-leads-with-access.md)).
- **lychee runs with `--offline --include-fragments --root-dir <root>` in the suites**, over the built site copied to `<root>/docs/`, so absolute `/docs/` links resolve. External links are the Live check `docs-external-links`.
- **Playwright tests the built site served locally under `/docs/` on port 0,** never a Vercel preview: every `sitemap.xml` page of every kept version answers 200 with no console, hydration or same-site request error, and the interactive components work. Search is tested against an index built with the site; a hosted search service is tested only by `docs-deploy-smoke`.
- **After each production deploy, the Live check `docs-deploy-smoke`** checks the deployed pages, and a `.md` page, `llms.txt` and `llms-full.txt` of each Product's section and of the root, with their content types.
- **`typos` checks the docs' spelling.** No Vale, no cspell.
- **The wording check, `xtask wording`, runs in the Quick suite** ([ADR 0097](docs/adr/0097-one-docs-site-renders-each-products-section-from-one-source-and-neither-product-leads-with-access.md)): it fails on a term of ADR 0035's list ("bypass" in any form, "undetectable", "undetected", "solve any CAPTCHA"), word-bounded and case-insensitive, in the prose of the docs pages (not `docs/adr/`, `docs/agents/` or `rnd/`), READMEs, `CHANGELOG.md`, skills, the catalog's, settings schema's and Error codes' texts, the Console's message catalog and package descriptions and keywords. Code is skipped. Rewrite the sentence; extend the list only on counsel's review.

### Docs code samples

- **Give every fenced block in `docs/` a language;** use `text` for output and prose. The docs build fails on an empty info string.
- **`xtask docs-samples` extracts every sample** from `.md` and `.mdx` pages into `target/docs-samples/`, with a manifest. The docs build's remark plugin lists every sample block it sees; a difference between that list and the manifest, or a runner whose case count differs from the manifest, fails the check.
- **Tag a block that can't run `no_run`** after its language. A `rust`, `ts` or `python` block is still compiled, type-checked or linted; a `console` block or an MCP pair is only listed in the manifest.
- **Mark MCP samples `json mcp-request` and `json mcp-answer`.** Other `json` blocks are not samples.
- **Each surface's tests run its samples:** `twoslash` type-checks `ts` and `tsx` in the docs build (our own remark plugin, on the TypeScript 6 alias) and the TypeScript SDK's tests run them; Ruff lints and the Python SDK's pytest runs `python`; `#[cfg(doctest)]` includes in `goodfetch-collie` and `goodfetch-foxhound` run `rust`; trycmd runs the `.trycmd` files from each executable crate's `tests/it` (`...` for any lines, `[..]` within a line); an MCP server test plays each `mcp-request` and `mcp-answer` pair through `rmcp`.
- **Run samples against the binary built from the commit and a local Proving ground,** never the online one. Hide setup with the tool's own convention (`# ` in Rust, `---cut---` in Twoslash).
- **Run a sample against the executable of the Product its page documents,** Collie's for what both Products carry, extracting it from each Product's rendering ([ADR 0097](docs/adr/0097-one-docs-site-renders-each-products-section-from-one-source-and-neither-product-leads-with-access.md)). A page rendered for both Products runs its samples once, on Collie's executable; its Foxhound rendering is checked statically against Foxhound's packages, never run: Twoslash for `ts`, Ruff and pyright for `python`, a `no_run` doctest for `rust`. A page rendered for Foxhound only runs on Foxhound's executable. No code block of a Product's rendering, `console` and MCP pairs included, may carry a name from the generator's list of the other Product, except in a part marked as naming that Product, which the generator leaves as written and checks statically against that Product's packages. Every published package's README is extracted too, and its samples run on that package's Product's executable.
- **Keep a migration guide's "before" programs out of the samples:** each is a file beside its guide, with a manifest pinning the competitor's version, type-checked against it (tsc, pyright, rustc) and never run, and the page includes the file rather than a fenced block. The type-check runs in Pre-merge when the guide or its manifest changes, and in the Release suite.
- **Only `docs/` (`next`) runs.** Frozen versions were tested at their release.
- **Pre-merge:** the affected samples on one runtime. **Release:** every sample on its surface's own matrix ([SDK and CLI tests](#sdk-and-cli-tests), [MCP, framework adapters and Integrations](#mcp-framework-adapters-and-integrations)).

## Feature coverage

Decided in [ADR 0072](docs/adr/0072-every-feature-in-a-generated-inventory-has-a-passing-test-and-code-coverage-and-mutants-are-signals.md). Coverage means every Feature has a passing test, never a percentage. Suites are those of [Suites, commands and time budgets](#suites-commands-and-time-budgets); quarantine is that of [Flaky tests and Live checks](#flaky-tests-and-live-checks).

### The Feature inventory

- **Never edit `features/inventory.json` by hand.** `xtask codegen` generates it from ADR 0037's matrix, `features/stories/`, `features/rows.toml`, `features/areas.toml` and the contracts the code defines, and `xtask codegen --check` fails on drift.
- **Every generator reads source files or Rust types in process.** `screen:` comes from the Console's route definitions read statically; if they can't be, from a route list committed in the Console's package, which the Console's Pre-merge build checks for drift. The Quick suite never builds the Console to get it.
- **Write a Feature as `<kind>:<id>`.** Declared kinds: `row:` (an ADR 0037 matrix row that is not `excluded`) and `story:<area>-<n>` (a spec's user story). Generated kinds: `operation:`, `error:`, `event:`, `setting:`, `command:`, `exit:`, `format:`, `plugin-kind:`, `block-cause:`, `sink:`, `mcp-profile:`, `skill:`, `adapter:`, `integration:` and `screen:`.
- **A contract you add is a Feature in the same pull request,** and that pull request adds a test that declares it. The Quick suite fails until it does.
- **Never renumber or reuse a story ID.** A new story takes its spec's next number; a removed one stays with `status = "retired"` and a `reason`.
- **Give every declared Feature a milestone**, and every generated Feature an owning area through `features/areas.toml`.

### Declaring what a test proves

- **Declare a Feature only when the test asserts its outcome**, never one the test merely passes through.
- **Declare it in the runner's own idiom**, never by a file or name pattern:
  - Rust: `/// Covers: operation:run.start, error:run_not_found` on the test function, or `//! Covers: …` on a test module for every test in it; on an rstest table, a `/// Covers:` line between `#[rstest]` or one `#[case]` and the next declares for the case below it only, as rstest gives that case the attributes written there, so each case declares the row it reaches;
  - Vitest: the Feature ID as a tag, on the test or its `describe`, as written (Vitest's tag names accept `:` and `.`); `vitest.config.ts` declares every ID of the inventory through test support's `readFeatureIds`, with `strictTags` on, so an unknown one fails the run;
  - pytest: `@pytest.mark.covers("<feature id>")` or a module's `pytestmark`, under `strict_markers`;
  - Playwright Test: an annotation `{ type: "covers", description: "<feature id>" }` per Feature, on the test or its `describe`; `playwright.config.ts` passes the inventory's IDs, and test support's `test` fails a test whose annotation names an unknown one;
  - Journeys, conformance cases (each `cases.toml` under `conformance/`) and Client conformance cases (`conformance/clients/`): their `covers` list. A retired Journey declares nothing.
- **`xtask features scan` reads every declaration with its level**, from where the test sits: a `journeys` folder below `tests/` and a Journey's `covers` are `journey`; `conformance/` is `conformance`; `src/` and `tests/unit/` are `unit`; any other `tests/` folder is `integration`. Rust's lines, the Journeys and the conformance cases are read from source; the Vitest tags, Playwright annotations and pytest markers from the runners' listings (`xtask journeys listings`), which start processes, so the check reads them only in the Pre-merge suite.
- **A test that drives the protocol and declares an `operation:`, `error:`, `event:`, `command:` or `exit:` Feature must reach it.** The exercise audit reads `rpc`'s dispatch records and the engine's outcome records (the engine's structured log for the binary, the test `tracing` subscriber for in-process tests over the in-memory transport) and test support's command record, and fails a declaring test that never called the operation, received the Error code or event, ran the command or saw the exit code. An outcome record (target `goodfetch::run::outcome`, `Run::record_outcome`) names an Error code a Run recorded and no reply carries, such as a work item's `handler_lost`, so a test that asserts one from the run file reaches it. Under `just test pre-merge` or `release`, each test nextest runs leaves its exercise record in `target/test-records/`; `just features-audit` reads the tests that nextest's JUnit file shows ran (`target/nextest/ci/junit.xml` under the workspace, even where `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` puts the build elsewhere, as in CI's VM), and fails when tests left records but no JUnit file names them; the suites run it as a gate ([The checks](#the-checks)). `just features-audit all` judges every declaring test, run or not, for a local look. A declaring test with no record fails, as does one whose record never shows its Feature, and any test whose record holds a command whose arguments named no `command:` Feature (`command-unmatched`); only a test whose record holds no dispatch record at all, or a unit test that left no record, may declare the `error:` or `event:` it asserts itself. A test above the unit level must leave a record, and a test whose record shows it dispatched is judged on every declaration, unit test or not. An in-process test reaches the record through its crate's helper that serves the engine on the in-memory transport (`goodfetch-engine`'s `attached_with`, `goodfetch-crawl`'s `crawl`), which installs the test subscriber unless the test installed its own. The engine's log is lossy (ADR 0122), so a killed engine loses the lines its writer's thread had not written yet, the dispatch records among them: a test's Client of an engine that does not exit by itself records the calls it read answered in the test's answer record (`goodfetch_test_support::AnsweredCalls`: `sent` and `read` on each message, `record_in(env.folder())` once the engine is killed or left to teardown), which teardown copies into the exercise record after the command record. Collie's and Foxhound's `Attached` helpers do it; any other Client that kills an engine does it too, or reaches what it declares on an engine that exits as it should. `env.exercise_lines()` gives the lines teardown will copy, as they stand (#1779).
- **A test that never reaches `rpc` may declare the `error:` or `event:` Feature it asserts,** but must assert the Error code or the event itself: a unit test of a module's own error, or a `tests/it` test of a module that binds a socket, such as the Browser cache's downloads against a loopback stand-in. The audit can't see it, so it is a review standard. A declaration the audit finds unreached is fixed by reaching it, or by moving or removing it, never by widening this rule. A test that answers a command in the executable without `rpc`, such as `config explain`, never declares the `operation:` of the same name.

### What counts as tested

- **Every Feature is tested, excepted or untested.** The gates fail only on untested Features.
- **A Feature is tested** when, on `main`'s latest Release suite run (while the repository is private, `main`'s latest commit whose cells have all run, across dispatches: [ADR 0108](docs/adr/0108-while-the-repository-is-private-the-release-suite-runs-its-macos-cells-on-the-owners-machine-and-its-hosted-cells-under-a-ceiling.md)), at least one test declares it, every declaring test ran and passed in every configuration it runs in, none is quarantined, every declaring Journey's variants of reached milestones are `proved`, and every declaring conformance case's capability is `proved`.
- **A story with `journey = true` also needs a proved variant of a Journey that covers it,** and every story needs a declaring test above the unit level ([Which stories need a Journey](#which-stories-need-a-journey)).
- **Test a Feature once.** The per-surface completeness and conformance of [SDK and CLI tests](#sdk-and-cli-tests) and of the surface ADRs, and a Journey's variants, prove each surface.
- **Once, not once per Product.** The inventory records the Products that carry each Feature, from ADR 0087's generated table of Foxhound's names, and `xtask features report` gives each Product's view.
- **A quarantined test leaves every Feature it declares untested** until its fix merges.

### Exceptions

- **List a Feature no pinned test can prove in `features/exceptions.toml`;** it is then excepted, never tested. Give it its `evidence` (`manual`, `live`, `check` or `none`), a `reason` and a `link` to the owner-test step, Live check, CI job or place where it is tested.
- **Never except a Feature a pinned test can reach.** The release pull request lists every exception, and the owner test lists the `manual` ones as manual steps.

### The checks

| Check | Suite or gate | Fails when |
|---|---|---|
| Feature check | Quick, over Rust's, the Journeys' and the conformance cases' declarations (a generated Feature none declares is only noted); Pre-merge, `just features-tests`, over every runner's | the inventory is stale, a declaration names an unknown or retired Feature, a generated Feature has neither a declaring test nor an exception, a non-excluded matrix row has no milestone, a generated Feature has no area or two, or an exception is incomplete or names a Feature a test also declares |
| Exercise audit | Pre-merge (tests that ran), Release (each cell's tests that ran: all but the quarantined ones and those the cell cannot run) | a declaring test never reached its Feature |
| Milestone gate | the owner test (ADR 0058) | a generated Feature, or a declared Feature due at the milestone, is untested (neither tested nor excepted) |
| Release gate | `xtask release` | a Feature present in the release is untested (neither tested nor excepted) |

- **Run `just features`** to see what the changed packages declare and miss, `just features-tests` for the check over every runner's declarations, and `just features-audit` after `just test pre-merge` for the exercise audit of the Rust tests that ran.
- **The suites run the exercise audit as a gate, the justfile's `exercise_audit` verdict:** `just pre-merge` and CI's `pre-merge` job, `just release` and each Release cell, each over the tests its nextest run ran, so a quarantined test (ADR 0064) or one a cell cannot run, which leaves no record, never fails it; a finding fails the suite. `just pre-merge` runs it after a red nextest run too: the Pre-merge suite still runs the doctests then and fails with 100 when they passed, the audit runs on that 100, and when it passes the incremental gate records green each crate that passed in full but those holding insta snapshots, whose unreferenced snapshots cargo-insta judges only once every test passed (the test-gate audit's option 4b). The step stays red. `just features-audit <scope> report` lists the findings and fails nothing, for a local look.

### The report

- **`xtask features report --milestone <M> --store <store> --listings <folder> --out <folder>`** writes `features.json`, for agents, and `features.html`: each active Feature tested, untested by cause, excepted by evidence kind or planned at milestone `M`'s gate, with its declaring tests and their outcomes, and each Product's view by area and by due milestone. Its `gate` and its last line give the milestone gate's state: ready only when the store holds a Release run of `main` and no Feature due at `M` is untested on it, with the untested Features' IDs. It fails no gate. The daily test-health job builds it at the gate of the next owner test, writes its lines to the job summary and attaches both files to its run.
- **Due:** a generated Feature at every gate; a declared one at its milestone; a story with `journey = true` at the later of its milestone and the earliest milestone of a variant of a Journey that covers it (ADR 0075). A declared Feature not due yet, or with no milestone, is planned.
- **Results:** `main`'s latest Release run is the newest commit of `main` on which every cell of the Release matrix has a test run in the store, across dispatches (a commit some of whose cells have not run is not read, ADR 0108); each test's newest run per configuration on it counts, and a skipped or inconclusive one is not run. A Journey's declaration stands for its variants due at the gate, each as the Journey report judges it from the result documents the run kept: `proved` passes, `failed` and `rejected` fail, `unproved` is not run, and one a quarantined test carries is quarantined. A conformance case's declaration counts as the `conformance` documents the run's cells kept give the case (`<suite> <case id>`), the worst across cells, and is not run when none holds it; the conformance suites' tests keep theirs as `conformance-<suite>-<product>-<mode>.json` in `GOODFETCH_TEST_RESULT_DOCUMENTS`, and each part of a suite run in parts as `conformance-<suite>-<product>-<mode>-<part>.json`.
- **Causes, the first that holds:** `no-test`; `failed`, `quarantined` or `not-run` for a declaring test that is no Journey variant; `no-test-above-unit-level` for a story; `no-covering-journey` for a flagged story with no proved covering variant; then `failed`, `quarantined` or `not-run` for a declaring Journey's variant.

### Code coverage and mutation testing

- **Never gate on a percentage:** no `--fail-under-*`, no coverage or mutation score in any check, badge or pull request, and no coverage service. The Quality register's `coverage.project` ratchet opens an issue on a fall and fails nothing.
- **The coverage job runs in no suite** on Linux x64, weekly (nightly once the nightly tier is on): cargo-llvm-cov over nextest and over every run of the instrumented binary through `show-env`, Vitest's `v8` provider on Node and in Browser Mode, coverage.py with branches. While the repository is private, it has a 60-minute timeout and runs only while the month's count toward the ceiling of [Flaky tests and Live checks](#flaky-tests-and-live-checks) is below 550 minutes; a skip says so in its job summary. Its results never decide a Feature's status: its step is no test step, and its JUnit files carry the runtime `instrumented`, a configuration of their own in the results store. It attaches its three reports to the run as `coverage-report` (`.github/workflows/coverage.yml`), and its `compare` job, which holds the issue token and runs none of the product, compares them with the [Quality register](#quality-register).
- **Run `just coverage`** for the Pre-merge suite's tests of what changed (`just coverage all` for the whole workspace, as the job runs): every workspace crate is built for coverage into a target folder of its own, test support's map passes `LLVM_PROFILE_FILE` to every executable a test starts, and the reports go to `target/coverage/rust/`, `vitest/` and `python/`, written even when a test fails.
- **Read coverage for gaps.** A function no test executes that hides a behaviour with no Feature gets a story, a Feature or a test.
- **Every pull request that changes Rust code runs cargo-mutants `--in-diff`** with nextest on Linux x64, sharded within the 15-minute job budget, in a job that never fails on a survivor and is not part of "all green". While the repository is private, it runs on the owner's machine with the pull-request tier, using no included minutes, so neither the four-shard cap nor the 550-minute threshold applies to it ([ADR 0135](docs/adr/0135-while-the-repository-is-private-the-owners-machine-runs-the-pull-request-tier-in-a-throwaway-linux-vm-and-private-fuzzing-nightly.md)). The `affected` job counts the change's mutants and gives the `mutants` job one shard per 4 (`ci/mutants.sh`); each shard writes its surviving mutants to its job summary and attaches its report as `mutants-<k>`. Run `just mutants` for every mutant of your branch's committed Rust changes at once: it writes each mutant into the working tree and takes it out again.
- **List each surviving mutant of your changed code in the Evidence section,** with the test that now kills it or why it stays (an equivalent mutant; caught only by a doctest, by a Journey in another runner, or by another OS's test).
- **Full mutation runs are weekly once the nightly tier is on:** cargo-mutants over the workspace, StrykerJS on the TypeScript SDK runtime and the Console's logic (its `command` runner until a release fixes [stryker-js#6210](https://github.com/stryker-mutator/stryker-js/issues/6210), then its Vitest runner and an in-diff pull-request job), and mutmut on the Python SDK runtime on Linux.

## Accessibility, localization and visual regression

Decided in [ADR 0073](docs/adr/0073-accessibility-meets-wcag-2-2-aa-the-console-ships-ready-to-translate-and-screenshots-compare-in-one-pinned-image.md). The runners are those of [Console and docs site](#console-and-docs-site). Two product decisions in it await the owner's confirmation: V1 ships in English, ready to translate, and the docs publish an accessibility statement.

### Accessibility

- **The target is WCAG 2.2 AA** for the Console, the docs site and the Live view packages' own controls. The remote page inside the Live view is third-party content; the Live view's controls, name and announcements are not.
- **Every oxlint `jsx-a11y` rule is an error.**
- **Every Storybook story runs Storybook's a11y addon with `parameters.a11y.test = 'error'`** and the tags `wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa`, `wcag22aa` and `best-practice`, in light and dark.
  _Why:_ axe's WCAG 2.2 rules are off by default.
- **Use the shared Playwright fixture: it runs axe with the same tags at the end of every served test and `console` Journey variant.** Call `expectAccessible(page)` at each state worth checking on the way (an open dialog, drawer or menu). The docs pass runs axe on every `sitemap.xml` page of `next`, light and dark.
- **The audit runs axe with every rule of those tags on**, at the end of each served test that passed, on every open page but `about:blank`, and fails the test with `axe_violations`. Its "incomplete" results are attached to the report as `axe-incomplete` (the page's URL and each result's rule, help and elements) and never fail. axe waits on the page's timers, so the end-of-test audit resumes a `page.clock` the test installed; a test that paused it resumes or runs it before calling `expectAccessible`.
- **Never disable an axe rule in a test.** Except a false positive only in the shared exception file, `console/axe-exceptions.toml`, one `[[exception]]` table per false positive with its `rule`, a `selector`, the `pages` it holds on, the `reason` and the open `issue`'s number. `pages` lists URL path patterns as `URLPattern` reads them (`["/runs/:run_id"]`), as few as the false positive needs; `["/*"]` names every page, for an element of the shared layout. An entry suppresses its rule's violations on the elements its selector matches, on the pages it names, and no other rule's. The fixture fails the test with `axe_exception_unmatched` when a page an entry names ran its rule (axe found elements to check) and the entry excepted no violation there, its selector matching no element or none of its elements violating the rule any more, with `axe_exception_too_broad` when its selector takes in the whole page (`html`, `body` or every element in it, as `*`, `body *` or `:not(#x)` do), which would turn the rule off, and with `axe_exceptions_invalid` when an entry lacks a field, names no page or a pattern `URLPattern` cannot read, or has a field the file does not define. An entry has no expiry date: its open issue is its review, and the fix that closes the issue removes it. Whether each issue is still open is checked outside the tests, which cannot reach GitHub ([ADR 0043](docs/adr/0043-nothing-leaves-the-machine-unless-configured.md)): `xtask axe-exceptions check`, in `just lint` beside the quarantine list's check, fails an entry missing a field or naming no page, or whose issue is closed or no issue of the repository. "Incomplete" results go to the report and never fail.
- **Each screen has a keyboard walk test,** one named served case per `screen:` Feature of the inventory: Tab and Shift+Tab reach every element with an interactive role, focus never falls to `body`, Escape closes each overlay and returns focus to its trigger, and the ordered accessible names are a snapshot. At each stop, the element must look different focused and unfocused, and must not be entirely covered.
  - `keyboardWalk(page)` and `focusChecks(page)`, from `@goodfetch/test-support/playwright`, check these rules; each walk starts at the page's top and ends when focus leaves the page. `keyboardWalk` tabs forward, opens each overlay a stop declares (`aria-haspopup` or `popovertarget`) with Enter, or ArrowDown on a combobox, presses Escape there, and tabs back; it gives each stop's `{ role, name }` in Tab order for the snapshot. It fails with `focus_trapped` when Tab comes back to a stop before focus leaves the page, `focus_lost` when focus falls to the body, `focus_unreachable` when Tab or Shift+Tab never reaches a visible, enabled element with an interactive role (a composite widget's options, items, tabs and radio buttons count through their widget), `overlay_not_opened` and `focus_not_returned`. `focusChecks` fails with `focus_obscured` when no point of a 5 × 5 `elementsFromPoint` grid over the part of a stop inside the viewport shows it on top, `focus_not_visible_unstable` when that area changes while the stop stays unfocused (an animation that never ends there would pass for a ring), and `focus_not_visible` when the stop, with 8 CSS pixels around it for its ring, looks the same focused and unfocused.
  - On WebKit, a walk presses Alt+Tab, since WebKit's Tab skips links and buttons as macOS does, and `focusChecks` takes its screenshots through the known-issue tolerance of the style element Playwright adds before every WebKit screenshot, which the production CSP refuses, so it checks focus visibility there too.
  - The cases are generated, not written: `console/screens.spec.ts` calls `testEachScreen` (`@goodfetch/test-support/screens`), which reads the inventory's `screen:` Features and gives each one served case named by its Feature (`screen:/runs/$run`). The case runs on the executable of the screen's Product (Collie's for a screen both Products carry, Foxhound's for Foxhound's screens), opens the screen's route in its sign-in state (`signed-in` follows the executable's single-user link first), fails with `screen_not_served` unless the route answers 2xx, waits for the screen's ready element, then runs `keyboardWalk`, attaching its stops as `keyboard-walk`, fails with `keyboard_walk_empty` when it reaches no stop and with `keyboard_walk_changed` unless its stops, each `<role> <accessible name>` in Tab order, are the walk recorded for the screen, then runs `focusChecks`, then `reflow` and, since every case prefers reduced motion, `noRunningAnimations`, and ends with the axe fixture's pass, whose `wcag22aa` tag checks target size. Area 12 gives each screen its route, every parameter filled, its sign-in state and its walk in `console/screens.toml` (`[[screen]]` with `feature`, `route`, `sign_in`, `walk`), and `ready`: the element it shows once it has read what it shows, as `<role> <accessible name>`, or as `text <its whole text>` for an answer with no role that takes a name, such as "text Nothing needs you now.". Every screen needs one, since the Console renders only once its sign-in and offline cache are open and keeps Sign out and a screen's actions disabled until it reaches the engine, so a walk started at the page's load event walks an empty or half-ready page; a screen missing any fails its case by name with `screen_setup_missing`, never a skip, an entry for a screen the inventory does not list fails by its Feature with `screen_setup_orphaned`, a Console whose packages commit routes while the inventory names no screen fails a case `every screen` with `screens_missing`, and a broken file fails the whole file with `screen_setups_invalid`.
  - A walk never grows with the data a screen shows: a data table whose rows hold controls is a grid (WAI-ARIA's data grid pattern) with one Tab stop, the arrow keys, Home, End, Page Up and Page Down moving between its cells and onto a cell's control, so its rows add no stop. Engine & settings' effective settings are one, walked as `columnheader Setting` however many settings the engine has (`console/collie/tests/screens/settings-keyboard.test.tsx`).
- **Check reflow, text spacing and motion with the shared helpers:**
  - at 320 × 256 CSS pixels there is no horizontal page scroll, except inside data tables and code blocks with their own scroll container. `reflow(page)`, from `@goodfetch/test-support/playwright`, lays the page out at that size, fails with `horizontal_scroll` when the page or an element of it scrolls sideways, unless the element is a table or code block (`table`, role `table`, `grid` or `treegrid`, `pre`, `code`) or its only child is one, and gives the page back its viewport;
  - with WCAG's text-spacing values and at 200% text, in the `component` project's screens, no text is clipped without a way to read it in full. `clippedText(locator)`, from `@goodfetch/test-support/vitest-browser`, fails with `text_clipped` when an ancestor whose `overflow` hides or clips cuts off text under the locator; text an ancestor lets the reader scroll to passes, as does visually hidden text (a box at most 1 CSS pixel wide or high). `textSpacing()` applies the style sheet until the test ends; the Release suite's `component-text-spacing` project applies it to every test of the Console's `tests/screens/` and, once each ends, runs `clippedText` over the whole page, so a screen test need not call it. It doubles the root's font size, so a size in `px` does not grow;
    _Why:_ served tests run under the production CSP, which refuses an injected style sheet, and Playwright has no option for either;
  - under `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`, `document.getAnimations()` holds no running animation after an action. `noRunningAnimations(page)`, from `@goodfetch/test-support/playwright`, reads them two frames after the call and fails with `animations_running`, or with `reduced_motion_off` on a page that does not prefer reduced motion.
- **Every drag has a single-pointer alternative,** and its test performs the action through it.
- **Test announcements with `@guidepup/virtual-screen-reader`** in the `component` project: live regions, route titles and Live view control changes. Assert names and roles in the spoken log. `virtualScreenReader(container)`, from `@goodfetch/test-support/vitest-browser`, starts one over the container until the test ends.
- **The Live view's escape key always returns focus to the Console** while a person holds control; a served test proves it and that every other key reaches the remote page.
- **The CLI's colour never carries information alone:** in a pseudo-terminal, the coloured output with ANSI sequences stripped equals the `NO_COLOR=1` output. `NO_COLOR` set to any non-empty value disables colour, an empty one does not, and `--color always` overrides it. The shared CLI helper fails any `tests/it` case whose piped output holds an escape byte (0x1B).
- **Accessibility tests declare no `screen:` Feature** ([Feature coverage](#feature-coverage)): axe, keyboard walks, announcements and screenshots check how a screen is built, not its outcome. A screen's `screen:` Feature is declared by the tests of its behaviour. They declare area 12's accessibility stories instead (ADR 0073's note of 2026-10-26): the generated walk `story:12-130`, the announcement tests `story:12-131`, the composition tests `story:12-134`. The keyboard walk has one served case per `screen:` Feature of the inventory, ending with the fixture's axe pass, so a new screen gets both.
- **Real screen readers run as the Live check `screen-reader`** (Guidepup, NVDA on Windows and VoiceOver on macOS), nightly, owned by area 12, blocking nothing directly; before each release candidate while the nightly tier is off.

### Localization

- **V1 ships in English.** The Console is internationalized: every interface string comes from its Lingui catalog with ICU messages.
- **Never hard-code a Console string.** `lingui/no-unlocalized-strings` runs through oxlint's JS plugins, and `lingui extract` in the Quick suite fails on an uncommitted catalog change.
- **Render data inside `translate="no"`:** URLs, names, IDs, page text and every value from the engine.
  _Why:_ browser translation then leaves data alone, and the pseudo-locale test tells data from interface text.
- **Render every Console story under `en-XA` and `ar-XB`** in the Release suite: the `component-stories-en-XA` and `component-stories-ar-XB` projects set the browser context's locale, from which the Console picks its catalog, and their setup, `@goodfetch/test-support/vitest-browser/pseudo-locale`, checks each story's page once it ends. No text is clipped (`clippedText`).
